# Release Notes

## Unreleased

### Added

- **Cards on jj** (HI-2866, HI-2868, HI-2882, HI-2869, HI-2870, HI-2874). Cards now run in a jj project, as in Git: each in a slot of its own (a jj workspace), built with jj, checked and reviewed, and then **you integrate it yourself**. Combwork never merges, moves a bookmark or pushes under jj.
  - **Waiting for you to integrate**, in Version Control → Repository: the cards that passed review, each with a `jj rebase` command to start from and what Combwork last saw. Move the target bookmark onto a card, or rebase it there, and Combwork moves it to Done by itself.
  - **Publish records to check**: after you push, anything that later looks different (the remote moved back, another remote, the work no longer in its target) is listed for you, with **Mark as checked**.
  - **Setting up a jj project's slots records your working copy once**, as your own `jj status` would, and only while no agent works in the project folder. What an interrupted set-up left is listed for you to remove; Combwork never deletes it.
  - **A stale slot** (another workspace rewrote it) waits for your **Recover…**, which keeps any edits jj hadn't recorded. A card's Work section shows its jj workspace and change id.
  - Projects switched to jj before this pick it up with no change, lanes already running there included.
- **jj repositories that aren't colocated with Git** (HI-2896). A jj repository that keeps Git's data inside `.jj` (no `.git` in the folder) can now use **jj** as its version control too, as a colocated one does: the same views and switch, and the same slots and review copies once jj cards run. Combwork never runs git in such a folder, never exports, imports or changes colocation, and agents there are told to use jj for everything.
  - **Back to Git** needs a Git working copy: make the repository colocated yourself (`jj git colocation enable`), then switch; **None** is always offered.
  - **Inside a Git repository**: a repository that isn't colocated and sits inside a Git working tree can't use jj, since git commands run in it would change that other repository.
  - **A past `jj git export` no longer counts against a target**: without colocation nothing exports by itself, so the target is your local bookmark.
  - **Changing colocation while a project uses jj** stops what was waiting with nothing written; earlier slots aren't lent, and nothing is deleted.
- **Edit a lane's cards yourself** (HI-2898). In Progress → Lanes, open a running lane and drag its waiting cards into another order (or Alt+Up and Alt+Down), take one off with × or Delete (it stays on the board), or add one with **Add Card…** or by dragging it from the board onto the lane.
  - **As the Assistant's changes are:** a change made while a card is being worked waits until that card is Done, shown under the lane with **Cancel Change**; between cards it applies at once. The card being worked and the cards before it keep their places.
  - **Refused in place, with why**, nothing changed: a card that's Done, not in Todo, of another project, keeping earlier work or placed before a card it depends on, or a lane that changed meanwhile. A card on another lane offers **Move Here**, which moves it in one step.
  - **Undo** in the notification, or **Ctrl+Z** in the tab, puts the last change back exactly, or not at all once something changed since.
- **Today's ended lanes** (HI-2898). **Ended today**, at the top of Progress → Lanes, lists the lanes that ended or were abandoned since midnight, with their builder and reviewer, cards, merge commit and how long they ran, each opening to its cards.
- **The Activity map** (HI-2903). Activity, a project's tab and the workspace's view, draws what its agents are doing as a honeycomb above the list (beside it when wide): the agent in the centre, and six stations its tool calls go to (You, Read, Edit, Shell, Agents, Tools), each counting its turn's calls and naming the last. Live, each call sends a dot out to its station and back; a failed call turns its station red, and the busiest spokes thicken. **Last actions** lists the newest calls.
  - **One agent** shows its own map, with its context as a ring (amber from 70 %, red from 90 %); **several, or All**, one shared map with each agent in its own colour and a context ring each.
  - **Names, never content**: a file by its last two folders, a web page by its host, a command only as its tool.
  - **Show** turns the map, its feed and the rings on and off. It is frozen when Activity is, draws nothing while hidden, and follows Windows' **Show animations** (Settings → Advanced → **The Activity map's moving pulses** to choose).

### Fixed

- **A slot no longer stays lent after its card is Done when the builder was restarting** (HI-2920). A builder restarted for its tools (or switched to another connection) just as its card finished took its old slot back for itself, so the slot never returned to the pool and the next card couldn't have it. Such a restart now resumes only where the agent may still work, and a stop while it gets ready leaves the slot alone.
- **An agent waiting for the Assistant's answer is no longer reported as stalled** (HI-2915). An agent that asks the Assistant for something on its own card in Active (a turn on the Windows Sandbox, say) now has the card marked waiting for that answer, until the Assistant has dealt with it. To make it wait longer, the Assistant marks the card waiting itself and clears the mark when it says go. A lane's row in the Progress panel shows **waiting** for a waiting card, and the agent's tab in Activity says what it waits for.
- **The Assistant's banner no longer assumes Claude Code** (HI-2899). On a fresh install the Assistant panel said *No Claude Code account is on in this workspace*, though the Assistant runs on any coding agent's account. Now it names only the coding agent the Assistant would really start on: with no account at all it asks you to add one, for any coding agent; with only a Codex account it starts on Codex, with no banner; if its account is signed out, it names that connection to sign in again; and where you chose a coding agent with no account here while another's is, **Assistant Settings…** lets you switch.
- **Version Control → Merge queue scrolls** (HI-2894). With more queues and history than fit, the page ran past the bottom of the window and couldn't be scrolled, so the queues further down couldn't be reached. Now it scrolls to its last queue, with the mouse wheel or the keyboard, and each history table keeps its own scroll inside it.
- **Only the live merge queues show** (HI-2893). The Merges tab and Version Control → Merge queue showed queues for branches long finished (`1.0.0`, `1.1.0`), with Pause buttons, and the two chose them differently. Now both show only the queues of the branches the project merges into, plus any queue that still has cards in it. Past branches' history is still in Version Control → Merge queue, under a folded **Past branches**, and the Assistant lists them only when it asks. The queue card in Version Control no longer links to the page it's on.
- **Bring Up to Date… only towards the next version** (HI-2892). A release branch's merge queue card (`1.4.0`) said it was behind the next version (`1.5.0`) and offered to bring it up to date with it, which would have merged all of the next version's unreleased work into the release. Now it's offered only from an older version, or `main`, into a newer one, by the branch names; where the names don't say which comes first, it isn't offered either way. The Assistant refuses the other direction too, saying why, unless you explicitly ask for it, and a forward card already queued the wrong way comes back without merging.
- **A lane's next card starts when its builder watches its own lane** (HI-2889). A builder that watched its lane after a card went Done ended its turn waiting on that watch, and Combwork never gives a card to an agent waiting on a watch, so the lane's next card sat in Todo until the lane-next stall raised it 15 minutes later. Now Combwork ends a watch on the builder's own lane alone (nothing else in it) and starts the card, which wakes the builder. A watch on anything else is still the builder's own wait, left as it is.
- **Taking a finished branch out of Other branches work can merge into** (HI-2886). A free slot whose last card merged into it no longer keeps it there: only a card, a lane, a queue entry or a slot still in use (lent, with changes, set aside or busy) does, and the message names it.
- **`main` into the next version after a release** (HI-2885). **Bring Up to Date…** on a merge queue's card now offers the repository's default branch (`main`) too, even when it isn't one of the branches the project merges work into, and even when the project merges into only one. It lands as a merge commit through the merge checks even when the next version already has every file of `main`'s, where before it was taken as merged already and nothing was merged.

## 1.3.0 — 2026-11-10

### Added

- **Signed merges without waiting** (HI-1185). When your repository signs its commits, Version Control → Branches & remotes has a **Signing** section: how it signs, and whether it can sign with nobody there (a quick check that signs a throwaway text, with no prompt). When it would ask for a passphrase, it says how to set up signing that doesn't (an SSH key in the Windows agent, or gpg-agent's cache).
  - **The merge queue checks first**: a key that needs you now pauses the queue before anything is merged, rather than after its time limit.
  - Combwork never changes your git, ssh or gpg settings, and runs only the signing programs of Git for Windows, GnuPG and Windows' OpenSSH by itself.
- **Agents' signed commits never wait for a passphrase** (HI-2867). An agent's commit in a repository that signs could wait for a passphrase nobody was there to type, or, with Windows' own ssh-keygen, on the agent's own terminal. Now it signs with nobody there, or fails at once and the agent is told why and how you can set it up.
  - **Signing stays on**: nothing commits unsigned, and your git and gpg settings are never changed (sessions get a signer of Combwork's in their environment only). Signing programs other than Git for Windows', GnuPG's and Windows' OpenSSH run as before.
  - **Settings → Advanced** turns it off (**Agents' signed commits never wait for a passphrase**) and sets how long a signer may run (**Agents' signer: give up after**, 120 seconds).
- **Submodules and Git LFS in slots and copies** (HI-1182). Slots, review and check copies and the merge queue's folder are worktrees, which git makes with empty submodule folders, and Combwork's had LFS files only where the project's setup did it. Now, in a repository that uses either, Combwork sets them up itself, before the setup script and before every card, review or merge check, as a run of its own in the Progress panel.
  - **Submodules** are checked out at the folder's revision, nested ones too, each folder cloning its own. Your repository's config is never written (no `git submodule sync` or `init` there), so the project folder and your URL overrides stay as they are; a submodule you turned off (`active = false`, `update = none`) is left alone.
  - **Git LFS files** are fetched with `git lfs pull`, downloaded once for every worktree of the repository. A submodule's own LFS files follow your git-lfs setup.
  - **Never touches what isn't its own:** a submodule folder that already holds files or another repository stops the setup, saying so, and changes nothing. A review copy removes only the submodule folders it cloned itself once its revision no longer has them; uncommitted changes inside a slot's submodule count as uncommitted work.
  - **No sign-in windows:** fetches never prompt, run within the setup's time limit and end with it. Without git-lfs, or with Git LFS not set up (`git lfs install`), slots and copies can't be prepared, and Version Control → Slots and copies says so.
  - **Set up submodules and LFS**, in Version Control → Slots and copies, shows what the project uses and turns this off.

- **Undoing a jj operation, previewed** (HI-2800). A jj project's Version Control tab has **Operations**: jj's operation log, each operation by the name jj gave it. **Preview Undo** shows what undoing one would change: what it changed, and any later work undoing it would also change. Combwork runs nothing: only when undoing it would touch nothing but that operation's own changes does it give you the `jj op revert` command to run yourself, and **Copy** checks the repository again first. No command for an operation that changed a working copy, recorded a change made in Git, or joins operations that ran at the same time, saying why.
  - **Agents in a jj project are told to use jj**: to record work with `jj describe`, `jj commit` and `jj new`, and not to run git commands that change HEAD, the index, branches or refs. Before, agents asked to commit there used `git add` and `git commit`.
  - **A stale workspace is marked** in Repository, with what `jj workspace update-stale` can do to edits jj hasn't recorded.
  - **`.jj` is hidden like `.git`**: not in the Files tab, search, the files watcher, the documents agents read, or what a None project delivers. A jj repository without a colocated Git one keeps `.combwork` out, in jj's own Git store. After a move, **Repair** leaves jj's workspaces as they are rather than repairing only git's half.
  - The user guide lists what jj doesn't support in Combwork, one item per limit the window shows.
- **A moved submodule in one line** (HI-2864). A card that moved a submodule showed an empty diff in Changes. Now Changes, a comparison of two branches and a card's Work section show **Submodule lib/sub: 0bbdde2 → 0fc182f** (the full ids in its tooltip), and say so when a submodule is added, removed, or replaced by a file or symbolic link, or has uncommitted changes inside its folder. **Show git's diff** shows git's own `Subproject commit` lines.

- **jj (Jujutsu) as a project's version control** (HI-2799). In a jj repository colocated with Git, the Version Control tab offers **jj** beside None and Git; Combwork never takes it by itself, never creates a jj repository and never changes its colocation.
  - **Read-only views**: **Repository** (where it is, the jj version, its workspaces, what isn't supported), **History** (each change id beside its commit id, *conflicted*, *divergent*, *working copy*), **Compare** (an id naming several commits lists them all, never one picked), **Bookmarks** (conflicted ones with their sides, ones not the same in Git marked) and **Conflicts**. Every read is pinned at jj's single operation head and changes nothing; with more than one head, nothing is read until jj reconciles them.
  - **You integrate reviewed work yourself**: Combwork never merges under jj. **Waiting for you to integrate** lists the passed cards with their reviewed commit and a `jj rebase` to start from. Cards don't run in a jj project yet: a start is refused, saying so.
  - **Switching to jj or back to Git is the repository's**: every project on it that uses Git or jj switches together, named in the dialog, and only once nothing runs on it (lanes, cards in flight, the merge queue, slots, review copies); nothing starts on the repository while it switches. To jj, the repository must use none of what jj doesn't support (Git attributes and filters, LFS, submodules, hooks, signing, at any level of Git's settings: Git for Windows sets some system-wide by default); back to Git, jj's bookmarks must be exported and unconflicted. `.jj` is always left in place.
  - **Git for Windows' own defaults don't refuse a switch to jj** (HI-2872): its LFS filter and the diff driver for Office documents no longer count, unless you've changed them or set them in your own Git settings. Its line-ending conversion (`core.autocrlf`) still does, and the dialog says how to turn it off for that repository alone.
- **Merge against your team's remote** (HI-1183). When others push to the same branch, a merge Combwork tested on your computer could meet a remote that had moved on. **The target branch follows its remote** (Version Control → Workflow, off by default) makes the remote branch the one the merge queue merges into.
  - **Fetches before each test merge** and tests on the remote's latest commit; a local branch with commits the remote lacks pauses the queue, never forced or merged silently.
  - **Pushes only if nobody pushed meanwhile**, testing the card again if someone did, then brings your local branch up to date (in its folder too). A card is Done once the remote has it.
  - **Never redirected, never guessed:** each push goes to the one address captured for it, with the remote's sign-in settings kept encrypted only while it's under way; a push whose answer was lost is checked on the remote, and if it isn't there, For you asks rather than pushing again.
  - New cards in slots start from the remote's latest commit. When the remote refuses a push (a protected branch, a hook), the queue now says so with the remote's own words, with or without this option.

- **Start the next version, and branches from anywhere** (HI-2807). Setting up 1.4.0 took a `git branch` in a terminal; now Combwork does it.
  - **New branch…** (Version Control → Branches & remotes) has **Starts at**: the folder's branch by default, or any branch, tag or commit, with what it points at shown. A branch made elsewhere than the folder's commit doesn't switch the project folder unless you tick **Switch to it now**. Its name is checked as you type, a branch or tag of that name included.
  - **Start the next version…** (Version Control → Workflow, under **Work merges into**, while that is a version branch such as `1.3.0`): proposes `1.4.0` (editable), then makes it from `1.3.0` as shown, adds `1.3.0` to **Other branches work can merge into** and moves **Work merges into** to `1.4.0`, offering to move the Todo cards and free slots that can follow. Started cards and running lanes stay on `1.3.0`.
  - **A branch that doesn't exist** can no longer be saved as **Work merges into** or one of the other branches: the field offers to create it from a branch you pick.
  - **The Assistant** does both with `cw_branch` (`create`, `nextVersion`), once you agree, and can undo them.

- **Copilot agents' status when their hooks don't arrive** (HI-2802). Where an organisation's policy turns Copilot's hooks off, a Copilot agent showed only "Status unknown". Now it shows **Working** and **Finished**, labelled as from its terminal, read from what Copilot writes there as a turn starts and ends. That can't show when it asks you something, such as a permission, so the banner says so, and nothing is typed into it until its hooks arrive. The pattern is **Turn state in the terminal** in Settings → Advanced → GitHub Copilot.

- **Claude Code under an organisation's policy** (HI-2777). Where an organisation's Claude Code policy allows only managed hooks (`allowManagedHooksOnly`, often set in its admin console), Combwork's hooks didn't run and Combwork couldn't tell what its agents were doing. Now **Admin snippet…** (on the warning, or **Claude Code Admin Snippet…** in the command palette) gives a block of settings for the organisation's administrator to paste into its managed settings once: Combwork's hooks and status line for every session, every account and every project, with your Agent API port filled in and no password or token in it. Combwork's own hooks are the same, so where the policy doesn't allow only managed hooks they still run once; if they differ (a hook timeout changed after pasting), Combwork takes each tool call and prompt once and asks for the snippet to be pasted again.
  - **The warning names the cause.** When an agent's hooks don't arrive, the banner says which setting Combwork found on the computer and where (the registry policy, the managed settings file, an allowlist such as `allowedHttpHookUrls`), and that the organisation's remote settings decide over it. When nothing on the computer explains it, it says the organisation's remote policy may. It no longer says `disableAllHooks` when the cause was `allowManagedHooksOnly`.
  - **Updates the policy undoes.** If Claude Code stays on its version after an update Combwork ran (or a session puts an older one back), Provider Setup says an organisation's policy may set its version or channel, instead of offering the same update as if nothing happened. Update stays available.
  - The user guide's new section, *Claude Code under an organisation's policy*, has the snippet, where it goes, and what `allowManagedPermissionRulesOnly` means for Combwork (more permission prompts; the Assistant asks before each of its tool calls).
- **GitHub Copilot on your company's GitHub** (HI-2779). Copilot's setup and sign-in check assumed github.com: on GitHub Enterprise Cloud with data residency (`yourcompany.ghe.com`) or with repositories on GitHub Enterprise Server, it couldn't be set up or checked properly.
  - **A GitHub host setting.** Settings → Version Control → Workspace defaults → **GitHub**, and a project's Version Control tab → **GitHub**: the repositories' host and where Copilot signs in, each showing the value used and where it comes from. Repositories on GitHub Enterprise Server choose where Copilot signs in (github.com or a GHE.com host). Only a host name is accepted: never a user name, token, path or port.
  - **One host for everything.** Copilot's sign-in (`copilot login --host`), its sign-in check, its model list and its sessions all get the same host (`GH_HOST` for the repositories, `COPILOT_GH_HOST` for Copilot). Each project's agents see their own host's models. Changing a host checks again, and says which running agents need a restart; sessions keep the host they ran on, shown in an agent's model hover and the Sessions tab. The Assistant can read and change the settings too, with Change settings on.
  - **What the check knows, and no more.** Provider Setup shows each host in use apart: not signed in, credentials set (never read, and not taken for a working sign-in), the GitHub CLI's login checked on that host, every model unavailable to the account, an error shown as it was given, or a host it couldn't reach. A login on github.com never counts for another host.
  - The user guide's new section, *GitHub Copilot with GitHub Enterprise*, sits beside the Claude Code one.
- **Agents on OpenCode 2** (HI-2759, HI-2760, HI-2761, HI-2762, HI-2764, HI-2767, HI-2769, HI-2771, HI-2772, HI-2773, HI-2774, HI-2775). Turn on **OpenCode** in Settings → Providers, add an OpenCode account in Settings → Accounts & Connections, and choose it when you add an agent.
  - **Install and accounts** (HI-2759). Provider Setup installs the official OpenCode 2 download (checked against its release's checksums) and updates it; OpenCode 1 and the archived project aren't used, and OpenCode doesn't update itself. Each account keeps its own provider sign-ins (made with `opencode auth login`) and its own sessions. OpenCode's Claude Agent provider isn't available through Combwork.
  - **Kept to itself** (HI-2759, HI-2769). Each agent runs a private OpenCode server that ends with it, even if Combwork closes unexpectedly. The project's own OpenCode config and plugins, keys set on your PC and its AWS, gcloud and Azure sign-ins stay out unless you let them in (Settings → Advanced → OpenCode). By default an agent uses only the provider in its connection's model: titles, compaction and image requests can't go to another provider, and OpenCode's free cloud models aren't used, so an OpenCode connection must name its model; Keep OpenCode to the connection's own provider (Settings → Advanced → OpenCode) turns that off. It decides which provider OpenCode uses, not where that provider's address points, and isn't a firewall: web search, MCP servers, plugins and commands can still reach the network.
  - **Status** (HI-2760). An OpenCode agent shows working, needs you (while OpenCode asks your permission), compacting and finished, from its private server's events, and catches up by itself after losing touch with it.
  - **Effort** (HI-2767). An OpenCode connection's **Effort** offers the variants OpenCode lists for its model (often low, medium and high), and Combwork sets the connection's model and effort on the session at every start and resume. With no effort chosen, the session uses the model's own default. An agent whose account no longer lists the chosen effort for its model doesn't start, and says why.
  - **Combwork's tools and skills** (HI-2761). OpenCode agents get Combwork's tools, the MCP servers they were given and the workspace's skills, and nothing else; an Independent OpenCode agent runs as OpenCode would in a terminal. Settings → Advanced → OpenCode says exactly what loads when the project's own OpenCode config is let in.
  - **Asks before reaching outside its folder** (HI-2775). OpenCode's own rules let its agents read and change any folder on your PC without asking; an OpenCode agent now asks first, as a Claude Code agent does, and waits as needing you until you answer in its pane (an unattended lane's agent too). A reviewer's review copies, the agent's skills and its temp folder stay allowed. **Approve all** approves these questions without asking, and a rule in the project's own OpenCode config, if you let it in, wins over Combwork's. Settings → Advanced → OpenCode → Ask before reaching outside the agent's folder turns it off.
  - **Review copies** (HI-2772). An OpenCode reviewer now reads and runs its checks in the review copy its review names, as the other coding agents' do, instead of reviewing the builder's folder read-only. The copies stay allowed when it asks before reaching outside its folder (HI-2775), and in Plan it edits nothing there.
  - **History, tokens, cost and plan limits** (HI-2762, HI-2773, HI-2774). An OpenCode agent's conversation shows in the transcript view and the Sessions tab, with its tool calls, interrupted turns and compactions, and its tokens and cost in the Overview and its pane's footer, read from the agent's own OpenCode server while it runs and once more when it stops. The cost is OpenCode's own; a $0 one is said to be no measure of a plan's allowance. Plan limits show for an OpenCode Go or Console workspace, the only limits OpenCode reports; for other providers, Combwork says none are available rather than make one up. A subagent OpenCode starts (HI-2773) is listed in the Sessions tab under the session that started it, with its own conversation, tokens and cost, counted once. Attached images and a tool's images show in the history, a revert marked in OpenCode shows as a notice until confirmed, and sessions made in an agent's own OpenCode window (`/new`, `/fork`) appear after the agent stops, a fork under its source with only its own usage; **Adopt** takes one over so it resumes like the agent's own (HI-2774). A history Combwork couldn't read to its end says so, and is read again later.
  - **Dialects** (HI-2764). Settings → Dialects has an OpenCode group: the texts of its CLI file apply to every OpenCode agent, as the other CLIs' do, **New Model File…** on the group makes a file for an OpenCode model ID (`ollama/qwen3:8b`, kept as typed), and Import Dialect File can put a file in its folder. Model files for OpenCode's models come once OpenCode agents' work has been measured.
  - **The Assistant** (HI-2771). An Assistant running on OpenCode now starts, resumes and comes back from a compaction with Combwork's short view of the board, lanes, agents and its journal, as on the other coding agents, put into its OpenCode session without starting a turn. With the journal off, Combwork takes its view out of the session at the next start.

- **Git options in the Files tab** (HI-2743). Right-click a file or folder in the project folder: **Exclude from Git** adds it, exactly, or all files with its extension, to the project's `.gitignore`, or only to this PC's `.git/info/exclude`; **Stop Tracking, Keep the File…** takes a committed file out of git and leaves it on disk; **Include Again** takes out the line that ignores a dimmed file, when it's a plain line for just that file; **Why Is This Ignored?** opens the rule that ignores it (hover a dimmed file to see the rule); and **Show Changes** opens a changed file's diff in Version Control.

### Changed

- **The Assistant's list of coding agents is shorter** (HI-2814). Its `cw_list_providers` tool now gives a line per coding agent (on or off, installed and tested version, default model, efforts, modes and connections), and one in full, with its models and what each mode does, when the Assistant names it. With OpenCode as the fifth, the full list of all of them had grown past the size Combwork keeps a tool's reply to. The Agent API's `GET /v1/providers` is unchanged.
- **Agents end processes only by their own handle** (HI-2865). An agent in one project ended every lane's runs with `ps -ef | grep cw-progress | xargs kill` (Git Bash's `ps` lists every process of yours), so other cards' tests stopped with exit 143. Combwork now tells every agent to end only what it started, by its handle or the pid and start time it took, never by name or a `ps`/`grep` pattern.
- **GitHub Copilot 1.0.107 tested** (HI-2780), beside 1.0.106: Provider Setup ticks either. 1.0.107's release notes are 1.0.106's, and no differences were found in what was checked (its help, its terminal output, its model listing, Combwork's Copilot suites); WinGet offered only 1.0.106 when this was tested.

### Fixed

- **A connection's model list is its own account's** (HI-2852). Claude Code offers each account its own models, but Combwork asked only the CLI's own home, so every connection listed those: one account's dialog showed Fable 5.1 while its sessions offered Fable 5.2. Now each account is asked in its own folder, and its connections' model and effort lists, their checks and their agents' launches use what that account offers. If an account can't be asked, its last answer is used, else the fallback list, never another account's.
- **The Assistant sees each connection's own models** (HI-2856). Listing the providers (`cw_list_providers`, `GET /v1/providers`) still gave the CLI's own home's models for every connection, so an agent choosing a connection's model could see Fable 5.1 where that account offers Fable 5.2. Now each connection carries its account's default, and its model list where it differs.
- **The card dialog's Merges into follows the project's branches** (HI-2830). After **Work merges into** or **Other branches work can merge into** changed (in Version Control → Workflow, or by the Assistant), a card's Merges into still offered the old branches until you switched project or reloaded the window, so a branch just added couldn't be picked. Now the dialog reads them again each time it opens. In 1.2 and 1.3.
- **The card dialog's Skip review follows the project's rules** (HI-2851). After a project's rules gained or lost a review (in Version Control → Workflow, or by the Assistant), the card dialog still offered Skip review, or didn't, by the old rules until you switched project or reloaded the window. Now it follows them as they change, the dialog open or not, and so does Merges into. In 1.0 to 1.3.
- **A `/rename` just before Stop survives a quick resume** (HI-2839). A Claude Code session renamed with `/rename` and resumed within seconds of stopping (sooner under load) went back to its older name for good: Combwork read the name from its usage history before that had the end of the transcript, and resumed the session with the older name. Now a resume reads the transcript itself when the history is behind it, and the name you gave in the CLI is kept. In 1.0 to 1.3.
- **Merges into on a card never started** (HI-2825). Changing the branch a card On Hold merges into, from the card dialog or by the Assistant, was refused as if the card had work, with a message naming an empty branch ("made from ,"), and the rest of the Assistant's change was refused with it. A card On Hold now changes its Merges into as a Todo card does, while its branch has no work of its own, and a refusal always names a branch.
- **A slot or copy is kept when git can't list worktrees for a moment** (HI-2810). When `git worktree list` failed (an index lock, a busy machine), a free slot or review copy was taken for gone and set up again at its next use, a slot in use was marked gone, and a recreation cut short was given up. Now an unreadable list leaves it as it is, and Combwork looks again next time.
- **"Hooks arrive twice" with no admin snippet** (HI-2850). An agent that got a message while it worked (from another session, or one you typed ahead) was said to have hooks arriving twice, and Combwork missed that prompt. Only a true copy of a hook now counts as one.
- **Stopping an agent a lane was about to start afresh** (HI-2815). A lane's builder that was busy as the lane started is owed a new conversation once it is free. If you stopped it meanwhile, Combwork counted it as free and started it again within a second. Now it stays stopped and the lane no longer waits to start it: start it yourself, or have the lane updated, which starts it on the lane. Nor does Combwork start a lane's next card on a builder you (or the Assistant) stopped: the lane says it waits until the builder is started again.
- **A reviewer told of a design or follow-up to check sees how to answer it** (HI-2808). When a reviewer from the pool was given a card and its builder's follow-up at once, its inbox showed only "2 changes, read the card", and the reviewer often started a review or asked what to do instead of checking the follow-up. The check now leads that inbox line, with how to answer it.
- **A stall after a safety filter stop says so** (HI-2754). When a lane's card stalls and the agent it waits on is idle because its latest turn was stopped by its provider's safety filter (Codex's cyber checks, Claude Code's safeguards), the stall's reason, the Assistant's lane wake and your lane notification now say so, with when and how many this session, and the provider's own advice. Before, it read as an ordinary wait ("only watches the card").
- **Cards in flight when an integration branch ends** (HI-2753). A card started on `1.2.0` that was merged into `main` and deleted couldn't be submitted, and couldn't be moved without losing its branch. A started card (Doing, Review or Passed) now moves to another branch with its branch and work kept, through its **Merges into** or the release retarget (Save…'s **Also retarget what can move**, the Assistant's `cw_queue` `retarget`), when all of the work it would bring there is its own; otherwise it stays and says why. A running lane moves with its card in flight. A reviewed card keeps its review only when its change into the new branch is the one reviewed, and the merge queue still checks the result on the new branch. Combwork holds git's own locks on both branches while it moves a card, and never removes a lock it didn't take. Submitting a card whose branch was deleted now says so, with how to move it, rather than "Check Provider Setup". A card started again keeps its branch, even after its title changed.
- **A resumed agent showed Ready before its CLI was** (HI-2816). An agent resumed in its conversation (after a restart for an update, or Resume) whose CLI was slow to start showed **Ready** a few seconds in, from the conversation's transcript as it already was, and Combwork tried to deliver its messages before it could take them. It now stays **Starting** until its CLI is heard from.

## 1.2.0 — 2026-11-08

### Added

- **Antigravity's and Copilot's plan limits** (HI-2734). The status bar and the Overview's plan limits now show Antigravity's weekly quotas, one for Gemini models and one for the other providers' models, with your plan's name, and Copilot's monthly quotas (premium requests, chat messages, code completions), with how many of how many are used. An unlimited quota says so, one your plan doesn't include says **Not in your plan**, and a plan named without limits says that, with no meter made up. Read from what the CLIs already record: no extra sign-in or request.
- **Local models in Claude Code's /model** (HI-2684). An agent on a server account sees the account's models in Claude Code's /model picker, by their exact IDs and under a **Label** you can give each model. Where the account serves no Claude model, Claude Code's own Opus, Sonnet and Haiku rows are hidden. A model lineup in your own Claude Code settings is used instead.
- **Context size and output budget for local models** (HI-2682). Each model of a Claude Code server account has a **Context size** and an **Output budget**: an agent started on it tells Claude Code the model's real context (rather than an assumed 200,000) and its output budget, with effort and thinking off. Names Claude Code reads as its own, and models your own Claude Code settings map to a Claude model, get none, and the context's hover says why. Settings files you set these in still win; a conflict stops the start, saying which values and where. The context's hover estimates where Claude Code compacts such a model: below its context, less its output budget (at most 20,000) and a 13,000-token buffer (about 46,536 of a 65,536 context with a 6,000 budget; HI-2710).
- **Retarget what's left when a release ships** (HI-2680). Moving **Work merges into** on (1.1.0 → 1.2.0) shows, in Save…, what still points at the old branch: Todo cards set to merge into it with no work of their own and free, clean slots still on it can follow the new one (**Also retarget what can move**); cards with work, cards already started, running lanes and lent or dirty slots stay, each with why. Each is checked again as it moves; a notification says what moved and what didn't. A slot found changed as it moves is set aside until you remove it. The old branch can't be taken out of Other branches work can merge into while anything still uses it. The Assistant's reply to the change lists what can move, and it moves only what that list showed (`cw_queue` `retarget`); it can now set Other branches work can merge into too.
- **Server diagnostics for a model server on your PC** (HI-2683). **Check Server…** on a server account shows its model's context as Ollama or llama.cpp reports it (trained, configured, allocated now, each with when it was read), compares it with the service's advice, takes your own figure where the server says nothing, and for llama.cpp says whether its template supports tools. **Test Tool Calls** checks that the model makes a real tool call and uses its result, through the API Claude Code uses, naming what went wrong (such as a tool call written out as text) rather than leaving you with an agent that "just prints text". A **llama.cpp** preset joins Ollama's.
- **Agents on Google's Antigravity CLI** (HI-2544). Turn on **Antigravity** in Settings → Providers and choose it when you add an agent. Provider Setup installs it with Google's installer, updates it with `agy update`, and says whether this PC is signed in: the sign-in is the PC's, shared by every workspace, and listed in Settings → Accounts as This PC's. Combwork starts it with its own updates off, keeps other tools' Gemini keys out of its environment, and runs each agent in a private profile of its own, so your own Antigravity settings don't reach it. Since Antigravity can't be kept from starting a project's own MCP servers (in its `.agents` folder), an agent doesn't start in a project that defines one it wasn't given, and says which. Its conversations resume by their own id, and an agent only resumes its own. Combwork follows its turns from its hooks and its record of your prompts (HI-2545), applies file locks to its edits (a subagent's too), shows the agent waiting for you while Antigravity asks you to approve something and ready again if you cancel, and follows the agent into a new conversation after `/new` or `/rewind`. Each agent gets Combwork's guidance and your agent instructions in its own profile's rules file, and its project folder trusted for the launch (HI-2547). Provider Setup has a one-time **Set up** for Antigravity's first-run screens (a color scheme, its terms), which you complete yourself; after it, agents start without them. An agent's legacy HTTP+SSE MCP servers, which Antigravity doesn't support, are left out and named as it starts, as is Auto on a Windows older than 11 24H2 with the August 2026 update. An agent whose instructions and notes would overflow Antigravity's 24,000-byte rules file doesn't start, and says why, rather than losing the end of them. Their transcripts show in the transcript view and the Sessions tab under Antigravity's own titles, with each request's tokens in the usage meter (no cost: Antigravity publishes no prices).
- **Bring a branch up to date with another, on a button** (HI-2679). A target's merge queue card (Merges tab, Version Control) says how many commits it is behind another of the project's branches (*1.2.0 is 3 commits behind 1.1.0*), and shows even with nothing queued. **Bring Up to Date…** merges the other branch, as it is then, into this one through its own queue with its merge checks, as a merge commit, on a card of Combwork's; the other branch never changes. A conflict or a red check returns the card for **Retry merge** (the branch taken as it is then) or **Remove from queue** (nothing merged). The user's and the Assistant's (`cw_queue` `forward`, `POST /v1/queue/forward`); agents can't act on these cards.
- **Lanes and cards can merge into another branch** (HI-2556), so the next release (`1.2.0`) can be worked on beside the one being finished (`1.1.0`). List the branch under **Version Control → Workflow → Other branches work can merge into**, then pick it as a lane's **Merges into** in **Start Lane…**, or a card's in its dialog. The lane's cards are made from that branch and merge into it through its own merge queue, with the same merge checks. A lane keeps its branch until it ends, and a lane is one branch. A card's branch can change only while it has no work of its own, since no rebase is made. The lane's header and the card's tile show the branch when it isn't the project's. The Assistant sets them too (`target` on `cw_lane`, `cw_create_task` and `cw_update_task`, and the Agent API). Bringing a later branch up to date with an earlier one (HI-2679) and retargeting at release (HI-2680) come next.
- **The Assistant's own memory, in the Assistant view** (HI-2666). Besides its journal, the Assistant's coding agent saves notes about working with you, kept for the workspace's folder: until now Combwork showed them nowhere. **Memory** in the Assistant view on the activity bar lists them, MEMORY.md first, in the same editor as a project's Memory tab, with the folder's path, **Copy Path** and **Open Folder**. It follows the coding agent the Assistant runs on now; for one other than Claude Code, it says that one's memory isn't shown here.
- **Resume or stop just one agent from the project header** (HI-2665). Resume (n) and Stop (n) get a narrow ▾ on their right listing those agents, each with its state and role: choose one to resume or stop it alone, as its pane would (asking first when its pane does). An agent whose sign-in was refused says Resume tells it to carry on, and one that can't resume where it ran just now is greyed out with why. Agents ▾ gets Start New for One Agent ▸ and Archive and Start New for One Agent ▸, the narrow header's ⋯ gets Resume One Agent ▸ and Stop One Agent ▸, and so does a project's right-click menu in the sidebar, under Resume All and Stop All.

### Fixed

- **An Independent agent starts as if from a terminal** (HI-2731). An agent whose role is Independent was given Combwork's tools, guidance and skills whenever its connection gave them, though Combwork never gives it work. It now starts with none of them, and none of the workspace's MCP servers, as an agent on a connection set to **Launch as from a terminal** does; changing its role restarts it. **Antigravity**: an agent's private profile now goes to the Recycle Bin when you remove the agent, and a launch without Combwork's tools leaves none of an earlier launch's behind in it.
- **Open Folder beside the Assistant's memory opens it** (HI-2736). In 1.2.0-beta.1, **Open Folder** in the Assistant view's Memory did nothing: Combwork's file guard admits Claude Code's memory files but not their folder, and the refusal wasn't shown. Combwork now opens the memory folder it lists for the workspace, and only that folder; if it can't, it says why.
- **The command palette opens empty** (HI-2730). Opened again after a command or project was chosen in it, the palette showed what was typed last for a moment before clearing it, so on a slow PC keys typed straight after Ctrl+Shift+P or Ctrl+P joined the old text and found *No matching commands*. It now opens empty, and what you type at once is all it searches for.
- **Provider Setup offers only an update the CLI's installer can make** (HI-2729). A Copilot installed with WinGet was offered npm's newer version, which WinGet publishes days later, so **Update** ran `winget upgrade`, which found nothing newer and exited with an error. A WinGet install is now compared with what WinGet has, and WinGet's *no newer version* is no longer a failure. Claude Code and Codex installed with WinGet are compared with WinGet too, and updated with `winget upgrade` (`claude update` doesn't update a WinGet install). Claude Code on the `stable` release channel (`autoUpdatesChannel`, from your organisation's managed settings or your own) is compared with that channel's newest version, not `latest`'s.
- **A CLI started from a `.cmd` or `.bat` launcher in a folder whose name has a space starts** (HI-2722). A launcher Combwork can't start as Node and its script runs through cmd.exe, which removed the quotes around its path: the agent, or the CLI's sign-in, update or setup, exited at once with *'C:\My' is not recognized as an internal or external command*. Combwork now quotes cmd.exe's command line the way it needs, so the CLI starts and gets its arguments as they were, for every provider.
- **Transcript images load just before you reach them** (HI-2726). In the Sessions tab, an image in a transcript loaded only once it was on screen, so an opened tool call's image just below the view showed its placeholder until you scrolled; images now load as they come near.
- **A CLI at a path with spaces is found** (HI-2548). Combwork checked a CLI's version through its `.cmd` with the path unquoted, so at a path with spaces the check failed and Combwork took another copy it found. It now runs it as it is. An Antigravity agent started without a prompt is also ready once its CLI is, rather than starting until its first prompt.
- **Antigravity in the Dialects view** (HI-2720). Antigravity agents had no CLI file, so the Dialects view had no Antigravity group: Combwork now ships an empty `antigravity/cli.json`, whose texts you can change for every Antigravity agent like the other CLIs', with **New Model File…** on its group, and Import Dialect File lists its folder too. Model files for the Gemini models it offers come once Antigravity agents' work has been measured.
- **The merge queue waits for a card's dependencies** (HI-2709). An approved card that depends on a card that isn't Done was merged anyway, and the other card's code went with it before its review had passed. That card was then closed as merged, with no verdict. Now a card that depends on another waits in the queue, marked **Waiting for HI-12**, while the cards behind it merge, and it merges once what it waits for is Done. A dependency added while the card is in Review, queued or being tested holds it. One added once its merge has begun is refused, saying why. And a card in Review is no longer closed because its code is already in its target, whether merged by hand or inside another card's merge: it stays in Review, marked *Its code is already in main*, and once its review passes the queue closes it as merged.
- **Card loop builders carry on after answering a message** (HI-2716). Asked by the Assistant how a card stands, a builder answered with a comment and then sometimes ended its turn watching its own card, with nothing waiting, until Combwork flagged the card as stalled. The card-loop and work-on-card skills now say a builder ends its turn only when its next step waits on someone else (a verdict, an answer, the user, a card it needs): a comment or a reply hands nothing over, so it carries on with the card.
- **Combwork types into an empty Claude Code input while its terminal has the focus** (HI-2692). Claude Code then draws its hint's first letter under its cursor, not dim like the rest of the hint (`Try "how does <filepath> work?"`). Combwork read that letter as a draft it couldn't clear, and held what it was to type, such as a lane end's `/exit` or a message's notice. The letter is now read as the hint it belongs to.
- **Performance says why the Agent API turned down its own wrong calls** (HI-2681). A request the Agent API refused itself (a card with no title, a column that isn't one) showed under Refusals and wrong calls by reason as "No reason recorded". It now shows that reply's sentence, its values left out, with its id (`agent:…`) under it. These are messages for agents, so the sentence is in English, and the table says so. Errors from the card watches, lanes and agents' messages behind the Agent API are said the same way (HI-2691), and their replies' words and statuses are as before.
- **A short commit id names a revision** (HI-2671). `cw_submit`'s `revision` and a check's took only a commit's full 40-character id, so the short id agents see in `git log --oneline` was refused as "isn't what's checked out here", although it was. Now a commit's id or a start of it of 7 or more characters works, in any case. A revision that isn't the checkout is refused naming the commit that is checked out, and a short id git finds more than one commit for asks for more of it.
- **A new connection or user instructions file says whom it's for** (HI-2667). When Memory opens a file that doesn't exist yet, it used to start every one with the project's name and "Instructions for Claude Code in this project.", even a connection's `CLAUDE.md`, which applies to that connection's agents in every project. Now a connection's file starts with the connection's name and "Instructions for Claude Code agents on this connection, in every project.", and your own user file (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, Codex's `AGENTS.md`, Copilot's `copilot-instructions.md`) with "All projects" and "Instructions for Claude Code in every project." A project's files are unchanged.
- **A `!` command typed into Claude Code no longer trips "Combwork isn't hearing from Claude Code's hooks"** (HI-2673). Claude Code 2.1.319 sends the command's output to the model and starts a turn without the hook a prompt sends. Combwork took that turn's hooks as old news, so the agent stayed finished while it worked, then Combwork said its hooks weren't arriving and stopped typing into it. While your Enter in the pane waits for its hook, a turn Combwork has never seen now counts: its first tool call shows the agent working, and its hooks show they arrive. The note's line for a Claude Code newer than Combwork was tested with no longer guesses that an update changed its hooks.
- **Changing Work merges into says what keeps the old target** (HI-2674). A card's target branch is set as it starts, and a running lane's as the lane starts, so a change of **Version Control → Workflow → Work merges into** didn't move cards in flight or a running lane's next cards, although the Assistant's reply said it applied now. Save… now lists each card and lane that keeps merging into another branch, and which. The Assistant's reply names them too, as it now does the lanes and cards that keep the old rules after a switch of version control (it had left them out).
- **Codex prices checked again** (7 Nov 2026) against OpenAI's published API prices: unchanged, with GPT-6.1 Terra added. Settings → Codex → API prices says when Codex's were checked, apart from the other providers'.
- **A session priced only in part shows as such.** When a session used a model Combwork has no price for beside models it has, its cost and the totals that include it show the known part with **+ ?**, rather than counting the rest as $0.

### Changed

- **An Agent API error's status no longer depends on the names in it** (HI-2704). Combwork now decides each error's status where it makes it. A project named `busy-work` that isn't found answers 404, not 409, and an error quoting another error keeps its own status. Agent API → Errors lists the few statuses that change. Performance's Refusals and wrong calls by reason now names more of them: a wrong watch, lane or build call shows its message instead of "No reason recorded".
- **Agents get only the Combwork tools their project's workflow can use** (HI-2694). On a project with no review (Solo), agents are no longer offered Start review, Verdict, Skip a focused test or Report merge, which its rules always refuse: about 2 KB less at each launch of an either-role or generalist agent, 1.6 KB for a reviewer and 0.4 KB for a builder. Pair and Team keep every tool. A tool stays while work already started still uses it. Changing a project's workflow settings restarts the agents whose tools it changes, as a role change does: at once if they're idle; if any are working, Combwork asks first (**Restart When Its Turn Is Over** or **Later**).
- **Agents get only the Combwork tools their role can use** (HI-2664). A builder is no longer offered the reviewer's tools (Start review, Verdict, Request builder, Skip a focused test), and a reviewer none of the builder's (Start card, Submit, Put back, Declare paths, Merge slot and the others), so every launch reads a shorter tool list: about 5 KB less for each reviewer, 2 KB for each builder. Either-role and generalist agents keep both sets, whatever lanes they're on. The Assistant loses the ten tools only a card's worker uses, about 3.6 KB less at each of its launches. Changing an agent's **Role in the workflow** restarts it to load its new tools, in the same conversation: at once if it's idle, and if it's working Combwork asks first (**Restart When Its Turn Is Over** or **Later**). Until it has restarted, it isn't given work that needs its new role's tools. A lane started by the Assistant or an agent now takes the same agents as **Start Lane…**: a builder or either-role agent to build, a reviewer or either-role agent to review.
- **Reviewers reuse the builder's recorded checks instead of running them again** (HI-2660). Start review now tells the reviewer which of the card's checks passed or failed at the revision it reviews, and the review skill makes those results stand: a reviewer runs what no record covers, its own probes, and a test again only for a doubt it names in its verdict ("reran X because Y"). A reviewer can also accept the builder's run of a test that needs a real CLI or the installed app (`cw_verdict`'s `accept`), so the merge takes it as proof without the reviewer running it again; its latest run at that revision must have passed. Reviews take less time, and say what they relied on.
- **Launch an agent as if from a terminal** (HI-2670). A connection's **⋯ → Launch Settings…** say what of Combwork's the sessions it launches get: **Combwork's guidance**, **Combwork's tools**, **Combwork types into the agent**, **Only the workspace's MCP servers**, and each of Combwork's changes to the CLI. **Launch as from a terminal** turns them all off, so an agent on that connection starts with the same context as `claude`, `codex` or `copilot` opened in a terminal in its folder, while Combwork still shows its pane, status, transcript, sessions and usage. A connection with some turned off is **Partly plain**. The Assistant always keeps its guidance, tools and typing. The Agent API and the Assistant's `cw_connection` take `launch`.
- **The Independent role** (HI-2670): an agent in the project's team that takes no part in the workflow. No lane, reviewer pool, merge queue or card start picks it, and Combwork never wakes it, delivers to it or types into it; you work with it in its pane. It's the only role offered on a connection without Combwork's tools. Making agents Independent while they're on a lane or hold a card asks first: their lanes pause and their cards stay for you to give to someone else.
- **Combwork's guidance and its changes to the CLIs are each connection's** (HI-2670), in its Launch Settings, no longer in Settings → Advanced. The first time this version starts, any you had turned off is turned off on every connection of its CLI, so nothing changes.

## 1.1.0 — 2026-11-06

<!-- old-name: begin changelog-upgrade -->
**Combwork, formerly called Hive Desktop**, and agents can now be given words tuned to their model: **dialects**. Another open-source app with the old name runs coding agents too, and names around agent tools with bees in them are crowded, so the app has a name of its own. The honeycomb icon and the colours stay.

**Before you update from 1.0:**
- **Close every copy of 1.0 that has the workspace open**, on this PC and any other. Combwork renames the workspace's `.hive` folders the first time it opens it, and it can't while another copy has it open.
- **The installer replaces 1.0.** It asks a running 1.0 to close, uninstalls it with your data kept, and moves your data folders to `%APPDATA%\Combwork` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Combwork`. Only Combwork is left in Installed apps. A taskbar pin of 1.0 and its notification history don't carry over: pin Combwork again.
- **Your agents start new conversations once.** A conversation from before remembers 1.0's tool names, so on its next resume Combwork starts a new one with the same brief, what it was waiting for and its messages, and keeps the old one in Sessions to read.
- **Update your antivirus exclusions.** If you excluded 1.0's folders from Microsoft Defender (or another antivirus), exclude the new ones: `%APPDATA%\Combwork` (Roaming), `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Combwork`, Combwork's install folder `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Combwork`, and each workspace's and project's `.combwork` folder if you excluded `.hive` on its own. Settings → Workspace → Antivirus scanning shows what Defender scans.
- **Scripts of your own** that read 1.0's `HIVE_*` session variables or call `hive_*` tools need the new names (`CW_*`, `cw_*`; below).
<!-- old-name: end changelog-upgrade -->

### Changed

- <!-- old-name: begin changelog-rename -->**Combwork, formerly called Hive Desktop** (HI-2458). The app has a name of its own. The window, the title bar, About, the tray, notifications, the installer, the Start menu and desktop shortcuts and Installed apps say **Combwork**, in every language, and the Assistant is just **the Assistant**. The honeycomb icon and the colours stay. The app installs as Combwork, your data folders and each workspace's `.hive` folder become Combwork's, and what agents see is renamed too: each is below. The move is made once, by the installer and the first time Combwork opens each workspace: afterwards nothing reads the old names, and going back to 1.0 isn't supported.
- **Combwork installs as Combwork and replaces the old app** (HI-2460). It installs in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Combwork` as `Combwork.exe`, under Windows app ID `it.surmise.combwork`, with a Combwork Start menu entry. Installing it, or updating from 1.0, asks a running 1.0 to close, uninstalls it with your data kept, then moves your data folders, so only Combwork is left in Installed apps. Launch at login follows the new app. A taskbar pin of the old app and its notification history don't carry over. If 1.0 won't close, the installer says so and changes nothing. If it can't be uninstalled, the installer stops before installing Combwork or touching your data folders, and says so; its uninstall may have stopped partway, so check Installed apps and run the installer again.
- **Your data folders are now Combwork's** (HI-2461): `%APPDATA%\Hive` becomes `%APPDATA%\Combwork`, and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Hive` (accounts and their sign-ins) becomes `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Combwork`. The installer renames them once 1.0 has closed, with your settings, usage and logs. If the installer couldn't rename them, Combwork does it at its first start (HI-2594, below). After the move, a connection whose sign-in didn't carry across is named once, so you can sign in to it again.
- **Workspaces and projects keep their files in `.combwork`** (HI-2462), part of the rename to Combwork: the `.hive` folder in each workspace and project (the board, shared notes, handovers, attachments, the Assistant's home, a project's sessions and settings) is now `.combwork`. The first time Combwork opens a workspace it renames each `.hive` folder of its own: nothing is copied or deleted, and links to attachments, notes and handovers in cards and notes follow. Close 1.0 first: while another copy has the workspace open, or a program has a file open in a `.hive` folder, Combwork doesn't open the workspace and says which folder it couldn't rename, and a move that stopped part-way finishes at the next open. A folder that has both keeps using `.combwork` and leaves `.hive` as it is, with a warning, and another tool's `.hive` is never touched. An agent whose conversation ran in a folder inside a `.hive` starts a new conversation there, its earlier one kept in Sessions.
- **What Combwork stored under the old names takes Combwork's once** (HI-2598). Upgrading from 1.0 or a 1.1 release candidate converts each value Combwork stored as `hive` to `combwork`, once, as Combwork starts and as each workspace opens: the board's records (who did what, lanes, merges, messages waiting in inboxes, the Performance view's tool counts), the Assistant's journal, workflow options ("Combwork merges" and its commit message) in projects, workspaces, templates and Combwork's own defaults, and settings (When Combwork quits, the Agent API's switch, Advanced's ids). Your choices and history carry on unchanged. **Scripts and agents**: the Agent API and Combwork's tools say `combwork` where they said `hive` (a template's `scope`, a skill's `level`, a session's and the journal's `source`, the developer console's `source=combwork`, the merge queue's `by`, the refusal `merge.combworkMerges`, the workflow value `finish: combworkMerges`, the setting `agentApi.provideCombworkMcp` and `<cli>.override.preApproveCombworkTools`), and the old value is refused. A template exported from 1.0 with workflow settings under the old values isn't imported: it is refused, saying so (HI-2611).
- **What agents see says Combwork too** (HI-2463). The MCP server Combwork gives every session is `combwork` (it was `hive`), its tools `cw_*` (`cw_inbox`, `cw_submit`… for `hive_inbox`, `hive_submit`…), the progress command `cw-progress` (it was `hive-progress`), its step lines `##cw-progress`, and the lines Combwork types into a session begin `[CW]` (`[Hive]`). Combwork's skills reach Claude Code as `combwork:<name>` and Codex and Copilot as `.agents/skills/cw-<name>`; its old `hive-<name>` copies go when they are provably its own and unchanged, and a folder of yours with either name is never touched. Guidance, tool descriptions and messages say Combwork, and the skill use-hive-api is now use-cw-api. **Your agents start new conversations once**: a conversation from before remembers the old names, so on its next resume Combwork starts a new one instead, with the same brief, what it was waiting for and its messages (those it had read but not acked shown again), and keeps the old one in Sessions to read. **Scripts**: sessions get `CW_*` variables instead of `HIVE_*` (`CW_API_URL`, `CW_API_TOKEN_FILE`, `CW_PROJECT`, `CW_PROJECT_PATH`, `CW_WORKSPACE`, `CW_AGENT`, `CW_PROVIDER`, `CW_SESSION_ID`, `CW_RUN_ID`, `CW_HOOK_TOKEN`, `CW_SLOT`, `CW_SLOT_PORT_BASE`, `CW_DIALECT`, `CW_DIALECT_FILE`, `CW_PROGRESS`, `CW_PROGRESS_WRAPPED`): a script of your own that reads an old name needs the new one. The Agent API's `app.name` is `Combwork`, and `TERM_PROGRAM` too.<!-- old-name: end changelog-rename -->
- **Listing the board in full leaves each card's history out** (HI-2568). `GET /v1/tasks` (and `cw_list_tasks` with `details: true`) used to give every card's whole history, so the reply grew with each change on every card. Each card now has `historyEntries`, how many entries its history has, in its place. `?history=true` gives the histories back, and one card's read is unchanged (api.version 13).
- **The project header's ⋯ menu no longer lists Project Settings** (HI-2551). It was there twice: open it from **More ▾** on the tabs' row, **Project → Go to Project Settings** or **Alt+0**. The menu's other items, Explorer, Terminal and Remove Project…, show their keyboard shortcuts where they have one, as the other menus do.
- **Settings → Accounts & Connections is tidier** (HI-2528). Each account is a card with the same fields in the same place: who it's signed in as, its plan, its state and, for Claude Code, when its sign-in expires. Each plan limit gets a small bar with how much is used and when it resets ("resets at 23:00", "resets Thu 09:00"). Its connections are a small table inside the card, with a column for each value (*CLI's own* where the CLI decides) and arrows to reorder them. The usage move moved off the card, into the account's **⋯ → Account Settings…**.
- <!-- old-name: begin changelog-rename-2 -->**Combwork's own Advanced settings are named `cw.…`** (HI-2517, HI-2518). Settings → Advanced's first tab, and a setting named on its own ("Combwork: Card attachments: more folders"), say Combwork, and Combwork's own settings' names are `cw.…` instead of `hive.…` (`cw.console.showText`, `cw.cards.attachmentFolders`…). Everything you set keeps its value under the new name, in Combwork's settings and each workspace's own, and `advanced.json` is rewritten with the new names. It is one way: 1.0 opened afterwards shows its defaults for them. Agents, the Assistant and scripts naming an old one are told its new name.
- **Updates, links and the MCP catalogue come from Combwork's repositories** (HI-2472). The app's GitHub repository is now `Suremise/combwork` and its MCP catalogue's `Suremise/combwork-mcp`. Combwork checks for updates there, and its links (Help, the release notes, issues and security reports) open there. 1.0.0, which still asks the old address, is redirected by GitHub to the same releases, so it updates as before. Servers you installed from the catalogue keep their updates: the names upgrade (HI-2587) points them at the new address.
- **MCP server zips and definitions use Combwork's names** (HI-2503). A server's `.zip` carries its definition as `combwork-mcp.json` (or `combwork-mcp.example.json`), and a definition points at the workspace's MCP folder with `${CW_MCP_DIR}`. Servers you installed keep working: the names upgrade (HI-2587) rewrites their stored definitions to the new names. A zip made for 1.0, with only `hive-mcp.json` or `${HIVE_MCP_DIR}`, needs remaking with the new names (HI-2594). A server added under another name has its folder references rewritten as `${CW_MCP_DIR}`.
- **The line Combwork adds to a project's git exclude file says Combwork** (HI-2575). New entries in `.git/info/exclude` read `# Combwork project metadata (added by Combwork)`, and moving a project's `.hive` to `.combwork` rewrites the old "added by Hive" comment above it, so the entry isn't added twice.
- **The other files Combwork writes into your folders say Combwork too** (HI-2580): a new workspace's `.combwork/README.md` (now linking Combwork's repository), its `.gitignore` and the Assistant's, the comment above the skill copies' lines in a repository's `.git/info/exclude`, and the `README.txt` in each launch's folder. The ones 1.0 wrote before are rewritten in Combwork's words once, by the names upgrade (HI-2587, below).
- **The rest of what 1.0 stored takes Combwork's names, once** (HI-2587). The upgrade is one way: the board's database is `.combwork/combwork.db` (it was `hive.db`), with its backups; the log is `combwork.log`; the workspace's `.gitignore` and README, the comments in your repositories' git exclude files, the marker in Combwork's skill copies (`.combwork-copy`) and stored MCP definitions use Combwork's names; and so do the databases' own column names. Each item is checked on its own, so a 1.1 release candidate's install only has what is still old renamed, and an upgrade stopped part-way finishes at the next open. Combwork never deletes a board's log that still holds changes: with no board file beside it, the workspace doesn't open until you put the board back. Performance exports and benchmarks are now `combwork-metrics/1` and `combwork-benchmark/1` files and templates `<name>.combwork-template.json`; Performance exports and templates exported from 1.0 aren't imported (HI-2611): the import says it is one of 1.0's. Templates saved in your workspace and projects are upgraded with the rest. Going back to 1.0 afterwards isn't supported.
- **Combwork stops reading the old names** (HI-2594). After the one-way upgrade, nothing reads an old name: if the installer couldn't rename your old data folders, Combwork renames them itself at start, and if it can't either (a copy of 1.0 is still running), it says so and doesn't start, with nothing changed, rather than run from the old folders. `cw-progress` is the only progress command: the `hive-progress` command is gone, and `##hive-progress` lines are a command's own output. Codex's and Copilot's skill copies named `hive-…` are removed when they are exactly as Combwork copied them. MCP server zips need a `combwork-mcp.json` definition and `${CW_MCP_DIR}`, and `hive` is an ordinary MCP server name. Copy Diagnostics leaves out the text of log lines written before 1.1. A commit's `Hive-Card:` line from before 1.1 is kept in a squash's message as written.
- **The Agent API's `assistant-activity` event no longer lists `revert-setting` or `revertOf`** (HI-2574). They came from the Revert on a setting the Assistant changed, which Undo replaced (HI-2481), so no event has carried them since.
- **The Agent API names itself Combwork** (HI-2519). `GET /v1/health` replies `"app": "Combwork"` and the discovery file `agent-api-discovery.json` says `"app": "Combwork"`, as the Agent API docs already showed; both said `Hive`. A client that checks the name (Apiary) looks for Combwork.
- **Combwork's environment variables are all `CW_*`** (HI-2586). The last `HIVE_*` names are renamed, with no old name kept: a focused-test picker or runner of your own (Workflow → focused tests) reads `CW_FOCUSED_BASE`, `CW_FOCUSED_TESTS`, `CW_FOCUSED_RESULTS` and `CW_FOCUSED_JOBS` (were `HIVE_FOCUSED_*`), and a development or test copy of Combwork takes `CW_USER_DATA`, `CW_API_PORT`, `CW_LANG`, `CW_UPDATE_FEED` and `CW_TEST_*` (were `HIVE_*`). Agents' sessions already got only `CW_*` (HI-2463).
- **Copy Diagnostics and Combwork's log say Combwork** (HI-2572). The report's heading is *Combwork diagnostics*, its version line names Combwork (HI-2577), and the log's start line and its other lines that named the app say Combwork. The upgrade renames the log `combwork.log` (HI-2587), and the report leaves out the text of lines written before 1.1 (HI-2594).
- **Commit trailers and the Agent API's headers say Combwork** (HI-2588). Commits Combwork's merge queue makes, and those agents make where two cards share a folder, name their card with `Combwork-Card: AM-12` (with `Combwork-Revision`, `Combwork-Updated` and `Combwork-Reviewed-By` on the queue's), where they said `Hive-Card` and the rest. Combwork reads only the new trailer: commits from before 1.1 keep theirs as history, but no longer attribute themselves to a card, so a card in a shared folder whose commits name it the old way is asked to amend them. The Agent API's `X-Hive-Workspace` and `X-Hive-Notice` headers are now `X-Combwork-Workspace` and `X-Combwork-Notice`, with no alias: a script that names its workspace with the old header has to change it.
- **The last window text with the old name says Combwork** (HI-2583). Exports are named `combwork-performance-….json` and `combwork-board-…` (they were `hive-…`; their contents, and importing older ones, are unchanged), a report's diagnostics write the app's own folder as `<combwork>`, the For you tooltip names Combwork when the app asks, and the Performance page's tool list heading and translated text that named `.hive` say Combwork and `.combwork` in every language.
- **The installer's "another installer is running" message names Combwork** (HI-2579), in all eight of its languages. Its message about the old app's installer still running is a separate one, not changed here.
- **Text about 0.9 calls it version 0.9** (HI-2623, HI-2625). The board upgrade's dialog and banner, restoring the 0.9 board backup, its tip and command, and the message refusing a 0.9 template said "Combwork 0.9", a version that never existed under that name. They now say version 0.9, in every language.
- **Branches Combwork makes start with `cw/`** (HI-2593). A card's branch is `cw/AM/AM-12-fix-the-login` and an agent's worktree branch `cw/<workspace>/<project>/<agent>` (they were `hive/…`), and the refs that keep submitted revisions and test merges are `refs/cw/cards/…` and `refs/cw/queue/…`. Branches named `hive/…` are left as they are and are no longer treated as Combwork's: a Done card's `hive/…` branch isn't deleted for you, Repair doesn't rename one, and Add Agent doesn't reuse a worktree on one. A card already in progress carries on on its branch.
- **Message ids no longer use the old name** (HI-2589). The ids of Combwork's messages that named it, such as `settings.advanced.hive.slots.deleteMergedLines.desc` or `brief.record.byHive`, are now `settings.advanced.cw.…` and `brief.record.byCombwork`; translations are unchanged. A dialect text saved under an old id is moved to its new id when Combwork upgrades your workspace (HI-2587).
- **Combwork's source and its last internal names say Combwork** (HI-2591). Its files, code names, styles and comments no longer use the old name (with HI-2616's sweep of the comments and docs), except where they name what the upgrade converts. What you or a script may notice: `GET /v1/projects/{name}/slots/releasable` names a held writer's Combwork as `combwork` (it was `hive`), and the API's contract version (`api.version`) is 14; the lock a repository's merges share is `.git/cw-merge.lock` (it was `hive-merge.lock`); an update a release candidate had downloaded is downloaded again, into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\combwork-updater` (it was `hive-updater`); and a benchmark made by a 1.1 release candidate shows no tool-call measures in Performance → Compare, whose measures are now `combworkCalls` and `combworkCallErrors`. The tips you've seen, the .combwork notice you dismissed and the Skills tab's folded groups are kept: the upgrade converts them once as Combwork starts.<!-- old-name: end changelog-rename-2 -->
- **Nothing turns an account on by itself** (HI-2650). An account you add is on in no workspace, and a new workspace starts with none on: turn one on with **On here** in Settings → Accounts & Connections. A new workspace used to start with the first account in the order on. Where accounts exist but are off here, Combwork now says so instead of asking you to add one: an agent's refused start, the project's and the Assistant's banners, a project with no agents yet, Add Agent and Provider Setup name the accounts that are off. With one off here, the banner's **Turn On Here** turns it on in this workspace only; with several, **Choose in Settings** opens Accounts & Connections.
### Added

- **Performance sorts failures by kind** (HI-2553). Most "failed" requests were a workflow rule saying no on purpose (a submit with nothing new, a move the rules don't allow) or a wrong call (a bad argument, an unknown card), so a real error was lost among them. The **Failures** card, the chart and the tools and API tables now show **real errors** in red, apart from calls **refused by a rule** and **wrong calls**, and a new table lists the most common **refusals and wrong calls by reason**, each said as a general sentence (Combwork keeps which rule or message it was, not what it said), so a tool agents keep calling wrong shows up. Failures recorded before this version are **not sorted**.
- **The Combwork tool list by provider** (HI-2553). Performance's Combwork tool list now says which CLI each list went to, and **If deferred**, Combwork's estimate of how much of it Claude Code puts in its prompt at the start when it defers MCP tools (names, and only the few Combwork marks in full); Codex and Copilot get it all. The Guidance per launch card shows the list beside it, and a size budget in Combwork's tests keeps the tool descriptions lean.
- **Merge checks know the card's target** (HI-2617): each check the merge queue runs gets `CW_CHECK_BASE`, the target's commit the merge is tested on, so a check can look at only what the card adds (`git diff $CW_CHECK_BASE...HEAD`).
- **Mark a card as waiting on purpose** (HI-2618). A card in Active that should sit for a while (held for a quiet window, waiting for an answer from someone) can say so: right-click it, **Mark as Waiting…**, and write why in the card dialog's **Waiting** box. Combwork then doesn't report it as stalled, on the board, in its lane or to the Assistant, until you **Clear Waiting** or the card leaves Active; its agent keeps running. Agents can mark their own card (`cw_update_task`'s `waitingFor`), and the Assistant any card.
- **Tag connections, so a usage move keeps each agent on its kind of connection** (HI-2530). A connection can have a tag, any text you like such as *Builders* or *Assistant*: set it in the connection's dialog. When an account's usage move sends its agents to another account, each now goes to the connection there with the same tag, else the same name, else that account's first. Before, only the name was matched, so builders and reviewers could end up on one connection. **The Assistant moves too** now, by the same rule and only between its turns; before, it stayed where it was. The move's notice says how each one was matched ("by tag Builders", "by name", "to its first connection"). The Agent API takes and gives `tag` on connections (api.version 13).
- **Agents can attach files to cards from a folder you choose** (HI-2480). Agents, the Assistant and scripts could attach only files from their project's folders, the workspace and the temp folder, so evidence kept in a folder of your own had to be copied into Temp first. **Settings → Advanced → Card attachments: more folders** lists more folders they may attach from, for all of Combwork or just the workspace (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\…` works). Only you can change it, not the Assistant, and another project's folders and Combwork's own stay off limits. A refused file now says which folders are accepted and where to add one.
- **Agents' temp files can go in a folder of your own** (HI-2496).
