Agents wrote their temporary files, such as Claude Code's scratchpad of working scripts, to Windows' Temp folder, which antivirus scans and which is a poor folder to exclude, as downloads land there too. **Settings → Advanced → Agents' temp folder** (empty by default: nothing changes) gives each agent and the Assistant a folder of its own in a folder you choose, for all of Combwork or just the workspace (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\…` works): Combwork starts them with `TEMP`, `TMP` and `TMPDIR` there, and Claude Code with `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR` too. It can't be inside a project or a slot, only you can change it, and Combwork never deletes anything in it.
- **Dialects: tune the words Combwork gives agents** (HI-2065, HI-2443, HI-2444). Everything Combwork says to agents, from its guidance to its tools' replies and wakes, is now a list of texts a **dialect file** can reword for one model, one CLI or the whole workspace, leaving the rest as Combwork wrote it. The new **Dialects** view in the activity bar shows every text with where it comes from, saves a change only once it has the placeholders it needs, and flags texts an update made outdated. Start a file for a model with **New Model File…** on its CLI's group, even one no agent runs yet (the model ID is typed), or **Duplicate…** one, with a model or as a test copy to try a change on one agent (Agent Settings → **Dialect**); **Set Model…** gives your own file to every agent on that model. A change applies at each agent's next launch; the files show **Restart to apply** until then. Combwork ships empty files for Opus 5.5, Haiku 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, GPT-6.1 Sol and GPT-6 Astra.

### Fixed

- **New Project refuses a key that's taken however soon it's opened** (HI-2653). Opened just after a workspace, before the window had read its projects' keys, the New Project dialog checked the key typed against none: another project's key, or one beginning like it, showed no error. Now the dialog checks it again once the keys are read.
- **A workspace keeps its settings while it opens** (HI-2651). A sign-in check, a settings change or another window opening a workspace at the moment a workspace was opening could save its settings before they were read, writing Combwork's defaults over its own: its connections, commands, workflow defaults and other workspace settings. A change made to them then, such as hiding a project, could be lost too. Now nothing saves a workspace's settings until its open has read them.
- **A card's oldest comments are archived, not lost** (HI-2641). A card keeps its newest 500 comments, and past that the oldest used to be deleted without a word, so design steps and answers that other cards pointed back to were gone. Now they move to the card's archive in the workspace's database. Agents and the Assistant read them with `cw_read_task`'s `archived` (`GET /v1/tasks/{n}/comments/archived`), and reading the card says how many are archived (`commentsArchived`). Export Board saves them as `<n>.comments-archive.json` beside the card. Comments dropped before 1.1 can't be recovered: a card that already had 500 comments says its earlier comments may have been dropped, without guessing how many. Reading a card of more than 100 comments also suggests splitting it into new cards.
- **Undo says a comment stays on a card with 500 comments too** (HI-2642). A comment the Assistant added to a card that already had 500 comments wasn't listed as something undo can't take back, because the card's count of comments didn't change. It is now.
- **A reviewer is told plainly where its commands run** (HI-2640). Start review's reply, which names the review copy, now also says that the reviewer's session and its shell stay in its own folder, so each command must change into the copy (HI-2643 kept the reply short). A Claude Code reviewer on Haiku had run the card's test in the project folder before running it in its copy.
- **A window reopened at start no longer shows no workspace now and then** (HI-2638). If Combwork started while its workspace was still reopening and the window finished loading after that, the window could miss the workspace and show none until it was opened again.
- **A busy workspace keeps its 30 days of performance history** (HI-2628). The history had a 2 MB limit left from when it was a file, so a workspace running many agents lost its oldest hours after about 5 days, well before the 30 it should keep, and nothing said so. The limit is now sized for the workspace's database (32 MB, about six times what a busy workspace needs). If it is ever reached, the oldest history goes as before, and that is now logged too. Folding a week-old day's hours into the day also keeps every total now: before, a day with more different requests and tool calls than it could hold lost the rest; they are now counted under *(other)*.
- **A lane card waiting on its dependencies is no longer reported as stalled** (HI-2618). A lane's next card whose dependencies were still being built in other lanes was reported to the Assistant as stalled again and again (29 times in 50 minutes on 5 Nov), as Combwork briefly lost track of what it waited on while looking at its lanes. The lane now says every card it waits on (*HI-2616 waits to start: it waits for HI-2610, HI-2611 and HI-2612 to merge*), never calls it stalled meanwhile, and counts any stall from when the last of them went Done. With workflow rules off, the lane says the same, and its builder is told once when the last one is Done.
- **A card waiting for its review is reported as stalled on the reviewer that owes it** (HI-2636). The stall named whichever agent was watching the card, so a builder that was only watching another lane's card for Done was blamed for not reviewing it, while the reviewer the pool had given it sat idle. It now names the card's lane reviewer, or the reviewer the pool gave it to, whether or not it watches (*HI-2625 was given to HR3 to review 18 min ago, and it hasn't started reviewing it: in Review for 20 min.*), and nobody while the pool hasn't given it out yet, on the board as well as to the Assistant. Only with workflow rules off does any watching agent count, and then only one that reviews, never a builder.
- **A bundled skill or mode no longer goes missing when an update can't read it for a moment** (HI-2620). While Combwork updates one of its skills or modes in your workspace, it checks the old copy again once it has moved it aside. If that check couldn't read it (access denied, another program holding it, a disk error), the old copy was left aside under a hidden name until the next time the workspace opened. It is now put back at once, as it was, and the update is tried again next time.
- **A Codex agent near its usage limit keeps its connection's model** (HI-2609). Near an account's limit, Codex offers to switch to a cheaper model, with that choice selected, and the Enter of a message Combwork typed into the agent took it: agents ran another model than their connection's, with nothing on screen saying so. Combwork now starts each Codex agent with that offer turned off for the session (**Settings → Advanced → Codex → Don't offer a cheaper model near the limit**, on by default; nothing is written to Codex's files). If Codex shows it anyway, the agent waits for your answer in its terminal and Combwork types nothing into it.
- **Codex's prompts at a workspace's limit wait for you** (HI-2619). On a workspace plan, when a turn stops at the workspace's credit or usage limit, Codex asks whether to notify the workspace's owner or request a limit increase, and a message Combwork typed into the agent could have answered it, even with Yes. The agent now shows **Needs input** with Codex's question, and Combwork types nothing into it until you answer it in its pane.
- **An agent running another model than it was started with is marked** (HI-2609). However its model changed, its footer marks the model with a warning, whose hover says what it was started with; Activity shows *Model changed*, you're told once in a notification, and the Agent API's agent activity adds `launchedModel`. Restarting the agent goes back to its connection's model.
- **A lane's builder starts on its first card at once** (HI-2558). A lane started from the window or by the Assistant gave its builder the brief first: it asked for its card, was told to end its turn while Combwork made the card's branch in its slot, and got the card a turn later. Now, where cards are built in slots and the builder is stopped (or, from **Start Lane**, idle elsewhere), Combwork starts the first card itself and gives the builder the card and its brief together, in one task. When it can't (the card can't start yet, say), the builder gets its brief first, as before, and hears why.
- **A pooled reviewer waiting on your answer gets the pool's other cards meanwhile** (HI-2559). A reviewer that asked you a question about the card it reviews counted as busy until its verdict, so the pool gave it nothing else: another lane's card sat in Review, and its design went unchecked, until someone prompted the reviewer. While its question waits for you, the reviewer is free for the pool's other cards and design checks. It keeps the card it asked about, which goes to nobody else.
- **An agent's commit without the card trailer is no longer refused as "nothing committed"** (HI-2562). Where cards are built in the project folder and two cards' agents work there at once, a commit has to name its card (`Combwork-Card: AM-12`). When it didn't, submitting the card said nothing was committed, and the agent was stuck with its work done. It now says which commit has no trailer and, when it is the folder's newest commit, how to add one (`git commit --amend --no-edit --trailer "Combwork-Card: AM-12"`), and the amended commit submits. When other commits follow it, the agent is told not to amend or rebase, which would rewrite them, and to report it for you to decide. A commit an amend replaced, or one made before the card started, no longer counts against it.
- **A provider's page's Check now checks only that provider** (HI-2550). **Settings → GitHub Copilot → Check now** (and Claude Code's, and Codex's) checked all three CLIs; it now looks again for that page's CLI alone: its install, version, problems and update, as Provider Setup's **Check again** does. **Help → Check Coding Agents for Updates** still checks them all.
- **A project's own settings are kept when its folder appears in the workspace** (HI-2542). A project folder copied or made in the workspace with its `.combwork\project.json` already in it could have that file replaced by a new project's defaults, its agents lost, if Combwork noticed the folder just before the file arrived. Combwork now makes the default file only where there still isn't one.
- **A project's settings written just after its folder appears are kept too** (HI-2563). Combwork gives a new project its id and settles its agents' connections in its `.combwork\project.json` as it takes the folder on. A `project.json` written in that moment, by a copy or a script, could be written over with the defaults and its agents lost. Combwork now reads the file again and makes its change to what was written.
- **A project folder deleted while Combwork is open goes completely** (HI-2564). Deleting a project folder outright (Shift+Delete, `rmdir /s`) removes its `.combwork` first, and Combwork could set the project up again while the rest was still being deleted, so the delete failed and left a folder with only Combwork's files in it. Combwork no longer sets up again a project whose settings have gone while the workspace is open. To reset a project's settings, delete its `.combwork`: it's set up anew the next time you open the workspace, or when you change the project or start an agent in it.
- **An account's Sign In and Set Up terminal opens in its own card** (HI-2541). Settings → Accounts & Connections opened it under the whole list of accounts, so Set Up on an account higher up showed its terminal far from it, below the others. It now opens in that account's card, under its name, scrolled into view.
- **Stopped agents' panes no longer scroll sideways in German** (HI-2536). At 900 px with two panes side by side, Neue Sitzung and Fortsetzen didn't fit on one row and each pane got a horizontal scrollbar; the buttons now wrap, one above the other, when a pane is too narrow for both.
- **Resume says when a conversation from before the rename starts fresh** (HI-2540). Pressing Resume on a conversation from before Combwork renamed the tools agents use asked whether to re-cache its tokens, then started a new conversation anyway and said so only afterwards. Now its Resume says it starts a new conversation (its tooltip, and the session pickers), and pressing it asks **Start a new conversation?** instead, carrying on the agent's work as before. The same goes for one that ran in a folder that moved to `.combwork` in 1.1. The Agent API's reply to such a resume says it started fresh, and why.
- **The "No account is on" warning fits narrow panes** (HI-2535). In a narrow window, with the sidebar and the Assistant open, the warning in a project and in the Assistant's panel squeezed its message into a thin column of six or seven lines beside **Add an Account**. The button now goes under the message when there isn't room beside it.
- **Performance's tables keep names readable in a narrow window** (HI-2534). At 900 px the Cards table squeezed a card's name to a word a line, and the Agent API table broke a route such as `/v1/progress` into fragments; a card's name now keeps a readable width and a route stays on one line, and the table scrolls sideways instead.
- **Files and Skills read in a narrow window** (HI-2533). With nothing selected, a project's Files and Skills tabs explain what to do beside their list, but in a narrow window (900 px with the sidebar open) the list took most of the room and the explanation became a thin column, a word or two a line. The list now gives way so the explanation keeps a readable width, and empty messages elsewhere keep less padding at their sides when space is short.
- **Text typed in Settings → Advanced isn't lost when another value changes** (HI-2537). A list box's text being typed was put back to its saved value whenever the advanced settings changed meanwhile: the workspace's own value saved, advanced.json edited, another window's change. Enter then saved nothing. The box now changes only when the value it shows does.
- **Folder lists in Settings keep what you type** (HI-2538). Card attachments: more folders and Agents' temp folder take folders separated by semicolons, but the box and the Assistant showed a space after each semicolon that you hadn't typed. The box now shows the folders `;` apart, as you typed them, and `a; b` and `a;b` are the same list. Only a semicolon separates folders: a comma is kept as part of a folder's name.
- **Settings' sections can be chosen from the keyboard** (HI-2532). Tab skipped the list of sections in Settings and Project Settings, so only the mouse could change section. Tab now reaches it, the arrow keys, Home and End move through the sections, and Enter or Space opens one.
- **Settings section names cut short show in full on hover** (HI-2531). In a narrow window, Settings and Project Settings cut long section names ("Accounts & C…", "Keyboard Sho…"); hovering one now shows its whole name in a tooltip, and the list keeps its compact rows.
- **Cut-off descriptions in side-panel lists show in full on hover** (HI-2527). Modes, MCP servers, Skills, Dialects, Templates and the MCP catalogue keep each row's description on one line, cut with "…"; hovering a cut one, or reaching it with Tab, now shows the whole text in a tooltip.
- **Clicking the side panel leaves Settings** (HI-2525). With Settings open, the side panel kept showing the last view (Dialects, say), and clicking a row in it changed nothing in the main area, so Settings felt like a modal. A click anywhere in the side panel now takes you to that view, as its activity bar button does, and the gear brings Settings back on the section you were on. Text half typed in a box that only saves on its button (a viewer token's name, a model to price, a catalogue's address) is still there when you come back.
- **A watch on a lane names the lane, not its internal id** (HI-2522). An agent waiting on a lane showed "Waiting on lane lane-1d1c05" in its pane, which reads like a commit id. It now says the lane's name, "Waiting on lane combwork screenshots", with the builder when two lanes share a name, or "Waiting on WB1's lane" for a lane with no name, in every language. Agents' own watch lines name lanes the same way.
- **Agents started by a Combwork inbox line read their inbox first** (HI-2516). The generalist skill's description said to use it when no other skill fits, so an agent woken only by "[CW] New in your inbox…" read it before its task named the right skill. Its description now says it isn't for an inbox line.
- **Settings names the Git projects a Claude Code connection doesn't trust yet** (HI-2520). Combwork counted a project as trusted when a folder above it was, but Claude Code asks about a Git repository unless the repository itself is trusted. So an agent moved to another connection could stop on Claude Code's trust question at launch, while Settings said that connection was set up. Such a connection now shows **Needs set up**, naming the repository, and **Set Up** opens in the repository's folder rather than the workspace's. Upgrading keeps the folders a connection already trusts.
- **An agent no longer waits for hours on a lane that has already ended** (HI-2521). A reviewer that watched its lane a moment after the lane ended (merged) sat watching it until its watch's limit, 6 hours, with nothing left to happen. Watching a lane that has ended or been abandoned now answers at once with how it ended, and a watch naming lanes is always woken when they end, even one that asked only for failed reviews.
- **A card that came back from the merge queue can be resubmitted after a review asks only for proof** (HI-2504). Once the builder merged the target in to fix a conflict and that commit was reviewed, a failed review that asked only for evidence left the builder unable to submit the same commit again: Combwork still counted the old conflict and asked for a new revision. It now takes that resubmission as after any failed review (with a note saying what it adds). Sending the revision that conflicted back unchanged is still refused.
- **A lane's old held-slot status is forgotten when its workspace closes** (HI-2469). Reopening checks what holds the slot afresh. Removing the project also clears its cached status and retry, while other open workspaces keep theirs.
- **A reviewer can no longer take over another reviewer's review by mistake** (HI-2487). A reviewer acting on an old wake could start reviewing a card another reviewer was already reviewing, and silently took the review from it. Combwork now refuses that start, saying who is reviewing it and for how long; you can still take a review over, and one whose reviewer was removed or stopped is free again. A reviewer's watch on a card someone else now reviews ends on that card and says who reviews it, rather than waking it as if the card were its to review.
- **The Assistant asks to stop a busy agent in your language** (HI-2449). Its question in the Assistant's panel, and the notification while the panel is hidden ("Stop <agent> in <project>?", what the agent is doing, Stop and Don't stop), was English whatever language Combwork showed.
- **A lane whose first card can't start yet says which slot is still in use, and by what** (HI-2468). A lane started while its builder was still running, whose slot something still held, said only that its card's start was refused for now. It now names the slot and what holds it: a program the builder's last run left running there, a session, a program with a file open in it, or Windows Sandbox's broker. It follows that until the slot is free. Combwork tries the start again as before.
- **Why a viewer token can't be made is in your language** (HI-2459). Settings → Agent API → Viewer tokens said why it couldn't make one (no name, a name too long or already taken, too many tokens) in English whatever language Combwork showed.
- **The Assistant names the right place for a setting it can't change** (HI-2470). Why it couldn't change *Maximum agents* in Combwork's or the workspace's defaults named a path that doesn't exist (Settings → Workspace → Maximum agents); it now names Settings → Version Control → Workspace defaults (or Combwork defaults) → Maximum agents. Asked to set a setting Settings edits with its own control, such as Accounts & Connections or the check commands, it said "(undefined)" for the reason; it now says the settings tools don't set it, and where you do.
- **A lane waiting for a slot names Windows Sandbox's broker when it holds the slot** (HI-2467). Windows Sandbox's broker, `WindowsSandboxServer`, keeps the folder a Sandbox was started from as its working folder long after the Sandbox has closed, so a Sandbox started from an agent's slot kept Combwork from lending that slot, and the lane said only that a process had a file or folder in it open. Now it says the broker is working in it, and whether a Sandbox is running. Combwork still lends the slot by itself once the broker lets it go.
- **A lane waiting for a slot gets it once the slot is free** (HI-2466). A lane started just after its builder's last one ended could find the only free slot still in use for a moment: the builder's last processes still closing there. It then waited for a slot even after that one came free, until the lane was ended and started again, and said every slot was in use. Combwork now looks again until the slot is free, and the lane says which slot it waits for and what still uses it.
- **A Claude Code session started just after another folder's is found at once** (HI-2446). Combwork keeps the list of Claude Code's transcript folders until that list changes, and judges that by the folder's time, which Windows stamps from a coarse clock. A folder Claude Code made in the same instant Combwork read the list left the time as it was, so a project's first Claude Code session, or a new session's transcript, could go unseen until something else changed there. Combwork now reads a list again until its folder has been unchanged for two seconds.
- **A failed review no longer shows its card as stalled at once** (HI-2445). A builder that watched its card through Review was counted as idle in Doing from when its watch began, so the moment a failed verdict sent the card back, the Assistant's lane watch heard it was stalled while the builder's wake was still on its way. The 5 minutes a builder may only watch its own card now count from when the card came back to Doing; a builder still only watching it after that is flagged as before.
- **Turning the Assistant's Control down, or stopping it, stops its other changes it already asked for too.** A change the Assistant asked for just before, still waiting behind another change, was still made: putting cards in order, a card's move by intent, starting a card on an agent, starting, joining or ending a lane, retrying a lane's new conversations, and cancelling an agent's messages. Now each is refused and nothing of it is saved; a card being started goes back where it was and its agent isn't given it (HI-2420, after HI-2415's cards and comments).
- **A builder's answer wakes its reviewer however it is written** (HI-2428). The fix for a builder's reply to its reviewer (HI-2302) only counted a comment written together with another change. An agent's comment on its own, the usual way to answer, reached nobody, so a reviewer that asked its builder for something and ended its turn sat idle after the answer: seven hours on one card. Now each comment of the builder's in that round wakes its reviewer, the first one or a later one, whether the reviewer is its lane's or the reviewer pool's, and whether or not it kept a wait. The same goes for a lane reviewer's comment before its review, which counts as its question to the builder. A card whose builder replied shows as stalled once neither agent has done anything for 15 minutes, so the Assistant's lane watch hears of it well before the lane's own wait runs out.
- **Cancelling Restart and Update leaves its notification's button.** Restart and Update with agents working (or unsaved files) asks before quitting; cancelling kept Combwork running, but its notification said it was installing and restarting, and lost its button. It now says so only once Combwork really quits to install, and a cancelled quit leaves the button to try again.
- **A cancelled quit dialog leaves no update for your next quit** (HI-2427). Restart and Update (or Restart Combwork) chosen while Combwork's quit dialog was already open only brought that dialog forward, but if you then cancelled it, your next ordinary Quit installed the update and restarted Combwork, even with updates set to install manually. Restart and Update now applies only to a quit that goes ahead from it; choose it again once the dialog is closed.
- **A notification kept only in the bell closes a tip that is showing.** With *Show pop-ups about agents for* set to None, an agent needing you went to the bell, but a tip on screen stayed there until you closed it. Any notification arriving now closes it, as the user guide says.
- **A card read with `comments: 0` gives none of its comments.** `cw_read_task` with `comments: 0`, asked for so as not to see a card's comments, returned every one of them; the Agent API refused `comments=0`. Both now give no comments, with `commentsOmitted` counting them, and none of their attachments (HI-2412).
- **Work on other cards no longer interrupts a waiting agent's wake** (HI-2486). Loop handoffs and answers to For you questions kept restarting their delivery when unrelated board changes arrived, sometimes leaving an idle builder waiting. Delivery now checks its own card, lanes and project settings, so unrelated traffic lets it carry on.
- **Advanced settings keep their latest values when reads overlap** (HI-2454). A slow read of advanced.json could finish after a newer load or saved setting and put old values back in memory. Superseded reads are now ignored; a read still pending when the store stops is ignored too.
- **Switch Them When Their Turn Is Over switches to another connection on the same sign-in.** An agent moved to another connection on the account it runs on, say with another model or effort, kept running on its old one after its turn ended: the switch compared accounts, not connections. Settings → Accounts & Connections' banner now counts the agents its button switches, too (HI-2413).
- **Turning the Assistant's Control down, or stopping it, stops a card or comment it already asked for.** A new card or comment the Assistant asked for just before, still waiting behind another change to the board, was still made. Now it's refused and nothing of it is saved, its files included (HI-2415).
- **Merge a branch merges the branch, even with a tag of the same name.** Merging into the branch the project folder has checked out, a tag named like the branch merged was taken instead: Combwork could say the merge was done when nothing of the branch's newer work was merged. A tag named like the target made the merge refuse, and could stop the agent's branch from moving after a squash. Combwork now names branches by their full refs there (HI-2422).
- **A tag named like a branch no longer renames it `heads/<branch>`.** With a tag of the same name, git's short names showed the project folder's branch as `heads/main` in the status bar and Version Control, and its upstream as missing. Delete branch and Clean Up Merged Branches no longer protected the checked-out branch, locally or on the remote, and Combwork could find no main branch for its worktrees. An unused worktree's Remove anyway named the branch wrongly and kept it. A tag named like `origin/<branch>` lost the remote's default branch. Combwork now reads the full ref wherever it reads a branch's name (HI-2426).
- **A background task's end is no longer reported as your reply.** When a Claude Code agent's background task ended soon after you pressed Enter in its pane (answering a permission question, say), Combwork could tell the Assistant that you had replied to the agent with the task's notification. Only what you send counts as your reply now (HI-2410).

### Security

- **DOMPurify 3.4.17.** Combwork cleans the Markdown it shows (cards, comments, docs, agents' messages) and the Files tab's editor with DOMPurify, and 3.4.16 has three advisories. None applied to how Combwork or the editor use it (they need options neither sets), but the fixed version now ships (HI-2421).
- **An agent's event stream ends when the agent does.** A program following Combwork's events with an agent's own token (`GET /v1/events`) kept receiving its project's events after that agent stopped or restarted, though every new request with the token was refused. Now the stream closes as soon as the token stops working, as a revoked viewer token's streams do (HI-2416).
- **A settings file Combwork can't read is never saved over** (HI-2414). When one of Combwork's files, such as a project's `project.json`, is there but can't be read for a moment (another program has it locked, or a disk error), a change to it is now refused with *… couldn't be read: nothing was changed*, and the file and its `.bak` copy are kept as they are. Before, Combwork could take it for a new file and save its defaults over the project's agents and settings, and over the backup too.

## 1.0.0 — 2026-11-03

**Hive 1.0 runs your board by your project's rules.** Choose how a card goes from Todo to Done (who reviews it, which checks pass first, who merges it, where it is built) with the Solo, Pair or Team presets; agents tell Hive what they did, and Hive moves the card, asks you only what's yours to answer, and can merge approved work itself, each card in its own slot. Agents run on connections Hive keeps (several sign-ins side by side), one notification system tells each thing once, and the Activity view shows what every agent is doing now.

**Before you install: this is a one-way upgrade.** Hive 1.0 upgrades your workspace's board and settings, and Hive 0.9.3 can't use them afterwards. Back up first: the workspace's `.hive` folder (with your agents' worktrees) and `%APPDATA%\Hive`. Then close Hive normally (Quit Hive, never by ending its process), with every 0.9.3 Hive on the workspace closed, before you run the installer. The next section says more.

### Upgrading from 0.9.3

- **Back up first: there's no going back.** The upgrade is one-way. Your agents' own worktrees become *slots* (folders Hive lends to cards), and Hive 0.9.3 can't use them again once slots have moved them. Before you install 1.0, back up the workspace folder (with its `.hive` folder), Hive's own settings folder `%APPDATA%\Hive`, and your agents' worktrees, which Hive keeps beside the workspace in `<workspace>.worktrees` (for `D:\Projects\Work`, `D:\Projects\Work.worktrees`), or wherever you made them.
- **Close every 0.9.3 Hive on the workspace first, normally** (Quit Hive, so it saves everything and lets go of its files), the installed one and any dev or test copy. The upgrade runs only while no other Hive has the workspace open, and otherwise says *Another Hive has this workspace open: close it first*.
- **Hive asks before it upgrades.** The first time 1.0 opens a workspace whose board 0.9 saved, it asks *Upgrade this workspace's board to 1.0?*, saying a 0.9 Hive can then no longer open it and to back up first. Until you agree, the board is read-only and no agent can start, saying so in a banner (with **Upgrade…**) and in the tooltips of what you can't use; **Not Now** asks again the next time Hive starts. The upgrade runs in one go, after one backup: a crash leaves the board as 0.9 had it (#1207, #1209, #1230).
- **What Help → Restore the 0.9 Board Backup… brings back:** the board's cards and their history, as 0.9 left them, after keeping a copy of the 1.0 board. Hive keeps that 0.9 copy for good. It isn't a way back for worktrees, sessions or lanes, and it is refused while any of the workspace's agents is running.
- **Nothing moves on upgrade day.** Every card keeps its column. Hive notes where each card in Doing, Review or Passed stands (its agent, its branch, its submitted change) and marks the other cards as from before 1.0; each project gets workflow options matching how it works today (its version control, a reviewer agent if it has one or runs card loops, its agents' folders), and a banner offers the presets once. A lane running at the upgrade finishes in its builder's old worktree, now a slot kept for that agent, and every agent's conversations resume where they were made. The agent's next card or lane takes the slot over once its work is committed and merged; **Release Slot…** in the agent's ⋯ menu gives one up. Once nothing holds it, Hive renames the folder to a slot name the way Repair moves a worktree (Settings → Advanced, **Rename adopted worktrees to slot names**). A reviewer's old worktree stays where it is, for resuming its conversations. Hive never deletes your agents' branches (#1218, #1441).
- **Hive asks how to merge.** The first time a card of an upgraded Git project reaches the merge queue, the queue waits and asks under For you how to merge the project's cards: the merge style (a merge commit, as 0.9 did, Squash or Rebase) and the commit message (Hive's, with the card's key, or the branch's own). Nothing about how your history looks is set without asking (HI-1697).
- **Projects get keys** (HI, AM…), suggested from their folders and listed in a quiet notice: cards are named `HI-12`, cards you have keep their number in their project (#1061 is HI-1061), and `#N` keeps working everywhere.
- **Scripts that use an agent's token read it from its file.** Agent sessions no longer get `HIVE_API_TOKEN` in their environment, only `HIVE_API_TOKEN_FILE`, a JSON file whose `token` is the agent's own token. A script run in a session reads that file: in PowerShell, `(Get-Content $env:HIVE_API_TOKEN_FILE | ConvertFrom-Json).token`. Agents are also told never to list their environment. In a trial, an agent that ran `env` printed its token (HI-2259, HI-2262).
- **Your 0.9 templates aren't used in 1.0.** They stay where they were, but aren't listed or loaded; the upgrade keeps a copy of each with the 0.9 board backup. Save your agents as a template again, or start from one of Hive's (below).
- **Add a connection, and sign it in once.** Claude Code and Codex agents (and the Assistant) now run on connections Hive keeps, never on your terminal's own sign-in (`~/.claude`, `~/.codex`), which stays as it is. Until one is on in a workspace, its agents can't start: a notice, the project, the Assistant panel and Provider Setup each offer to add one. Every agent keeps exactly what it launched with: its model, effort, context, mode and extra arguments go into a connection on its sign-in (Connections, below). GitHub Copilot keeps its own sign-in.

### One notification system

- **Each thing is told once, in one place.** While you use Hive, an agent finishing or needing you, a lane's or a card's news, plan usage and Hive's own messages are all one kind of **pop-up**, bottom right by default (left of the Assistant's panel), with one look, one stacking and one timing; with Hive in the background, the agents', lanes', cards' and plan usage's come as a Windows notification instead, never as both. A long conversation or a plan usage warning no longer shows twice (a corner toast and a banner, or a Windows notification). Hive's own messages wait in the window, their pop-up paused until you come back (HI-2320).
- **The bell keeps everything.** The Notifications panel now has every notification its window showed or told, agents' finishes and questions too, each linking to its agent's pane, its card or its project. A notification's buttons (End Them, Hand Over to…, Restart and Update, Resume, Allow, Release Slot…) are on its row there too, and stay only while they still apply: done, or no longer needed, the notification says what happened in their place (*Ended 3 processes*, *Answered*). Each button checks again when pressed; End Them ends exactly the processes it listed, by id and start time (HI-2320).
- **The offer to resume agents names everyone it acts on.** After a quit with agents in two workspaces, the one that opened later showed a second notice naming only its own agents, while the first notice's Resume or Don't Resume acted on both. Now the later notice lists every agent still offered and the earlier one says *Replaced by a newer offer*; Resume's buttons go at once, and its notice then says how the resumes went (HI-2356).
- **Esc closes the Notifications panel, and Ctrl+Alt+U toggles it.** The panel used to close only by its ×, and new pop-ups stay in its list while it's open, so one left open hid them (HI-2376).
- **What an action says goes to the window you started it from.** With several Hive windows, the result or warning of something you did in one window pops up there, and is kept under that window's bell, even if you moved to another window while it ran (HI-2355).
- **Tips wait for a quiet moment.** A tip now shows only when nothing else is going on (no other pop-up, no agent waiting for you, nothing typed for a few seconds), in the same place as the pop-ups, so it never comes beside the notification it's about; one that finds no quiet moment that day isn't shown (HI-2320).
- **Settings → Notifications:** *While Hive is focused* is gone (Windows notifications come only when no Hive window is focused). *Show nothing* becomes **Show pop-ups about agents for: None (only in the bell)**, which still gives Windows notifications in the background. **Banner position** is now **Pop-up position**: the old default, top centre, moves to bottom right; another place you chose is kept (HI-2320).

### Hive moves cards by your project's rules

- **Agents say what they did, and Hive moves the card.** Agents have a tool for each step of a card's work: start it, submit it for review, start a review and give a verdict, report its merge, ask you something, say it can't go on within a rule, or (a reviewer) ask the builder for something. Hive checks each step against the project's options, moves the card and records the step on it; a refused step says which rule, what happened and what to do next. An agent that still moves a card the 0.9 way (to Review, to Done) makes the step that move stands for, and is told the tool to use next time. Agents name cards by key (`HI-12`) or `#12`: a bare number is refused, so an old card number never reaches another project's card (#1265, #1267, #1268).
- **A refusal names the step that's the agent's own.** A reviewer refused because its card left Review hears that its review is over: wait for the card's return if it went back for more work, or leave it if it moved on. A builder refused while its card waits in Review is told to wait for the verdict. Stages reach agents by the names your board shows (*Active*, not `active`) (#1604).
- **Your moves follow the rules too, and you can skip them.** Moving a card does what the move stands for: into Active it starts the card's work, into Review it submits what the card's folder has. A drag past a rule (to Done before its review, say) asks first and names the rules it skips; **Move Anyway** records an override with your reason, shown by an open lock on the card. A column the card's rules don't have takes no drop. The Hive Assistant's moves that skip a rule need the reason you gave it; agents can't skip rules (#1265, #1275).
- **One switch turns the rules off.** **Settings → Advanced → Workflow rules are enforced** (on by default): off, cards move wherever you put them and Hive only records what happens to their work. Work merged outside Hive is noticed either way: the card moves to Done, marked *outside the rules* if its review hadn't passed (#1263). A card waiting in Review or Approved is checked for a squash merge with fewer git commands: a "not merged yet" is reused for up to 30 seconds while nothing is committed, so a change only to git's config or `.gitattributes` can take that long to count (HI-2189).
- **No-code cards.** A card can be **No code**: a decision, a write-up or a trial, submitted with a summary instead of commits and Done with nothing to merge. Make one from the New Card dialog; an agent can only propose one, which you confirm.
- **For you.** Cards waiting for you (a review or OK that's yours, an agent's question, a card loop at its round limit) show a **For you** chip, the Board icon counts them, and the board's **For you** filter shows only those. Answer in the card's dialog: Approve or Decline with your reason, reply, or record your answer as a decision; the agent waiting on it hears at once, and a reply stays on the card as your comment (#1626: a reply to a long question no longer reaches the agent cut off). When the answer is something only you can do on the card, the agent can offer it as a button under its question (**Move to Done**, **Make It No Code**, **Unblock**) with its suggested reason: one click does it as the board would, recorded with your reason, and answers the agent (#1630). Only you answer: agents and the Assistant can ask, never answer for you (#1269, #1276).
- **Skip review on a card.** A card that needs no reviewer (an installer build, a quick trial) can skip its review, decided when you make it (**Skip review** in the New Card dialog) or before it is submitted: submitting it finishes it, and a code card still goes on to its merge with its merge checks. It shows **Review skipped**, with who set it and why. The Hive Assistant sets it only with your reason; an agent can only propose it (#1625).
- **Your answer, given in a chat with the Assistant, closes the questions it answers.** The Assistant records it as a decision on the card naming the questions it answers, and Hive closes them: the agents that asked are told and a card loop paused on them goes on. The card's For you section lists them under *Answered by your decisions*, with who recorded each. Approvals and the round limit stay yours to answer in For you, and no agent can close its own question (HI-1932). Recording it confirms the decision and each question it closed, never *No change* (HI-2220).
- **Approve with a note.** "Approved, but change X" has a place: Approve, under For you and in the card's merge queue, takes an optional note. On an agent's question the note is recorded as your decision on the card as well as your comment, so the agent works to it and its reviewer checks it; on Hive's own requests (your review, the OK before merging, the merge queue's update) it's your comment. The box says which. Left empty, Approve is still one click. Declining the merge queue's update now asks why, as For you does (HI-2286).
- **"Need you" counts things, not requests.** The status bar's *n need you*, the window title's count and the taskbar badge count each card with requests for you once, however many requests it has, and an agent stopped on such a card isn't counted again; the tooltip says *2 cards and 1 agent need you*. In the list, a card with several requests shows *3 questions* with each under it (HI-1931).
- **The Tiles view pages only when it must.** A page holds as many tiles as fit the window, not a fixed 12: 13 agents in a large window are all on one page with no page buttons, and a small window pages them, six to a page at least. Resizing pages again, keeping the tile you're on in view (HI-1951).
- **Recent merges in the Progress panel.** Under each merge queue's card, the Merges tab lists what the queue did lately, newest first: merged (with the merge commit to copy), returned (with why), taken out and paused (with why and how long), each with its time and how long the queue took over the card, and, when the project pushes after merging, whether each merge was pushed. Click a row to open its card; it follows the panel's filters and updates as the queue works (HI-1924).
- **A check that doesn't count says why.** `hive-progress --check` prints one line when its run passed but didn't count (a file changed while it ran, uncommitted changes, another checkout), naming what changed and what to do, and the submit's refusal gives the same reason instead of only *hasn't passed* (#1654).
- **A check that just passed counts at once.** Submitting a card straight after its check passed (commit, `hive-progress --check …`, submit) could be refused as *hasn't passed*, because the check was still being recorded; the submit now waits for it (#1651).
- **Hive asks you at a card loop's round limit.** When a card fails as many reviews as its lane's rounds (10 unless you said otherwise), Hive asks you how to go on (more rounds, split it, accept it with follow-ups, or take it over), tells you with a notice and pauses the lane until you answer.
- **Reopening a Done card starts its work again,** with what was done before kept in its Work section; a card brought out of On Hold carries on where it was (#1290).
- **A card's work.** The card dialog's Work section shows what its work went through: its branch, base and folder, each revision submitted with its round, files, verdicts and checks, marks such as a stale pass or an override, and how it was merged. Board cards show their revision, last verdict and checks in one quiet line, and a lane's segments in the Progress panel say the same on hover (#1223, #1224, #1225).
- **Runs count as checks.** A run for a card (`hive-progress --card`, `--check <name>` for one of the project's checks) shows the card and the revision it ran on, and counts as that check of the revision, or says why not: uncommitted changes, something else checked out, files changed while it ran. **Tested on** names the commit a run in a shared folder or a reviewer's copy actually tested (#1220, #1222, #1273).
- **The Progress panel shows why work isn't moving.** A lane's segment gets a red notch when its card's request was refused, amber dashes when its pass went stale, a dot for an override; a lane's header says why it waits (*waiting: AL-1 not merged*, *waiting for you (approval): AL-3*), and the Lanes bar counts those waiting for you.

### Presets and the Version Control tab

- **Merges use the branch's own commit messages by default.** Every preset now merges with the messages your branch's commits have, rather than Hive's card key, title and trailers, since card titles can name people and details a public history shouldn't keep. Choose **Hive's** under **Commit message** in Version Control → Workflow to link each merge to its card. Projects set up before switch once when Hive first opens them, unless you chose Hive's yourself, which they keep.
- **Maximum agents: your team size, in the workflow.** The first workflow option, under **Team**, says how many agents the project has: Solo sets 1, Pair 2, Team and All off 12. Nobody adds agents past it, you included: at it, Add Agent is greyed out saying why, with a way to Version Control → Workflow to raise it (up to 24), and the Hive Assistant (and any program using the Agent API) tells you where to raise it, so a request to "get this done fast" never brings in agents you didn't plan for. It waits for **Save…** like the other options, and lights it as you type or step it (HI-2058). A template's workflow carries its size: loaded with its workflow, a template raises it by itself, saved with its agents; without, one with more agents than the maximum can't be loaded. It replaces **Agents per project** in Settings → Advanced; your value moved over.
- **The Git tab is now Version Control, with the workflow in it.** A project's tab (still **Alt+8**) shows its version control, **None** or **Git**, and what Hive finds in its folder; Git isn't required any more, and a folder with Mercurial or Jujutsu works without version control. Its **Workflow** section holds the workflow options (Project Settings → Workflow opens it), then a Git project's Changes, History, Branches & remotes and Merge queue.
- **Solo, Pair and Team.** The Workflow section groups the options as outcomes (Review, Checks, Merging, Where cards are built, Stages), and a preset fills them in, previewing what it sets, why, and what it would change: **Solo** (committed is done, everyone in the project folder), **Pair** (a reviewer agent, the quick checks before review, each card in a slot, and Hive's merge queue merging each approved card), **Pair (the agent merges)**, and **Team** (Pair plus the unit tests before review and on the merged result, dependencies installed from scratch before every merge, and your OK for cards labelled `ui` or `release`). Options that contradict each other say why; saving shows what changes and keeps running lanes and started cards on the rules they began with. Options can be copied as JSON into another project, and the Assistant can change them within its Control (#1278, #1355, #1361, #1418).
- **"How do you work?" for a new project.** A project added since 1.0 asks once, at the top of its Version Control tab: Solo, Pair or Team, the one that fits its agents suggested. **Customise…** opens the options filled in; **Not Now** keeps the workspace's and Hive's defaults (#1423).
- **Switch a project between None and Git** from the top of its tab, applying from its next card; running lanes and cards in flight finish under the rules they started with. When a folder changes (`git init`, the repository gone), Hive offers the switch, never making it by itself (#1419).
- **The board shows your workflow's stages.** **Doing** and **Passed** are now **Active** and **Approved** wherever Hive shows them (agents and the Agent API keep `doing` and `passed`). Review and Approved appear only when a project uses them, and when Hive merges, **Merging** sits under Approved, filled by the merge queue rather than by drops. Stages can be renamed; the toolbar's **Workflow: Git · Team** opens a project's tab, and a lane's Progress hover names its cards' stages, with a legend on the **Lanes** bar. A lane started before an options change shows **old rules** (#1417, #1420).
- **Compare two branches, such as 1.0.0 with main.** Version Control → Changes compares any two local branches without checking anything out: the commits each has that the other hasn't, the files one changes since it left the other, and a file's diff when you open it. Its Slots group lists every slot with a branch, with its card, or free.
- **Clean up leftover branches from Changes.** Leftover branches lists every branch nothing uses now (a superseded or archived card's, an agent's from before slots), merged or with commits of their own, each with Compare and Delete…, asking as Branches & remotes does. It opens on your target branch against main when they differ, and stays quick across thousands of commits.
- **Branches next to their cards.** Branches & remotes shows the card a branch works on, with its stage, the revision under review and, once Done, the commit that merged it.

### The merge queue

- **Hive merges approved cards itself.** With **Who merges** set to **Hive merges**, each approved card is merged in turn in Hive's own folder, the project's merge checks run on exactly that result, and the target moves only when they pass. A conflict or a failing check sends the card back to its builder with the files or the check's log; a card merged by hand meanwhile is never merged twice. The checks show in the Progress panel as runs of **Hive (merge queue)**; one that fails for the network (Node's or npm's network errors, or exit code 75) lets the builder resubmit the same revision (#1348, #1349, #1362).
- **Into a branch checked out in a folder too.** When the target (`main` in the project folder, say) is checked out, Hive updates that folder after checking that no agent works there, nothing is staged and you have no changes in the files the merge changes; otherwise the queue pauses, naming the files. Each step is recorded, so a crash halfway is finished or undone at the next start. To approve each update first, set **Settings → Advanced → Merge queue: merge into a checked-out branch** to **Ask each time** (#1351).
- **You decide what the queue does.** Pause and resume a queue, take a card out of it, or retry a returned card once as it was approved. The Progress panel's **Merges** tab shows each queue (the card being tested and its step, those waiting, what it sent back with **Show log**), as do a card's Work section and the Version Control tab's **Merge queue** section, with each queue's history as a table, a line a card (#1641); **Merge anyway…** in the Merge dialog merges a queued card by hand, recorded as an override (#1353, #1356, #1357).
- **Merge commits as your repository makes them:** the project's identity, its hooks, and signing as it says. A failing hook sends the card back; a signer waiting for a passphrase or a missing identity pauses the queue with git's message (#1350).
- **Work kept up to date before it merges** (off in every preset): a card whose target moved on after its review is brought up to date by the queue in its own folder, its review still counting if it's the same change.
- **Pushed after merging, if you want** (off by default): **Push the target branch after merging** in Version Control → Workflow has the queue push the target to the remote it tracks after each merge, never forced and with no prompt. A refused or failed push pauses the queue and says why: pull and resume, or turn the option off and resume to keep merging locally. A push Hive still owed when it stopped is made when it starts again (#1179).
- **Your history, your messages:** **Commit message** in Version Control → Workflow sets what the queue's merge commit or squash commit says. Hive's is the card's key and title with Hive's trailers. The branch's own (the default) uses the messages of the card's commits, with no card key or trailers, so with Squash each card lands as one commit with its builder's message. Hive still knows which card each merge was (HI-1697).
- **Each card's focused tests on the merged result** (on in Team when the project has the command that runs them): your project's command chooses the tests a card's change needs when it is submitted (or its builder names them, for its reviewer to check), and the merge queue runs them after the merge checks, picking again for any file the merge changed differently. A choice that fails never passes by default; tests needing a real CLI or the installed app count from the reviewer's run, and a skipped test waits for the reviewer's decision (HI-1367).
- **A linear history, if you want:** the merge style **Rebase and fast-forward** has the queue rebase each card onto the target, test exactly that, and move the target forward to it, with no merge commit. Merge commits on a card's branch are flattened. A conflict, or a flattening that would change the work, returns the card to its builder (#1184).
- **Agents don't merge what Hive merges.** Under Hive merges, an agent's merge report or claim of the merge slot is refused with where the card is in the queue, and a card the queue sends back wakes its builder with what came back (#1354).
- **Two Hives take turns at a repository.** The installed Hive and a dev copy never merge into one repository at once: one waits, saying *Another Hive is merging into this repository*, for up to 30 seconds (**Merges: wait for another Hive**). Each queue is run by one Hive, the other's panel saying *Run by …*, and if that Hive ends unexpectedly the other takes it over within about half a minute. The **Merge** dialog no longer commits a worktree's uncommitted files for you (#1347, #1455).
- **A git command that fails for a moment doesn't stop the queue.** Before anything is merged, a git read that fails (a lock held by another program, a busy PC) sends the card round again a few times, a moment apart; the queue pauses for you only if git keeps failing, with git's own message. A commit identity git couldn't read is no longer reported as missing, and a git command that couldn't run on a busy PC is never taken for git's answer. A test merge whose commit couldn't start is tried again too (HI-2020). Your Pause queue, or a change to the card, made meanwhile still stands (HI-2019).
- **A git command the queue's own processes saw ended by a signal is never taken for git's answer.** It was read as git's exit 1 ("not an ancestor", "not set"), and a merge, rebase or commit ended so could be taken for a hook's refusal; now the card goes round again, as for a git that couldn't start. On Windows a process another program ends exits with the code it was given, which still can't be told from git's own (HI-2042).
- **A hook's refusal of the queue's merge commit is told as one on a busy machine too.** The queue read a failed commit's output as soon as git exited, and on a loaded machine its end (where git says a hook refused) could still be coming, so the refusal could pause the queue for you instead of returning the card. Now its output is read whole; an output another process holds open after git has exited is taken after a quiet spell, so a merge never waits on it (HI-2043).
- **A failed read of the repository's settings is never taken for "not set".** Where the queue pushes a target, and whether the repository signs its commits, were read as "no remote" (pausing to ask you for one) and "unsigned" (merging without signing's time limit) when git couldn't read them for a moment. Now the push pauses as "couldn't read" until you resume it, and the merge goes round again (HI-2054).
- **The merge queue's git starts faster.** Each git command the queue runs started a helper process of Hive's first, which took about 80 ms. One helper per queue run now starts them all, so a card merges about a second sooner, with half as many processes. A push and a signed commit still get a helper each, which can end them if they wait too long (HI-2079).
- **A card the merge queue sends back says why, first.** An agent watching its approved card was told *is in Active; latest comment: Round 2: PASSED* when the queue returned it for a conflict, so it took the card as merged and moved on. Now everything it's told about that card starts with the return, why and what to do next, and Hive leaves a comment on the card saying so (HI-2087).
- **A card submitted again after the merge queue sent it back wakes its reviewer.** A reviewer watching its passed card didn't hear when the builder resolved the conflict and submitted it again, because the card was back in Review, where it had been when the watch began. Now a card that leaves and comes back counts, and the reviewer is told it is back after the queue returned it, with the files resolved, and to review the resolution (HI-2143).
- **Nothing the merge queue runs outlives it.** The queue's reads of the repository (its status, where the branch is checked out, what is already merged) ran outside the queue's own processes, so one stuck in a slow disk, a filter or an fsmonitor hook kept running after the queue stopped, and another Hive taking the queue over couldn't end it. They now run with the queue's other git, and end with it (HI-2078).
- **The merge queue starts one git process fewer for each card.** Its commit identity is read in one git rather than two, so a merge runs 26 git processes for a card where it ran 27 (strict 37, from 38). Every check it makes is still made (HI-2050).

### Slots and review copies

- **A no-code card takes no slot.** It changes nothing, so it starts where its agent is, even when every slot is in use, and a lane's no-code card leaves the lane's slot to the lane. That holds however it's started: from the window, by the Hive Assistant or by its lane, no longer refused as having got no slot (HI-2395). Code cards still wait for a free slot (HI-2335).
- **A lane of only no-code cards starts at once.** Started from the window or by the Hive Assistant, it no longer waits for a free slot: its agents start where they are. If one of its cards becomes a code card later, that card gets the lane a slot when it starts (HI-2338).
- **Each card in a slot, on a branch of its own.** With **Where cards are built → Each card in its own folder**, Hive keeps a pool of worktrees per project (*slots*), set up by the project's setup script as Hive's own runs, and lends one to each card or lane: the card's branch is made there from the target (`hive/AM/AM-12-fix-the-login`), the builder works there in a new conversation, and a lane keeps its slot from card to card. With every slot in use, the card waits for one. A card's branch is deleted once it is merged, and idle slots are removed only when nothing would be lost (Settings → Advanced) (#1429, #1438).
- **Agents aren't pinned to worktrees.** Add Agent puts an agent in the project folder; a builder takes a slot for each card. Beside an agent's name: **in slot-b**, **carried: hb2** (its old worktree, kept for it), or **home: hr4** (a reviewer's old one). A conversation always resumes where it was made; once its slot holds other work, Hive says why and offers **Read Transcript** and **Start Fresh with a Handover** (#1434, #1442, #1451).
- **Slots and copies** in the Version Control tab's Workflow section holds the slots' setup and archive scripts, the files copied into new slots, the slots in use and ready, and the number of review and check copies (#1445).
- **Reviewers review in review copies.** At the start of a review Hive gives the reviewer a folder at exactly the submitted revision, which nothing else writes; checks it runs there count for that submission, and its verdict hands the copy back. The copies folder is given to the reviewer's CLI on its command line (`--add-dir`), never in your CLI settings. **Settings → Advanced → Reviewers review in review copies** turns it off (#1439, #1559). Two reviews starting at once no longer fail now and then to get their copies: Hive makes a project's copies, slots and worktrees one at a time (HI-2322), and tries again when another Hive, an agent or you were adding a worktree to the same repository at that moment (HI-2349).
- **Isolated checks.** `hive-progress --check <name> --isolated` runs a check in a copy of its own at the card's revision, so another agent's edits in a shared folder can't spoil it (#1435).
- **A reviewer pool.** With **Reviewers → A shared pool**, Start Lane offers *The reviewer pool*: each submitted card goes to the next free reviewer, never the agent that built it (#1443). A pool lane's builder can have its design checked before building: the design goes to the next free reviewer, who then reviews the card when it's submitted if free (HI-2310). Its answer wakes the builder, and the builder going ahead unchecked wakes that reviewer, before the card's first submission too (HI-2325). A reviewer watching a card still counts as free, so a one-reviewer pool no longer stalls once its reviewer starts watching (HI-2333). Once a revised design or a later round goes to another reviewer, the earlier one is out of the card's loop, never shown as *not watching* it (HI-2321).
- **Pause the team before an update.** **Pause the Team** (the Progress panel's Lanes, the command palette, Help → Check for Updates) holds every open workspace's lanes, card wakes, pool reviews and merge queue, tells each running agent once to park, and lists what still runs (with **End It…** for a process, or **Show Its Window** for another open workspace's, HI-2095) until it says **All parked: safe to install**, with **Install Now**. Nothing restarts on its own until you resume the team; a pause lasts only while Hive runs, so Hive always opens with the team running (HI-2156). The Hive Assistant can pause its own workspace's team too (HI-2081).
- **No false stall while an agent waits for your answer.** An agent that asked you something on its card (under **For you**) and ended its turn is shown as waiting for you, no longer as stalled for not watching the card: your answer wakes it. A reviewer that asked you while reviewing isn't shown as stalled for only watching the card either. Once you answer, the usual stall rules apply again (HI-2080).
- **The Assistant can't leave lanes unwatched by mistake.** When the Hive Assistant ends a turn while lanes are running and no watch of its covers them, Hive keeps it going once to say so, naming the lanes' builders, so it watches them before it finishes: a question or a stall in a lane no longer waits for hours. Settings → Advanced → **Lanes: remind the Assistant to watch them** turns it off (HI-2069).
- **Cards reviewed outside a lane too.** A card that reaches Review outside a lane, in a project whose work an agent reviews, goes to a free reviewer of the project the same way (a stopped one is started for it), with no step from you or the Hive Assistant (HI-2046).
- **Roles and templates for 1.0.** Each agent has a **Role in the workflow** (Builder, Reviewer or Either); agents from before 1.0 read as their names suggest, and the old free-text role is now the agent's Note. Hive comes with six templates (Solo, Pair, Pair: one provider, Team 3×3, Team 6×6, Team 6 + pool), named from the project's key (HI-B1, HI-R1…), and loading one offers its preset (#1430, #1436, #1440).
- **Free a stuck slot.** When Hive can't tell that what an agent's session started in a slot has ended (Hive closed with it running, say), it keeps the slot. **Release Slot…** in the agent's ⋯ menu, or **Release…** in Slots and copies, frees it, offering to end what's left running first; the Assistant can too, with Control agents (#1506, #1561).
- **Repair, Remove Project and Unused worktrees know slots.** After a workspace moves, Repair finds the slots, review copies and reviewers' old folders and links them again with their cards' work and conversations. Remove from Hive removes clean, merged slots through Hive's guarded clean-up; Delete sends them to the Recycle Bin. **Unused worktrees** lists a slot only when it needs you, and **Merge a branch** (⑂ in Branches & remotes) merges any branch, a card's unfinished one after you confirm (#1447).
- **Every way into Done finishes a card the same way.** Merged by Hive, reported by its builder (checked or not), moved by you or the Hive Assistant, accepted, or moved on a board with no rules: the card's slot leaves its branch (detached at the card's target, so a merged branch is deleted at once and the next card starts from the line the lane works to, not the branch the slot was made from: HI-2185), its questions close, its lane goes on, and cards that depend on one you moved to Done by an override can start, told so on their card. A slot with uncommitted changes stays on the card's branch and For you asks you about it; the lane's next card waits until it's clean (HI-2014, HI-1986).
- **The Progress panel shows slots and copies:** a lane's slot (*HI-B1 / HI-R1 · slot-b*) or pool, a check run in a copy (*AM-12 · a3f2c1d · in copy-2*), and *waiting: preparing slot-b* (#1446).

### Lanes and card loops

- **Progress → Lanes shows a lane's queued list change.** A change to a lane's cards made while one is being worked waits until that card is Done; the Lanes tab now says what it will do under the lane's bar (*Next, once HI-12 is Done: HI-14 added, HI-9 moves to HB6's lane*), and the line goes once it applies (HI-2151).
- **A lane's end lets its builder finish.** When a lane's last card merges (or you abandon the lane), its builder is no longer stopped mid-reply: Hive waits for its turn to end, tells it the lane is finished with each card's merge commit, then ends its CLI the way you would (`/exit` in Claude Code, `/quit` in Codex), never over what you're typing, killing it only if it doesn't exit. Its slot stays its until then; after 10 minutes Hive ends it anyway. **Settings → Advanced → Lanes: let the builder finish when its lane ends** turns it off (HI-1706).
- **Hive sets a lane agent's wait after its handoff.** A builder that sends its card to Review, or a reviewer that fails one, now waits for the other side without setting the wait itself: Hive starts it, for the lane's wait, and says so. Builders that stopped without waiting had their lanes shown as stalled (#1639).
- **Start a lane yourself.** Right-click the Todo column for **Start Lane with These Cards…**: choose the builder and reviewer, order the cards and add a note; both start in a new conversation with a brief. A lane's row has **End Lane** and **Abandon Lane…** (#1280).
- **Hive keeps each lane's list, one card at a time.** A lane's next card starts once the one before it is Done, and the lane says why it waits. A change to a running lane's cards waits until the card being worked is Done, and the builder is told. A lane ends by itself once all its cards are Done, with the merge commit Hive recorded (#1270).
- **Hive starts a lane's next card itself.** With workflow rules on, once a lane's card is Done Hive gives the next one to the lane's builder as soon as its turn is over (or starts it, if it isn't running), so a lane no longer sits still until you or the Assistant prompt the builder. The builder's wake for the Done card names the next card, or says it was the lane's last (HI-1990).
- **A builder that starts its lane's next card itself still gets the lane's instructions.** Some agents (Codex) take the next card rather than wait for Hive's start: the start's reply now gives them what Hive's start would have, the card loop to follow and the lane's brief (HI-2092).
- **A list change that arrives as its lane ends keeps the lane's brief.** When the Assistant changes a lane's cards just as its last card merges, the new lane Hive starts now carries the ended lane's brief and name, leaves out cards already Done, and says both in its reply (HI-2097).
- **Reorder a running lane now.** **Apply now** in Start Lane (and `applyNow` for the Assistant and the Agent API) applies a new order at once: the card being worked goes back to Todo with its work kept on its branch, and the builder is told. No more abandoning a lane to move its card (HI-1995).
- **A lane's busy agent starts afresh once it is free.** An agent that is busy just as its lane starts (reading a message Hive had just given it) no longer stays in its old conversation: Hive starts it in a new one with the lane's brief once its turn is over, and the lane's row says so meanwhile. If it isn't free in time, Hive stops trying and tells you: **Retry New Conversation** on the lane's row tries again (HI-2034).
- **A lane's first card starts too.** A lane started over a running agent no longer sits "active" with its first card untouched: Hive starts it once the agent is free, and until then the lane's row says why it waits. A new lane's task for its builder is no longer cancelled as an out-of-date brief when it is offered a little after the lane starts (HI-2029).
- **A new lane from the Assistant starts its idle builder afresh.** Where cards aren't built in slots, a lane the Hive Assistant starts for an idle builder that is still running now gives it the lane in a new conversation, as Start Lane does, rather than carrying on in its last one; the last stays in its Sessions history. A builder in the middle of a turn is left as it is (HI-2232).
- **A lane card waiting on another card's merge is no longer called stalled.** Where your project's rules start a card only once the cards it depends on are Done, a lane whose next card waits for one says so (*HI-12 waits to start: it waits for HI-11 to merge*), and Hive starts it once that card is merged, so it no longer shows as stalled after 15 minutes while its builder waits (HI-2129).
- **A new card goes on a lane as it is made.** The Hive Assistant and agents create a card straight onto a lane (`hive_create_task`'s `lane`, `POST /v1/tasks`), so they no longer guess the new card's key: a guess had started a lane on another agent's card that took the number. A lane started with a card someone else created moments before now asks whether it is the card meant.
- **A lane keeps the cards you added to it when its builder registers.** A builder starting its card loop named only its first card, which took off a second card the Hive Assistant had just added: the lane ended once the first merged and the second stayed in Todo. A builder's own lane start now only adds or reorders cards; you and the Assistant still take cards off.
- **A lane never waits on another agent's card.** A card in a lane's list that another agent has (moved to another lane) is no longer taken as its next card, never pauses it, and never holds up a change to its list: taking cards out applies at once. Starting a lane with a card another lane lists warns (HI-1821).
- **Loops wake each side when it matters,** without the Assistant too: the builder for a verdict, your move to Approved or Done, its reviewer's question mid-review or a decision you record; the reviewer for a card arriving in Review, a design to check before building or a follow-up to check (each unanswered too long shows its card as stalled). An agent waiting on a card that's since Done, or a lane that ended, is told so and goes idle. Hive notifies you when a lane's review fails, a card is blocked or stalls, or every card has passed so the merge is due; **Card loops → Wake reviewers and builders** in Project Settings turns these off (#1266, #1272, #1410, #1475).
- **A follow-up during a review is no longer a round.** A commit the reviewer asks for while it reviews a card is submitted as a follow-up: the card stays in Review and its builder's, no round is counted, the history says so, and the reviewer is woken to review that commit, its review copy moved to it. A reviewer moving a card it reviews back to Doing no longer takes the card from its builder or ends the builder's wait (HI-1832).
- **A builder is told its card in Review has commits it hasn't handed over.** When a builder watches its own card in Review (or asks its lane for the next card) and the card's folder has commits on its line past the submitted one, Hive's reply says how many and to submit them as a follow-up first, rather than to end its turn: agents took the card's column for the hand-over (HI-2245).
- **A lane's builder that waits on nobody is nudged, then shown as stalled.** A builder that starts watching its own card while nothing on it waits on anyone (it's in Doing and not submitted, with no question open for you and no design step out for its reviewer) is told so at once: carry on with the work, or ask someone. If it keeps only watching, its lane reports the card as stalled after 5 minutes, so the Assistant and you hear of it without asking.
- **Start fresh at natural breaks.** **Start Fresh (Idle Agents)…** in a project's Agents menu gives each idle agent a new conversation, the old ones kept; Hive suggests it once when a project's work has finished (#1437).

### The Hive Assistant

- **Undo the Assistant's changes.** Hive keeps what the Assistant changes for 7 days, grouped by your message. **Done by the Assistant** in its panel lists them, each with **Undo**, and a message's **Undo** takes back all of its changes, keeping what you or an agent changed since and saying so; you can also ask the Assistant to undo. It no longer stops after 30 changes for one message (#1170, #1282, #1283).
- **It asks you less.** Its skills and modes describe what Hive now decides (a lane's review, its round limit, who merges, a lane's end), so it doesn't ask you about them; Coordinator looks a fact up before asking you, and Release manager asks for each step that builds, versions, merges, tags or publishes (#1527, #1528).
- **It asks before putting a card in Active with nobody on it** (HI-2062): asked only to move a card to Doing, the Assistant could leave it there with no agent. Hive now refuses that move and has the Assistant ask you which you meant: nobody on it (with your reason, kept on the card), an agent on it, or an agent started on it.
- **A fold button on its panel** (Ctrl+Alt+I), as the Progress panel has (#1330).
- **It sets a team up from a template.** Ask the Assistant to load a template into a project (*"load Team 3×3 into web, and call the first builder Coder"*): it shows who goes and who comes, names another project already uses (with free ones), and what the template's workflow would change, then loads it as Load template does and saves its workflow options, never setting up Git for you. **Undo** puts the project's agents, layout and options back (HI-1502).
- **It sees an agent's open questions on a card first** (HI-1992): reading a card, the Assistant and agents get its questions and requests waiting for you just after its decisions (who asked, what, and when), whichever way they read it, even only its latest comment, so it doesn't miss why a card loop waits for you.
- **The Assistant's notifications open what they're about** (HI-1961): a notification about one agent or one card ("HB4 needs an Enter in its pane") opens that agent's pane or the card when you click it, in the corner or in the Notifications panel. If the agent or card is gone by then, Hive says so. Agents and scripts give it with `hive_notify`'s new `target`.
- **An agent's question for you always wakes the Assistant** (HI-1991): a builder or reviewer asking you something on a lane's card (it shows under For you) now wakes the Assistant's lane watch, naming the card and the question's first line, even when the Assistant had stopped following lane pauses. A lane pausing for a merge is now an event of its own (`paused-merge`), so the Assistant can leave out merge noise without missing your questions.
- **Skills as contents pages** (HI-2011): Hive's long skills keep a short `SKILL.md` (at most 4,000 characters) that links files beside it, which agents read only when they need that part; `card-loop` gives the builder and the reviewer a file each. A skill's page shows its files as tabs, each readable and editable, and a link to one opens its tab.
- **Shorter hive tool descriptions** (HI-2012): every session is given all of Hive's tools, and their descriptions were long, some over 1,000 characters. Each now says what the tool does and the rule a caller can't guess; its cases, defaults and options are in the `use-hive-api` skill's `tools.md`, which agents and now the Assistant read only when they need them. Rules that protect your work (asking you before a release, hold and Done being yours) stay in the descriptions.
- **A card chip keeps its key on one line** (HI-2000): a chip with a long title, such as in a Journal entry's Links, no longer breaks its key at the hyphen ("HI-" over "1985"); only the title is cut.
- **A builder isn't woken by its own submission** (HI-2015): an agent watching its card was woken when its own submission moved the card into Review, a wasted turn each time. Its own moves no longer wake its watch; the reviewer's verdict still does.
- **The Assistant follows an agent's build beside its lanes** (HI-1987): asked to tell you when an agent's work on no card is done while lanes run, the Assistant can follow that agent in the same watch as the lanes (*Waiting on all running lanes and B2*), instead of swapping its lane watch for an agent watch and missing the lanes meanwhile. If it swaps anyway, Hive's reply tells it which running lanes it stopped following.

### Connections
