- **Move an account's agents before its plan runs out.** On an account in **Settings → Accounts & Connections**, choose *When it reaches 98% of its weekly limit (or its 5-hour limit, or either), move its agents to* another account: its agents (never the Hive Assistant) move to the matching connection there once their turn is over, resuming their conversations, once per limit period and never back on their own. A notice says who moved. The Assistant sees each account's usage in `hive_connection list` and can wait for a threshold (`hive_wait_for_tasks` with `usage`). **Settings → Advanced → Move agents at a usage threshold** turns it off (HI-2195).
- **Several sign-ins, side by side.** **Settings → Accounts & Connections** keeps Claude Code and Codex sign-ins, each in a folder of its own and signed in through the CLI's own sign-in: two Max plans, a work and a personal account. Each workspace chooses which are on; every agent, and the Assistant, runs on the connection it chose. Conversations are shared between them (Codex keeps its memory per connection). Switch an agent's connection in Agent Settings: a running one moves at its next safe point (HI-980, HI-1755, HI-1757).
- **A connection can get its model from a server** with a base URL and key: DeepSeek, Ollama on this PC, or your own gateway, each model standing in for a Claude name. Read Models lists what the server has. Its key is stored encrypted and masked wherever Hive prints it. A conversation run only on this PC is **free (local)**; Ollama's cloud models and other servers are priced only by your own prices, never by the CLI's Claude price (HI-1772, HI-1773, HI-1825).
- **Templates choose connections when you load them.** A template keeps each agent's name, role, note, MCP servers and the coding agent it suggests, never a connection, model or effort. Loading one has a **Connections** section: one connection for each role (each starting on the first connection of the CLI the template suggests), **Same for all**, or **Per agent**. **Project Settings → Agents → Choose Connections…** does the same for a project's agents as they are, changing only their connections ("high effort for every builder here"), and the Assistant can do both. Templates saved by the release candidates load without their models and connections, nothing else lost (HI-2109).
- **Accounts and connections.** A sign-in or server is now an account, and a connection a named setup on it (its model, effort, context, permission mode and extra arguments): an agent and the Assistant launch from their connection alone, with no agent, project or CLI setting on top. Updating moves each agent and the Assistant onto the connection with exactly what it launched with before (made where none matches, named after what differs, shared by agents set up alike), so nothing they run with changes; GitHub Copilot's PC-wide sign-in becomes an account too. There is no default provider any more: new agents go on the first connection in your order of accounts that's on here. An agent whose connection is off or removed starts on its CLI's first one here, saying so, and an effort its model doesn't take is refused when you choose it (HI-2106).
- **Settings → Accounts & Connections** (replacing Settings → Connections) lists your accounts in one order for every CLI, each with who it's signed in as, its plan usage, **On here** and what it needs (Set Up, Sign In), and under it its connections: each with a summary of its values, who uses it, and **Edit**, **Duplicate** and **Remove**. **Add Connection** makes another setup on a sign-in ("Claude · High" beside "Claude · Medium"); its dialog has Name, Model, Effort, Context, Permission mode and Extra arguments, and saving offers running agents on it a restart. GitHub Copilot's sign-in is listed as its account. **Settings → Providers** keeps only the CLI itself (on or off, its path, updates, fallback lists, prices); the default provider, the providers' order and each CLI's default model, effort, mode, arguments and context are gone, as are Project Settings' Claude Code, Codex and Copilot pages and Default provider (HI-2107).
- **A 0.9 default provider no longer counts anywhere.** A new template agent suggests the first usable connection's CLI, a project with no agents names that CLI and warns only when no account is on here or none of their CLIs is installed (no longer *Codex is turned off* for an old default), and the providers banner and status bar warn about the CLIs agents and new agents actually use (HI-2132).
- **Coding agents are listed in Hive's order** (Claude Code, Codex, GitHub Copilot) everywhere: Settings, Provider Setup, Settings → Advanced's tabs, the Sessions tab and the overviews. An order you set in 0.9's Settings → Providers is no longer kept; the order you set is your accounts' (HI-2139).
- **Model, effort and context belong to the connection.** The rows, the Overview, the status bar's plan limits and the Agent API name the connection each agent runs on; the plan limits are per connection, Codex's included (from rc.9: until then a Codex connection's limits never reached the status bar) (HI-1756, HI-1769, HI-1770, HI-1771, HI-1882).
- **Agents and the Assistant choose a connection, and nothing else.** **Add Agent** asks for a name, a **Connection** (grouped by account, the first in your order chosen; **New Connection…** makes another setup there), a note, its role and MCP servers: no provider, model, effort or mode, which come from the connection. **Agent Settings** changes its connection (another CLI's only while it is stopped), with a line summing up what it sets. **Assistant Settings** has just **Connection** and **Mode**, and Settings → Assistant loses its provider and per-provider values. The guide explains running the Assistant on DeepSeek through a Claude Code server connection, with its caveats. An agent whose connection was removed, or is off in this workspace, says so in its pane and a notice names the one it started on (HI-2108). With no account on yet, Add Agent links to Accounts & Connections and greys out **Start a session now**, saying why: the agent can still be added for later (HI-2127).
- **Agent API:** `hive_add_agent` and `hive_update_agent` take `connection` only: `provider`, `model`, `effort`, `mode` and `context200k` are refused, the reply naming the connections to use (api.version 12). The new **`hive_connection`** lists connections and, with Settings → Assistant → Control → Change settings on and your OK, lets the Assistant add one on an existing account or change one, for example High effort for a project's builders (HI-2108).
- **A stopped agent's mode badge** lists the connections on its account with their modes, the one it uses ticked: choosing one moves the agent there, and **Edit "*connection*"…** opens that connection's dialog. It no longer changes a hidden mode of the agent's own, which nothing used (HI-2122).
- **Starting a card on a new agent chooses its connection too.** The board's **Start…** (and **Move to Active → Assign and start**) asks a new agent's **Connection** where it asked its provider, the first in your order chosen; the Assistant's `hive_start_task` takes `connection` and refuses `provider`. A project's **Skills** tab starts on the CLI of your first usable connection (HI-2121).
- **A launch refused for extra arguments with a bare `--` names the connection to fix,** in Settings → Accounts & Connections, where it pointed to Project Settings and Settings → Assistant (HI-2140).
- **The connection dialog warns when Claude Code won't run Auto with the model chosen** (Haiku 4.5: it runs in Manual instead), as Settings → Assistant did before connections (HI-2145).
- **GitHub Copilot signs in once for the whole PC,** and that sign-in is its account; its agents choose a connection on it like any other (HI-1758, HI-2107).
- **Codex connections without admin prompts.** With several Codex connections, each one's admin sandbox setup reset the others', so they kept asking for Administrator permission. **Settings → Advanced → Codex → Connections: use Codex's non-admin sandbox** (off by default) runs Codex on every connection in its non-admin sandbox instead, without changing Codex's config; the user guide says what it gives up, that Codex's sandbox protects writes but not reads of secrets, and why a connection's first commands in a large project can be slow (HI-1893).

### Agents and their guidance

- **Agents' skills and guidance describe what Hive does.** The bundled skills name the steps agents take and leave the rules to Hive's replies and refusals; a builder works in the slot its card's start names, a reviewer in its review copy. Hive's replies name the one step that comes next on a card, and agents act on an inbox item, then ack it; one `hive_inbox` call can ack and read what's new. Workspaces' untouched copies of the skills update by themselves (#903, #1274, #1444, #1524–#1531).
- **Agents take a refusal's next step without asking you,** unless it would discard or overwrite work or needs your decision (#1609), or is a commit you didn't ask for. "Submit" in Hive is always *submit for review*: it hands committed work over and never commits. Asked only to submit uncommitted work, an agent is refused with "Nothing committed to review…" and asks you before committing; asked to commit and submit, or working the card itself (in a card loop, say), it commits and submits (HI-1618, HI-2257). Asked to merge a branch or a lane, an agent uses `merge-ready`, which merges through the merge slot (#1610), and the Assistant's brief for each agent of a new lane names its role in the card loop (#1607).
- **Workspace notes for every agent.** Shared Notes has a pinned **Workspace notes** for what's true of this machine; Hive adds it to every agent's instructions on any CLI, a section headed with a project's name going only to that project's agents (at most 4 KB).
- **Choose which skills agents get.** **Delivered to agents** beside each Hive skill says whether agents get it from their next session, and Hive removes the skill copies it made for Codex and Copilot once its last agent there stops (#1431).
- **Choose how agents hear of their inbox.** Settings → Advanced has a switch for each way Hive gets an agent to read its inbox (at the end of a tool, when it would stop, when it's idle), each on as before; a project can set its own. Nothing is lost with one off: with wakes off, the pane's **Deliver Now** types the line once (#1432).
- **An agent whose hooks stop arriving says so:** *Status unknown: no hooks from Claude Code*, with a banner saying why and **Restart session**, and its lane pauses until they come back (#1336, #1371).
- **Claude Code agents call Hive's most used tools without loading them first** (HI-2229): an agent's inbox, card reading and card list (and the Assistant's project status and agent activity) come with their full description, so a model needn't guess their arguments; the rest still load on demand. A hive call refused for an argument its tool doesn't take, or one it left out, now says which arguments the tool takes.
- **Rules weaker models skipped are where they read** (HI-2226): a reviewer checks the builder's run record before running anything, from the review skill itself; running a card's checks takes an agent to its card skill, with its isolated checks; and the Assistant's skill has its rule for an agent's push or release OK and when to bring you a simpler option for a finding that keeps coming back. The Assistant no longer suggests a fresh start for a project's agents: Hive's own notice does that (#1437).
- **Reviews, follow-ups and your decisions go as they should on lighter models** (HI-2228): a reviewer Hive starts is pointed to the review skill rather than a list of steps; a builder's change on a card in Review is handed over before it waits; and the Assistant leaves your decision on the card for Hive to pass on, rather than typing it into a busy agent.
- **Leaner card reads for agents** (HI-2402): reading a card's latest comment, its newest comments or its summary no longer brings back every file attached to the card, only those of the comments read, with a count of the rest. A busy card's latest step costs agents far fewer tokens; a full read still lists them all.

### Switches for Hive's changes to the CLIs

Every change Hive makes to how a CLI behaves now has a switch in Settings → Advanced, each defaulting to what Hive did before, or a reason in the user guide why it can't. Among them:

- **The CLI's own setting**, a permission choice for every CLI: Hive passes no mode, and the badge says **CLI's own** (not offered to the Assistant). **Pre-approve Hive's tools**, **Only the workspace's MCP servers** and, for Copilot, **Keep GH_TOKEN and GITHUB_TOKEN from Copilot** (#1281, #1284).
- **Hive's status line in place of yours** (Claude Code; off, your own runs after Hive's) and **Hive's guidance for agents**; on Codex, Hive's guidance now follows your own `developer_instructions` instead of replacing them (#1285).
- **Ctrl+C and Ctrl+V go to the CLI**, **All keys go to the CLI** (but the command palette's shortcut), **A CLI putting text on the clipboard** (Allow, Ask each time or Block; a CLI never reads your clipboard), **Send prompts as typed**, **Clear the CLI's draft before Hive types**, **A card's start in its own words** and **The Assistant starts with Hive's start view** (#1286, #1292).
- **Set Codex's terminal title**, **Don't let Copilot update itself**, **Include every MCP server's instructions**, **Resume the Assistant with its current instructions**, and right-click passed to Claude Code and Codex. On Codex, the Assistant pre-approves only the Hive tools its Control allows, as on Claude Code and Copilot (#1167, #1174).
- **Sessions look the same however Hive was opened.** Each session's terminal now says it is `xterm-256color`, as Hive's terminal is, and no longer passes on Windows Terminal's `WT_SESSION`: Claude Code drew `>` instead of `❯` when Hive was opened from the Start menu. A `TERM` of your own is kept; **Sessions say their terminal is xterm-256color** in Settings → Advanced turns it off.

### Also new

- **See what a Codex script did.** Codex in code mode runs its tools from a script, which a transcript showed as one **Script**. Its line now says what the script did (*3 hive calls, 1 command, 2 edits*); open it to read each call with its own input and result. `hive_agent_activity` lists those calls in the script's place (HI-2240).
- **Scenario benchmarks show the agent under test apart from the others.** In **Performance → Compare**, a scenario's details now show its measures for the session it tests (the Assistant, or the agent under test) first, then the other agents' (a lane's builder and reviewer, agents it or Hive started) and all of them together, which is what its result is judged on, with each agent's hive calls under them. So a lane's agents doing more no longer looks like the Assistant doing twice the work (HI-2186). A benchmark from before has totals only, and Compare says so.
- **Performance tells builders and reviewers apart.** Each launch is recorded with its workflow role (builder, reviewer, either, or none for generalists and the Assistant; a lane's part counts over the agent's own role), shown in the guidance table, and the hive tools' list is now counted in characters too. **Compare** shows the tool list a launch and, for each workflow role, its launches, guidance and tool list a launch, so a change to what reviewers get shows apart from the builders' (HI-2049). A **Hive tool list** table shows what the tools' list costs each session a start, split into the tools' descriptions, their parameters' descriptions and schemas, and Help → Copy Diagnostics includes it (HI-2063).
- **Your own commands, run by hand or as a workspace opens or closes.** Name a command once in **Settings → Commands** (Hive's own, or the workspace's) and run it from the command palette (**Run Command**), or pick commands, in order, for **When this workspace opens, run**, **When this workspace closes, run** and **When Hive quits, run**: a local server or database your agents need, a backup, a sync. A workspace closes when its window closes, the window switches workspace, you choose **Close Workspace**, or Hive quits; its commands start once Hive has finished saving it, and **When Hive quits, run** once every window's own are done. Hive shows them running in a dialog; as a workspace opens, its agents start once they're done or you choose **Don't wait**. If one fails, **Rerun**, **Skip it and continue** or **Stop here**. Placeholders give the workspace's folder, its name, a project's folder and why the command runs; restarting to install an update skips the close and quit commands unless you tick the box. Commands are saved in Hive's settings or the workspace's `.hive`, never brought in by a repository, and only you set them: the Hive Assistant can't change them (HI-1368, HI-1954).
- **A Generalist role for agents.** Under **Role in the workflow**, **Generalist** is an agent that does whatever you ask in its pane and takes no side in the workflow: it is never put in a lane, never asked to review, and doesn't count toward the project's slots. A card it takes still follows the project's workflow. Every agent also gets a new **generalist** skill on how to do such work well (HI-686).
- **See what a command left running.** A process an agent's command started that keeps running after its shell has gone (a background server, a search left behind) now shows in Activity as *Left running*, with how long and its CPU time, and **End It…**. Past ten minutes, one notice says so, opening the agent, and the agent is told once so it can end it; Pause the Team counts it as still running. Settings → Advanced turns off the notice and the agent's line (HI-2111).
- **See an agent's hooks.** **Hooks…** in an agent's ⋯ menu shows the hooks its session has (Hive's own, and yours and the project's, each saying how sure Hive is that it's loaded) and the last 50 hooks Hive received from it, with Hive's reply and whether Hive applied or ignored each one and why. Activity shows an edit Hive's file lock is blocking while it stands (*Edit blocked: app.ts is locked by HB3*). Read-only: Hive never changes a hook (HI-982).
- **Multi-line prompts for Codex.** With **Send prompts as typed** on, Hive types each line break of a prompt into Codex as Alt+Enter, a key Codex takes as a new line, so the prompt arrives as written instead of on one line. That's the default with Codex 0.164.0, where it was tried; other versions get spaces unless you choose a key in **Settings → Advanced → Codex → Typed prompts: line breaks** (HI-1327).
- **See when Codex is rate-limited.** When Codex's model request is refused (most often a rate limit, 429), Codex waits up to several minutes and tries again while its screen says only *Working*. Hive now reads that wait from Codex's own log and the agent's status says it: *Rate-limited (429) · retrying at 15:05 (1 of 5)*. Hover it for what it means, and, for a large conversation, a hint that a new one sends less (HI-1972). Claude Code and GitHub Copilot agents show it too, from Claude Code's transcript and Copilot's error reports, and an agent still retrying after 5 minutes is reported to the Hive Assistant once (Settings → Advanced, **Retry wait: tell the Assistant after**). A Copilot turn that gives up on a rate limit now ends as failed instead of staying *Working* (HI-1973).
- **See how long an agent waited on rate limits.** Hover the model in an agent's footer: it says how long this session has waited for its coding agent to retry refused requests, so a slow round can be told from a throttled one. The Hive Assistant reads the same from `hive_agent_activity`, with the model and effort the agent actually runs with (HI-1996).
- **The Hive Assistant starts pool lanes too.** In a project whose reviewers are a shared pool, the Assistant can now start a lane reviewed by the pool, as **Start Lane** in the window can; it no longer asks you to start such lanes yourself (HI-1581).
- **See why Hive felt slow.** The Performance page lists the main process's recent stalls under **Main process stalls**: when, how long, whether it was working or waiting, the database or file step that held it and what else was under way, the last 50 since Hive started (HI-1135).
- **Shorter card titles from agents.** Agents and the Assistant write a card's title in about 70 characters and the detail in its description, so `git log` and the board aren't cut off; a longer one is kept, and Hive suggests to the agent a shorter one (Settings → Advanced → Suggest a shorter title past). A title naming a file, a path, a card or a measurement gets the same kind of hint, to put those in the description (HI-2061). Your own titles are never questioned.
- **Archived cards' attachments clean up after themselves.** A card's screenshots and logs go to the Recycle Bin 30 days after the card is archived; choose 3, 7 or 90 days, or Never, per project in Project Settings → Storage, where **Clean Up Now…** shows what is due and removes it at once. Open and Done cards keep theirs, and the card says when its files were removed (HI-1842).
- **Cards keep attachments.** Paste or drop a screenshot, log or any file into a card's description or a comment: it's kept with the card when you save or post, shown in it, and listed for the agent that starts the card. Copy a file to a card from an agent's pane (the pin on its attachment chip) or the Attachments tab, which now has a Cards section. Agents and the Hive Assistant attach files with `hive_create_task` and `hive_update_task`. Clean Up and Purge never remove them; deleting the card sends them to the Recycle Bin.
- **Agents carry on after an update.** Installing a new version (Restart to Update, or an installer you run, which now asks Hive to close first) tells the agents that were working, watching their cards or in a card loop, and when Hive starts again it resumes them in their conversations, one at a time, each told where it was: its card, round and lane. Their lanes don't show them as stalled meanwhile. After an ordinary quit, the next start offers to resume them instead. **Settings → Advanced → Resume agents after an update** turns the automatic resume off (HI-1707).
- **Agents can carry on after a connection drops.** Turn on **Settings → Advanced → Resume agents after a connection drops** (off by default): an agent whose turn a router restart or a Wi-Fi blip ended shows **Waiting for the connection…**, Hive checks its CLI's API host now and then, and once it answers the agent is told to rerun what was cut off and carry on. Never for a usage limit, an expired sign-in or an overloaded server. After 2 hours, or three resumes in an hour that end the same way, Hive stops and says what to try (HI-1612).
- **History shows where your work goes.** Version Control → History opens on the project's target branch (1.0.0, say, rather than main), has a branch picker for any local or remote branch, and pages back through older commits (#1643).
- **Clean up merged branches.** Version Control → Branches & remotes groups the branches as In use, Merged and Not merged against the branch your work merges into, and **Clean Up Merged Branches…** deletes the merged ones nothing uses, with the merged branches on your remotes offered apart, never ticked for you. An unmerged branch's 🗑 lists the commits it would drop first (#1642).
- **Agent API:** contract version 9 (0.9.3's was 5), with a route for each step of a card (`POST /v1/tasks/{card}/submit`, …); each card ends with its `stage`; `GET /v1/tasks/{n}/facts` lists what happened to its work; `GET /v1/tasks?q=` searches the board through a full-text index; `GET /v1/projects/{name}/slots/releasable` lists slots that can be released.
- **See a returned card's conflicts.** When the merge queue sends a card back for a conflict, its Work section lists each conflicting file with its conflicts, unfolded read-only as the queue's test merge had them, the target's side and the card's under their names. Its builder's wake says where they are (HI-1181).
- **Update a branch from its target.** In Version Control → Branches & remotes, a branch behind the target has **Update**: merge the target in or rebase onto it, where the branch is checked out (or in a temporary folder). It says which card and agents it touches, waits while an agent there works, and undoes a conflict at once, naming the files (HI-1180).
- **Keys everywhere.** The board, card dialog, Progress panel, notifications and agents' terminals name cards by key, and so does everything Hive tells agents and the Assistant: by key alone, never with the old workspace number, so a card someone quotes is the card you find. Search for `HI-12`, `HI-`, or just `12` (every project's twelfth card); `#1061` still finds a card by its old number. Project Settings changes a project's key, the old one still finding its cards (#1219, #1221, HI-1661).
- **The Progress panel has tabs:** Pulse, Merges, Runs and Lanes. Runs keeps every run by day, with search and filters; Recent is gone.
- **Runs grouped by card:** each day's runs sit under their card's heading (its key, title, how many ran and how many failed), open while one failed and closed once all passed. A run is one line (who, what, how long), with its commit and copy in the hover; the merge queue's checks for a card are one line, and runs for no card go under **Other** (HI-1701).
- **Start and Resume show that they're working** (**Resuming…**, the agent's other start buttons greyed) until the agent runs.
- **Reorder agents from the focus view:** drag in the strip, or **Alt+Up** / **Alt+Down** (#1053).
- **Builds of Hive go into the journal** when you build Hive from its source code (Settings → Advanced → **Record builds in the journal**) (#1534).
- **Learn more links survive renamed headings:** every user guide heading has a fixed id (#1535).
- **Push to any remote** from the Git view, rename or remove remotes, and choose what a branch tracks; never forced (#1385).
- **Repair a Moved Workspace** shows its plan and report as tables, renames agents' branches named after the old place, and moves Hive's Defender exclusions to the new folders; Settings → Workspace → Antivirus scanning lists Hive's exclusions of folders that are gone, to remove (#1302, #1305, #1313, #1389).
- **Tables instead of long sentences** in Remove Project's *What it touches*, Load Template's plan and Unused worktrees (#1314, #1315, #1316).
- **Copy buttons** beside every command Provider Setup shows, whose install notes are now in your language (#1204).
- **1.0's text reviewed in every language:** about 2,000 translations corrected against the 1.0 glossary (#1126).
- **Every window text in all seven languages again before 1.0:** the messages added since, which some screens showed in English, translated, and about 1,000 more terms and meanings corrected; the Activity tree and a lane's cards name the board's columns as the board does (HI-1928).
- **Tidier settings for 1.0.** The files copied into new slots and review copies are now a Git workflow default (Settings → Workflow defaults, and the workspace's), inherited by each project, which can still set its own in Version Control → Slots and copies, where it says where the value comes from. The merge dialogs start from the project's merge style, so the global **Default merge style** is gone. Settings' **Agents & Worktrees** is now **Agents**, and Project Settings splits it into **General** (the project key) and **Agents** (HI-1765).
- **Card loops and file locks are workflow options.** Wake reviewers and builders, a loop's rounds and wait, the design check wait and File locks are set in Version Control → Workflow, for projects with or without Git, and inherited from the workspace's and Hive's workflow defaults, each saying where its value comes from. They aren't rules: no preset changes them, and a change applies once saved. Your values from Settings → Workspace, Settings → Agents and Project Settings → Card loops move over; those sections, and Project Settings → Workflow, are gone (HI-1766).
- **Workflow defaults for Hive and for a workspace, in Settings → Version Control.** Named like a project's Version Control tab: **Hive defaults**, for every workspace, and **Workspace defaults**, for the open workspace's projects, each with its own None · Git choice and Save…, set the options projects start from; a project's own still win. An option inherited from Hive's level says **From Hive defaults** (#1422, HI-1768).
- **Version Control as tabs.** Settings → Version Control shows **Hive defaults** and **Workspace defaults** as two tabs, each only its own form; switching keeps each draft, a tab with unsaved changes has a dot, and Settings opens on the tab you last chose. **From Hive defaults** and **From the workspace** in a project's options link to their tab. A project's Version Control sections (Workflow, Changes, History…) are tabs of the same kind, and the arrow keys move between them (HI-1837).
- **Keyboard Shortcuts by category.** Settings → Keyboard Shortcuts (and Project Settings') has a tab per command category, with **All** first, each with its count; it opens on the tab you last chose. A search shows **All**, with each tab's matches counted, and a shortcut used by a command in another category still warns, naming its category (HI-1838).
- **Advanced settings by Hive and each CLI.** Settings → Advanced has a tab for Hive and one per CLI (Claude Code, Codex, GitHub Copilot); a CLI that's turned off keeps its tab, dimmed. While a search or Modified only filters the list, each tab shows how many values match, and the list opens on a tab that has some (HI-1839).
- **Attachments: Pane and Card attachments.** A project's Attachments tab has two tabs, each with its own count and filters. **Show in Attachments** in the card dialog opens a card's files there, and **Show Session's Attachments** in an agent's ⋯ menu its running session's (HI-1841).
- **Project Settings… in More ▾.** The project tabs' More ▾ menu ends with **Project Settings…**, with its shortcut (HI-1840).
- **Templates keep your workflow.** Save as Template can include the project's workflow options, the rules only (review, checks by name, merging, stages), never its target branch, commands or identity's name, one section each for None and Git; each card's focused tests too, its command the loading project's own (HI-1949). Loading shows what it would change, then opens Version Control → Workflow with it filled in, to save there; a Git-only workflow in a project without version control offers to set up Git first. Hive's own templates carry their preset's (HI-1910).
- **Initialise repository… is under Git.** In a project with no repository, Git can be chosen in the Version Control tab: its page offers Initialise repository…, saving nothing until you use it, and the project switches to Git once it's done. None's page no longer has the button (HI-1905).
- **A stopped agent looks stopped.** Its pane is dimmed, with a bar saying when it stopped and why (stopped by you, its lane ended, its CLI exited…), and Resume and New Session; its header and tile say *Stopped · 20:46* (HI-1881).
- **The session toolbar's summary chip counts stopped agents as stopped,** as the project header does, not as idle; with every agent stopped, its mark is grey (HI-1908).
- **Branch rows keep their buttons.** In Version Control → Branches & remotes, a busy branch row no longer pushes its buttons off the edge: its time and buttons stay whole and lined up on every row, and what it says about the branch is cut with … instead, the whole of it on hover (HI-1897).
- **Even spacing between sections** in the side panels, and plain buttons with an outline (#1304, #1306).
- **A connection says when it needs setting up.** In Settings → Accounts & Connections, a connection whose first-run setup isn't done yet (Claude Code's questions, choosing Codex's Windows sandbox), or that's on in a workspace whose project folders its CLI doesn't trust yet, says **Needs set up**, with a highlighted **Set Up** that opens where it's needed, and **Set up** once it's done. The Sign In and Set Up terminal always has **Close**: while the CLI still runs, Hive asks before ending it. Removing a connection whose terminal is open offers to close it, rather than refusing (HI-1870).
- **Claude Code no longer asks before each memory it saves on a connection.** A connection's conversations folder is a link to Claude Code's own, so Claude Code saw its memory files as outside its memory folder. Hive now gives each Claude Code agent and the Assistant on a connection the same memory folder by its real path, allowing nothing more and writing nothing to Claude Code's files (Settings → Advanced → Claude Code, **Auto memory by its real path on connections**, on by default) (HI-1899).
- **Unarchiving many cards at once reads the board once.** **Unarchive Selected** brought the cards back one by one and read and drew the board after each; it now reads it once after them all (HI-1864).
- **The Assistant changing many cards at once is lighter on the window.** Its actions are shown together, a few times a second, and its list of changes is read once per burst, instead of a window update and a read for each card (HI-1863).
- **Starting many agents at once is lighter on the window.** Each agent's new terminal, its run's end, its usage and its start finishing are now shown with the agents' states, a few times a second, instead of one window update each: restarting a dozen agents at once redraws the window about a sixth as often (HI-1862).
- **A changed search in Keyboard Shortcuts shows All again.** A tab clicked while searching held on when you changed the search, showing no rows while another tab had the matches; it now holds for that search only, as in Settings → Advanced (HI-1885).
- **The shortcuts editor's filters stay in view in a narrow window.** Settings → Keyboard Shortcuts (and Project Settings') shows its **Set** filter above the table, so a narrow window no longer shows an empty band under the headers with the filter scrolled out of sight. The **Category** filter is gone: the tabs above choose the category (HI-1878).
- **MCP servers are added where they run, with no switches.** A workspace's MCP servers are available to every project and the Hive Assistant, and run only where you add them. A project's **MCP** tab lists only the servers added to it, each with **Remove**, and **Add Server…** picks one of the others, showing what it runs first. The MCP view keeps the servers' definitions, says where each is added, and its **Add to…** ticks the projects (and the Assistant) at once; deleting a server asks first, saying where it is added. The MCP view and the picker search by name, description and tags, and show only those added somewhere or nowhere. The workspace's on/off switch and the projects' switches are gone. Upgrading changes nothing that runs: a server that ran in a project is added there, and a choice kept for a server the workspace had off is dropped, which a notice says once. In the Agent API, `GET /v1/mcp` says where each server is `added` (a project agent hears only about its own project) in place of `globallyEnabled`, and a project's status has `mcp.added` (`on` is kept for now) (HI-2059).
- **Install MCP servers from catalogues.** The MCP view's **Catalogue** tab lists servers to install from catalogues, Hive's own (Suremise/hive-mcp) first, each catalogue its own category, with search and filters (not installed, installed, updates available, runs on this PC). A server's view checks its requirements on this PC (system, Windows edition and build, Windows Sandbox, Node.js, Docker). **Install** downloads it, checks its size and checksum against the catalogue, and shows what it runs before it is added to the workspace; it runs nowhere until you add it with **Add to…**. **Update** is offered when a catalogue has a newer version, never automatic: it says what changes and keeps where the server is added and your own values. Catalogues are read only when the Catalogue opens or you refresh it. Add a company's or a community's catalogue in **Settings → MCP Catalogues** (a note says once that Hive doesn't check its servers) (HI-545).
- **MCP servers for the Hive Assistant.** The Assistant gets a workspace MCP server the way a project does: only once it is added for it, so a server with your own notes can reach the Assistant and no project's agents. Add them in **MCP Servers** in the Assistant's view on the activity bar, or tick **Hive Assistant** in the MCP view's **Add to…**; Settings → Assistant links there. This replaces Settings → Advanced's **The Assistant uses the workspace's other MCP servers**: if you had it on, each workspace gives the Assistant the servers that were on in it (HI-1472, HI-2059).
- **Trusting a workspace's folders takes fewer answers.** A new connection's **Set Up** now asks Claude Code to trust the workspace folder once, which covers every project, slot and copy in it; Codex no longer asks at all: Hive trusts each Codex agent's own folder for that launch, writing nothing to Codex's files (Settings → Advanced → Codex, **Trust Hive's folders on each launch**, on by default). **Needs set up** counts only the projects a connection would actually be used in: those with an agent on it, or on its CLI with it first in your order (HI-1871).

### Fixes

- **A signer that wants your passphrase pauses the merge queue as "Signing waits for you" with newer Git too.** Newer Git (Git for Windows 2.56, for one) prints the signer's message only after the signer has stopped, and the queue read that as "Signing failed" with the wrong advice. Now it reads it as the signer's, and a hook's words still never count (HI-2399).
- **An agent's footer no longer marks its connection's model *(default)*.** A model chosen on the agent's connection showed as, say, *GPT-6 Astra (default)* once the agent was running, as if its CLI had picked it. It now shows plainly before and after the agent starts; *(default)* means only that the CLI runs its own default (HI-2398).
- **A running agent's footer names its connection, not its account.** With two connections on one account, both agents' footers named the account (*Codex-1*), whichever connection supplied their model. The footer and its hover now name the connection each agent started on (*Codex-1 Astra*), and its rows mark it ⟳ when another on the same account has been chosen since (HI-2397).
- **The Agent API says which connection a running agent launched on.** An agent row's `launchedConnection` named only the account, so two agents on two connections of one account looked the same to the Assistant and to agents. It now also gives `connection`, the connection the run launched with (HI-2406).
- **Each git Hive runs is one process with Git for Windows 2.56 too.** Its UCRT64 build keeps the real git in another folder, so Hive went back to starting every git through Git for Windows' launcher; it now finds it there as well (HI-2400).
- **File locks hold back a Codex edit made as a shell command.** Codex sometimes applies its patch by running `apply_patch` in a shell rather than as its own edit, and that edit went through even when another agent held the file. Hive now reads the files from the patch and locks, asks or warns as it does for any other edit (HI-2408).
- **Allow on a file lock's notification stays until you answer.** With File locks on **Ask me**, a Codex agent stopped by another agent's file ends its reply within seconds to wait for you, and in the release candidates that took the notification's **Allow** away, or left it doing nothing. Allow now stays until you answer, and Hive then tells the agent to go ahead. If the other agent finishes with the file first, the notification no longer asks, and Hive tells the agent to try again (HI-2386).
- **A branch merged into the project folder's branch no longer lands on the folder's card.** With cards worked in the project folder itself (no slots), merging a branch into the folder's branch noted every commit it brought in on a card there: those naming a card on that card (often one long done), the rest on the folder's only unfinished card. Only the branch's own commits count now, the merge commit among them; the merged branch's commits stay with their own cards (HI-2382).
- **A big merge or checkout no longer freezes Hive's window.** With a card worked in the project folder itself (no slots), a merge bringing thousands of commits into the folder's branch held Hive for up to a minute while it noted each commit on the card (3,536 commits: 40 s). It now notes them in batches, and Hive stays responsive meanwhile (HI-2379).
- **Quit when agents finish no longer loses edits made after reopening Hive.** Opened again during the wait, a file left with unsaved changes is asked about (save or discard) before Hive quits, when the last agent finishes or on Quit Now; Cancel keeps Hive running (HI-2336).
- **A file path's tooltip in a terminal is in your language** (HI-2346). Hovering a file path an agent printed showed *Open in Files · Ctrl+click* in English whatever Hive's language; it is now translated, and follows a change of language without reopening the session.
- **Clearing a journal number no longer deletes journal entries** (HI-2345). In Settings → Assistant → Journal, emptying **Keep everyday entries for** or **Size cap** and clicking away saved the smallest value allowed (7 days, 5 MB), and Hive at once deleted the everyday entries past it; a number out of range did the same. An empty box now keeps what it had, and a number out of range says so under the box and changes nothing.
- **Starting or submitting a card without version control no longer pauses typing** (HI-2340). Hive read and hashed every file of the project on the thread your keystrokes go through: on a few thousand files, every pane froze for up to about four seconds each time a card started or was submitted. That read now runs in the background (at most **Settings → Advanced → No version control: longest read of the project**), as do a check's reads of the project when it starts and ends (HI-2358: up to about three seconds on main's thread for a card covering the whole project), and a no-code card doesn't read the project at all.
- **A workspace's own Resume agents after an update shows that it follows Settings, and can again** (HI-2344). In Settings → Advanced, the **This workspace** switch looked off while the workspace followed Settings' value (on), and once clicked it kept an on or off of its own, with no way back to following Settings. It's now a list: **on (Settings')**, which follows Settings' value and shows it, then **on** and **off**.
- **Without version control, a card's later files are no longer left out of its work without a word** (HI-2339). Once a card's paths are set, a submit that names no paths says which files they leave out: its agents' new edits, and other changes in the project since it started. The builder then includes them or leaves them. Nothing is added by itself, and a declared folder covers every file in it.
- **Without version control, submitting a card yourself shows what its paths leave out** (HI-2352). Dragging a card from Doing to Review asks first when files changed that its paths don't list, naming them: **Include Them** adds them to its paths and submits it with them, **Leave Them Out** submits it as it is.
- **The Files tab no longer corrupts text that isn't UTF-8 when you save it** (HI-2331). A file in another encoding, such as a Windows-1252 `.txt` or `.csv` with *café* in it, opened in the editor with its accented characters shown as �, and saving any edit wrote those � over the original bytes for good. Such a file now isn't opened for editing: the Files tab says it isn't UTF-8 text and offers **Open in Default App**, as it does for binary files. UTF-8 files, with or without a BOM, open and save as before.
- **Privacy: a console recording attached to Copy Diagnostics no longer shows file paths** (HI-2332). An attached recording only replaced your workspaces' and home folders, so a file an agent read on another drive or a network share (`E:\Clients\…`, `\\server\share\…`) kept its full path and name in the report. Every path in the attached recording (the files tools read, the agent's folder, its transcript) now shows as `<path>`; the developer console itself still shows them.
- **Turning off automatic update checks soon after Hive starts stops its first check too** (HI-2324). Hive checks for updates 30 seconds after it starts: with **Settings → Updates → Check for updates automatically** turned off within those 30 seconds, that check still ran, and with automatic downloads on could download a release. It no longer does; **Check Now** still checks.
- **Security: an agent can no longer hand over, start or stop another project's agents** (HI-2327). An agent calling the Agent API with its own token could already not read or type into another project's agents, but it could still ask one of them for a handover (`POST /v1/projects/{name}/handover`), which prompts that agent, and start or stop them. These now answer `403` for another project's agents, as typing into them does; its own project's, scripts and the Hive Assistant are unchanged.
- **The Assistant's agent change no longer goes through once you've turned its Control down** (HI-2328). An agent added, renamed or changed, started or stopped by the Hive Assistant could still be carried out after you lowered **Settings → Assistant → Control**, or after the Assistant's session ended, if it was waiting for another change to the project's settings to finish. It is now checked again at the moment it is made, and refused with nothing changed.
- **Security: an agent can no longer turn another project on or off** (HI-2330). An agent calling the Agent API with its own token could activate or deactivate any project in its workspace, hiding it from your sidebar. Another project's `POST /v1/projects/{name}/activate` and `/deactivate` now answer `403`; turning its own project on or off, scripts and the Hive Assistant are unchanged.
- **The Task Board's introduction fits every workflow** (HI-2341). Under its title it said agents put finished work in Review and reviewers move it to Approved, even in a Solo project, whose board has neither column. It now reads *Plan work as cards, start them on agents, and see where each one is on its way to Done.*
- **The window title is shown in full when there's room** (HI-2319). A title such as *website — HIVE — Hive Desktop* could end in "Hive Deskt…" in a wide window: a fraction of a pixel lost to rounding cut its last letters, for some project names and not others. It's now cut with "…" only when the window really is too narrow for it.
- **A pause soon after the PC wakes is counted as busy, not as the sleep's** (HI-2317). When the main process was busy within about a second after the PC woke, hive.log said it was *paused … while the PC slept: not counted as busy*, and the Performance view left it out. A resume that came before a pause began now means that sleep was over, for the busy warning and for slow file calls alike. And a pause of up to about three quarters of a second that began just after one of Hive's half-second checks went unnoticed: it is now noted too.
- **A release candidate or beta with Include pre-releases on is offered the release** (HI-2311). With **Settings → Updates → Include pre-releases** on, an installed RC was only ever offered a later RC: never the release (1.0.0 over 1.0.0-rc.2), and with no later RC the check said no release had been published. A beta was never offered an RC. A pre-release install is now offered the newest version newer than it, whether it's a beta, an RC or a release. A release install is offered what it was before.
- **No "finished" notice about a turn that's already over as the agent works again** (HI-2312). When Hive gave an agent more work the moment it finished (a delivery typed at its turn's end, a wake), the *finished* notice, held a few seconds to group finishes, arrived as the agent's next turn started, with the last turn's message, so it looked as if notices came only when an agent started again, and it could stand where the agent's *needs your input* should be noticed. A finish whose agent is working or waiting again by the time it would be told is now left out: you hear from it when that turn ends, or at once if it needs you. Each notice Hive tells, or doesn't, is now in the log with where it went and why, for Help → Copy Diagnostics.
- **Windows Sandbox no longer holds an agent's slot after it closes, and a held slot is waited on, not retried** (HI-2304). Windows Sandbox's own broker, `WindowsSandboxServer.exe`, stays running after a Sandbox closes. Hive took it for something the agent had left running, so the agent's slot stayed held and its connection switch never resumed it. Hive now lets it go once no Sandbox runs. It recognises the broker only by where Windows installs it, never ends it, and keeps the slot held while a Sandbox still runs. When a slot is held by something else, Hive no longer retries the agent every 20 seconds. It waits until the slot is free, and says what holds it on the card, on the board (*MB1 waits to start: …*) and in the agent's pane (**Waiting for its slot**). A connection switch waits for the slot the same way, with a notice, and resumes the agent once the slot is free. The broker doesn't hold the slot after Hive quits or restarts either, and isn't listed as something the agent left running. After Hive closed unexpectedly, Hive asks the process helper that stayed behind what the session still runs, and frees the slot by itself when that is only the broker (HI-2313). When the helper can't say, the slot stays held, and a notice names the broker and says releasing it is safe, with **Release Slot…** (HI-2309).
- **A builder's reply to its reviewer's question reaches the reviewer** (HI-2302). When a reviewer asked something on a card in Review and its builder answered with a comment, nothing woke the reviewer, and a reviewer that started waiting on the card after the answer landed never heard it, so the review sat idle. Hive now wakes the reviewer with the builder's reply (the card's history keeps *Replied to the reviewer: "…"*), and an agent's wait on a card counts what others did after its own last change to the card, so an answer that came in before the wait still wakes it. Off with **Wake reviewers and builders**, as the other loop wakes are.
- **A card is no longer shown as stalled because another lane's builder waits on it** (HI-2381). When a builder whose next card depends on another lane's card watched that card and its wait ran out, the card showed as stalled on that builder while its own builder and reviewer were working on it, and the builder was told to ask you whether to keep waiting. A card's stall now counts only its own builder and reviewer, and a builder waiting on another agent's card in progress is told to keep waiting instead of asking you.
- **A watch that times out on a card in Review says so, without sending agents to bother you** (HI-2295). When an agent's card watch reached its limit just after the card moved to Review, its wake told it to tell you and ask what to do, so a builder notified you that a review was late seconds before a reviewer took the card. The wake now says the card is in Review, who reviews it or that it waits for a reviewer (from the pool, too), and to watch it again for the verdict; only the watch's other cards still ask the agent to tell you.
- **A lane's brief names its own cards with two workspaces open** (HI-2288). A lane started in one workspace (by the Assistant, a script, after waiting for a slot, or in a new conversation it was owed) could brief its builder with another workspace's card of the same number, key and title. Each lane now reads its cards in its own workspace, and its brief names only its own project's cards.
- **A card in the project folder submits its commits even while another card starts there** (HI-2287). When several cards are built in the project folder, a submit could be refused with *nothing committed to review* although the card's commit was there, if another card in that folder started or finished at the same moment (a lane starting its next card, say). Hive now reads the folder's cards again and finds the card's own commits.
- **A decision the builder hasn't read holds its submit without review too** (HI-2275). Under rules with no review where Hive or the agent merges, a builder could submit a card while a decision recorded on it was unread, and the work went on to be merged without it. Hive now refuses that submit until the builder has read the card, as it already did with review. With every option off, and Solo, a submit stays free.
- **A guessed reviewer no longer drops a lane's reviewer** (HI-2273). A builder registering its running lane again with a reviewer name that is no agent (a guess) left the lane with no reviewer, and its next failed round went unwatched. Hive now refuses that, naming the lane's reviewer; a new lane still starts without one. And `hive_lane next` right after a lane's last card is Done says the lane has ended, rather than inviting the agent to start one.
- **Workflow previews show the identity Hive commits as** (HI-2269). After the identity was corrected in git, loading a template, a preset's preview or the save's list of changes could show **Commits use the project's identity** changing from the old name and email, though Hive no longer uses it. They now treat the option as on or off and show git's identity now.
- **What agents started is still offered after a crash, even if Hive's process tracker stopped first** (HI-2263). If the tracker stopped while agents ran and Hive then closed unexpectedly, the next start couldn't list or stop what those agents had started, though it was still running. Hive now keeps each running agent's processes as last listed until that agent stops, and the next start lists those still running, as before.
- **A changed skill asks running agents to restart** (HI-2268). The banner over a running session says to restart when its skills changed, but only a change to MCP servers or settings made in Hive's window brought it up: editing a workspace skill (in the Skills view, an editor or by an agent), adding or removing one left running agents on the old version with nothing said. Now any change to a workspace skill shows the banner on the agents that started with the older version, and the sidebar marks their project; a restart gives them the new version.
- **An agent started while its pane is hidden draws at its own size** (HI-2250). Started behind Settings or another view, an agent's terminal could stay 80 × 24 while the agent drew at its own size, so its first screens landed in the wrong places until you looked. The terminal now takes the size the agent starts at, even when its pane is made before the agent's process is.
- **A Copilot agent stopped mid-turn keeps its token totals** (HI-2272). Stop pressed Ctrl+C three times at a fixed pace. On a busy machine, cancelling the turn took long enough that the second key arrived as it wound down and was lost, so Copilot never exited by itself, was ended after 10 seconds, and the Sessions tab had no tokens for it. Now Stop cancels the turn and waits for it to end, then presses Ctrl+C until Copilot shows *ctrl+c again to exit*, and once more.
- **Commits use the project's identity follows git's identity when it changes** (HI-2266). The option kept a copy of the name and email it showed when it was turned on: after you corrected the identity in Version Control → Branches & remotes → Commit identity (or in git config), the merge queue still committed as the old one, and agents' submissions with the corrected one were refused. Now Hive reads the identity from git each time, the one the option shows; with none set, a submission is refused saying so.
- **A Claude Code agent working in a slot no longer stops at reading its own skill** (HI-2274). Hive's skills for a session are in the project's `.hive`, outside a slot or worktree, so Claude Code asked before reading a skill's reference file there (such as card-loop's builder steps), and a lane's builder in its slot waited on that question with nobody to answer it. Its launch now gives it that folder (`--add-dir`).
- **Agent tools that name no agent work again in a one-agent project** (HI-2258). `hive_agent_activity`, `hive_start_agent`, `hive_stop_agent`, `hive_prompt_agent`, `hive_message_agent` and `hive_update_agent` without `agent` failed with *No route for GET …/agents//activity*. Now they use the project's only agent, and in a project with several they say to name one.
- **A card refused in a batch says what to do next** (HI-2238). When `hive_update_tasks` (or `POST /v1/tasks/batch`) left a card as it was because of its rules, the reply gave only why, never the next step a single change gives, and named the stage by its id (*active*): the Assistant, refused a card in Doing with nobody on it, then chose an agent itself instead of asking you. Each such card now reads as a single change's refusal: its card, why, the rule, the next step and where the rule is changed.
- **Hive's refusals no longer send agents to you for what their card already covers** (HI-2227). A card submitted with nothing committed told its agent to ask you before committing; now it says to commit the card's work (asking you first only when it was asked just to submit the card, not to do it: HI-2257), or, when the card needs no change, to ask for it to be made no-code. An agent refused a card On Hold asks the Assistant where you've already decided to park it. One refused on a Done card you asked more work on is told to move it back to Doing, not to make a new card. With Look and advise and Change settings on, the Assistant now changes a setting you ask for instead of saying it can't. A new card's title leaves names, numbers, files and paths to its description.
- **Project status says which card each running lane is on** (HI-2271): its pairs carry the lane's state as the lane list words it ("active, on AL-12"), where an Assistant reading project status guessed the card from the Doing column.
- **Putting back or reordering a lane's card says the lane keeps its own order** (HI-2267): the reply names the lane and how to change its order (start it again with the cards in the new order, applied now), where the Assistant reordered the column and the lane took the card straight back.
- **A skill you delete no longer stops an agent's card work** (HI-2265): agents carry on by Hive's board rules without it, where Codex stopped for a missing skill. In Planner mode, the Assistant starts work you ask it to start, instead of saying its mode prevents it.
- **The Assistant no longer holds off from an agent you've finished typing to** (HI-2261). An agent's activity said when you last typed in its terminal even after you pressed Enter, so the Assistant wouldn't give it work; it now says so only while you may still be typing, as Hive itself waits. A decision you record on a card a reviewer is reviewing now tells the reviewer to read it before its next verdict, not to carry on with the work.
- **Two of Hive's replies now say the step to take** (HI-2260). A card title naming a file or a measurement is answered with "retitle it without them", where agents left it as it was; and asked to move a card past its rules with your reason ("I merged it by hand"), the Assistant is told that reason is enough and moves it, instead of asking you again.
- **Hive's own line to an agent is no longer told to the Assistant as your reply** (HI-2218). If you had pressed Enter in an agent's pane in the last 15 seconds (such as *Yes* on Claude Code's trust screen as it started), the task Hive started it with, or a line Hive typed into it a moment later, such as a lane's *New in your inbox* nudge, could reach the Assistant as *you replied to the agent*. The task an agent is started with, and a prompt it takes after Hive's own Enter, are now Hive's; your own replies are still told.
- **An agent's terminal is made once as it starts** (HI-2213). Hive hears a session's start more than once (the workspace's report of its agents, then the session's own status, sometimes twice), and each made the agent's terminal again: the Assistant's terminal, already drawn, could be replaced a moment later on a busy machine, losing its WebGL drawing. A terminal is now made once for each launch, and again only for the next one.
- **"Hive closed unexpectedly" no longer misses a command started while its record was briefly held** (HI-2201). When Windows refused Hive's update of its record of what agents have started for a moment (a virus scan of the file just written), that update was dropped, and a command an agent had just started, such as a test run through `hive-progress`, was missing from the notice after a crash until the next minute's update. Hive now tries the update again for a couple of seconds.
- **Hive started just after it closed unexpectedly keeps its Agent API** (HI-2196). If the port was still in use as Hive started again, the Agent API stayed off for the whole session, and agents resumed after the close had no hive tools. Hive now tries the port again for up to 5 seconds after an unexpected close; any other start fails at once as before.
- **The Assistant at Look and advise is no longer told to watch running lanes.** Its "Lanes are running" reminder at the end of a turn now comes only when **Settings → Assistant → Control** lets it control agents; at Look and advise the lanes are yours to run, and the reminder cost it an extra step for a job that isn't its own (HI-2187).
- **The activity bar keeps its width in a narrow window** (HI-2180). With the Projects list expanded in a small window (at 125% zoom, say), the activity bar on the left was squeezed to under half its width, its buttons cut off under the list. It now always keeps its width, and the work area gives way instead.
- **The Assistant panel's welcome stays in its place in a short window** (HI-2198). In a small window (at 125% zoom, say), the panel's welcome before the Assistant starts was taller than its room and spilled up over the Workspace section, its picture drawn over the inactive projects row, and down over the footer. It now scrolls within its own area.
- **Hive reopens your workspace when started again just after it closed unexpectedly** (HI-2179). Started straight after a crash, Hive could fail to open the workspace's board with *disk I/O error* and show the start page instead, with no merge left half done in its notice and with nothing to resume into. Hive now tries the open again a few times, for under two seconds in all, before it gives up, and the workspace's open first waits, up to 20 seconds, while the board's file or the Assistant's journal is still held by something of the run that ended (HI-2199, HI-2200: seen for over ten seconds after a kill). The usage history waits the same way, without holding up the window.
- **The Lanes tab's buttons stay inside the Progress panel** (HI-2164). In a long language (French, say) Legend, Start Lane… and Pause the Team didn't fit a narrow panel and spilled out to its left, over Settings' *Modified only* box. They now wrap onto further lines within the panel, and in a narrow window Settings' search box gives way so *Modified only* stays whole.
- **Progress → Merges leaves out the projects you aren't working on** (HI-2165). Under All projects, every project whose options have Hive merge showed its merge queue, idle ones too (*Nothing queued*). Now a project that isn't in Working on shows its queue only while it's doing something (a card testing, waiting or returned, a pause, a merge in the last hour); choose it in the project filter to see it anyway.
- **No For you flash as a card merges and moves to Done** (HI-2170). As a lane's last card was merged, Hive could briefly ask you about uncommitted changes in its slot that weren't there: it read the slot while switching it off the card's branch. Now you're asked only about changes really left in the slot, once its builder has stopped and Hive has looked at it.
- **A board column's name never breaks inside a word** (HI-2160). With every stage on the board the columns are narrow, and a long name ("Zatwierdzone" in Polish, "Fusionando" in Spanish) broke across two lines in the middle of the word; a word too long for its column now ends in "…", and pointing at it shows the name in full.
- **A builder's next card is never held off by a wait from its previous conversation** (HI-2197). When a lane ended while its builder was waiting on its card, that wait outlived the conversation: in the next lane's new conversation the builder showed as waiting on cards, so Hive couldn't give it its new card (50 minutes, until the Assistant stepped in). Now a new conversation ends the agent's earlier wait first; a resumed one keeps it. An agent can't start a wait while Hive is handing it a card in a slot (it's told to end its turn), and a card number that reads as another card's key (2189 for HI-2189, when 2189 is HI-2182's number) is refused, naming both.
- **No agent sits waiting on a card that's already Done** (HI-2153). A reviewer that started watching a card after it was merged waited for a move that never came, for hours, and Archive All in Done skipped the card as one an agent was on. Now a card reaching Done ends every agent's wait on it, a wait started on a card already in Done answers at once, and Archive All takes a Done card someone is still watching.
- **Opening the workspace a window already shows leaves it open.** Opening it again (from Recent, say, or by another name) closed it and opened it again, and when Hive was still opening it at start, what Hive was doing in it then (placing a card in a slot, say) failed part way through. Now nothing changes: the window keeps its workspace as it is, once it has finished opening (HI-2141).
- **Hive starts git far less often while lanes run.** With three lanes building, Hive started git about nine times a second, and each start costs CPU, a console host and Windows Defender's attention. A finished card's slot is asked about again only once something in the repository has moved, answers about commits that can't change are kept for the session, and the Git status item asks git again only when the branch, its upstream or the git config has changed. Git still gives every answer, and what's shown is as current as before (HI-2115).
- **A tooltip closes when what it describes moves away.** A tooltip stayed up after the row under the mouse moved (the Progress panel's lanes reordering as one went active), even once the mouse moved on, and the next tooltip opened beside it. It now closes as soon as the mouse is over anything else, and only one tooltip shows at a time (HI-2052).
- **A deleted project stays deleted.** Deleting a project (or moving its folder out) just after Hive had started looking over the workspace's folders could bring its folder back, holding only an empty `.hive`, so it showed in the project list again. Hive no longer makes a project's folder when it finds the project gone (HI-2045).
- **A preset you choose right after loading a template stays chosen.** Loading a template opens Version Control → Workflow and fills in the template's options once Hive has read the project's. When that read was slow and you chose a preset (Team, say) before it came back, the template's options replaced your choice under the same preset name, without the target branch or the checks' commands, and Save… saved them that way: the checks then never counted. A choice made meanwhile now stays, as it does when the options change elsewhere (HI-2033).
- **A long command takes two lines in the commands dialog.** A command running or failed showed its whole command line, which could fill six lines; it now shows the first two, ending in an ellipsis, and pointing at it shows the whole line.
- **The Assistant stops an agent that is only watching cards without asking you,** as it does an idle one; it still asks for an agent that's working, waiting for you or running background jobs. A stop question you don't answer within about 4½ minutes is withdrawn from the Assistant's panel and the agent keeps running, so none is left waiting there after the Assistant has given up (HI-1994).
- **A review waiting on the builder's long job isn't stalled.** When a reviewer waited for a proof it had asked the card's builder for (a long stress run in the background), the card showed *started reviewing … but has given no verdict* as soon as 15 minutes had passed, and the builder's run said **Stopped reporting** because the command printed no steps. Now the builder's run for the card, or its background tasks, count as progress until they end (up to the background task limit in **Settings → Agents**), and the 15 minutes count from their end. A command run with `hive-progress` that keeps printing output counts as reporting (HI-1993).
- **A moved workspace or project keeps how you left its views.** After a workspace folder (or a project moved into another workspace) was moved or renamed, each project's Skills tab choices, Sessions tree, dismissed notices and its fold in the Hive Assistant's list, and the workspace's panels (the Hive Assistant and Progress open or not, their folds and sizes), went back to their defaults. Now they follow the workspace as it opens from its new folder.
- **A moved workspace keeps its window's pin and its close command's runs.** After a workspace folder was moved or renamed, Repair left Always on Top off for its window, and the output Hive keeps of the workspace's close command (its last 5 runs) under the old folder, never pruned. Now both follow the workspace as soon as it opens from its new folder, and a run still going then follows once it has ended. A copied workspace's original keeps its own.
- **A wake Hive stops part-way no longer stays in a Claude Code agent's input.** Claude Code's Ctrl+U clears only the row the cursor is on of a line wrapped onto several, so in a narrow pane a wake Hive stopped typing (no longer wanted, or the agent got busy) kept its first rows, and the next wake was sent glued to them. Hive now takes such a line back with a Backspace for each character, wide ones too; Codex and Copilot clear the line with one Ctrl+U, as before (HI-1974).
- **A draft left in a Claude Code agent's input is cleared whole before Hive types.** A draft wrapping onto several rows lost only its last row, and Hive's message was sent after the rest. Hive now clears it a row at a time and types only once it sees the input box empty; if it can't see that, it types nothing and tries again later (Settings → Advanced, **Clearing the input: wait for each key**). **Compact** clears it the same way (HI-1979).
- **No more "isn't running" stall for an agent that is still starting.** A card started in a slot was reported stalled (*HB5 isn't running*) a moment after Start, while Hive was still getting the slot ready. An agent being started is now starting, not stalled, on the board as in agents' notices, also when the Assistant or another agent started it; a start that fails stalls the card with why, on the card too (*HB5's start failed: …*) (HI-1976, HI-1977).
- **GitHub Copilot 1.0.104 and later: a question shows what it asks.** From 1.0.104, Copilot's notice of a question says only *Information requested*, which Hive showed as the agent's waiting status. Hive now shows the question itself, as with earlier versions (HI-1945).
- **Settings is reachable in a short window.** With the new Activity button, the activity bar's buttons no longer fitted a window about 650 pixels high, and Settings, Docs and Notifications slid under the status bar where they couldn't be clicked. The buttons now shorten to fit, and in the smallest window (or at a large zoom) the bar scrolls with the mouse wheel (HI-1957).
- **A Copilot agent whose model can't be reached no longer shows working for ever.** When GitHub Copilot gives up on its model after its retries (the network down, a local model server stopped), it ends the turn without saying so in its hooks. Hive now reads that end from Copilot's session file: the agent shows the turn ended on an error, with Copilot's message, and **Resume agents after a connection drops** carries it on like the other CLIs' agents (HI-1960).
- **A click on a card's file opens it.** In a project's Attachments tab, a card attachment opened nothing on a click. It now opens in the same viewer as a pane's file, with ← / → through the card files shown and **Remove from Card**. In the viewer, an image or a PDF has **Open in Its App** (Windows' own viewer, for zoom and printing) and any other kind **Show in Folder**, since Hive never opens an attached file that could run (HI-1958).
- **A card from before 1.0 starts in a slot.** Starting such a card (the Assistant's `hive_start_task`, the board's Start, its agent's move to Doing) gave it no slot and launched its builder in the project folder, on the main branch, in a project that builds cards in slots. Its start now gets a slot like any other card's, and a start that still gets none there is undone and says so, never starting its agent in the project folder (HI-1966).
- **Claude Code's offer to teach auto mode about your environment waits for you.** Claude Code 2.1.312 sometimes asks this on its screen after a turn in auto mode, without telling Hive, and Hive typed a wake into it: its Enter could answer the question, and the line it took left the agent held behind an empty input. The agent now shows **Needs input** with the question while it's on screen, Hive types nothing into it until you answer it there, and a line it took is typed again once the input is empty. **Settings → Advanced → Claude Code → Offer auto mode's environment setup** turns the offer off in Hive's sessions (HI-1959).
- **An agent whose input takes no keys waits for you instead of being typed at.** Claude Code 2.1.314 draws that offer under its input box, which Hive didn't recognise, and Hive typed cards and wakes into it again and again for up to an hour while nothing showed you. Hive now sees the offer there too. For any other dialog that takes an agent's keys, after three tries with no answer the agent shows **Needs input** (*Something on its screen is taking its keys*) and Hive stops typing there. Answer it in the agent's pane, then press **Try again**. A card its lane would hand the agent waits meanwhile, instead of being handed over and called off every second (HI-2191).
- **Starting a card back from Review starts its agent.** Starting a card in Review with its builder stopped (the Assistant's `hive_start_task`, the board's Start) moved the card to Doing and replied *Started*, but never launched the builder or gave it the card, when the card had been built in a slot. Hive now launches the builder with the card and the note, as for a card in Doing (HI-1953).
- **Loading a template ends the replaced agents' lanes.** A card loop whose builder or reviewer a template's load removed stayed running with nobody on it; it now ends as it does when you remove the agent, and the removed agent's kept terminal output goes with it (HI-1502).
- **The Progress panel's "runs failed" line stays in sight.** On the Pulse tab it sat under the runs going on, so with several runs it was pushed out of view. It now sits above them, under the merge going on (HI-1923).
- **No control characters in prompts Hive types.** A prompt Hive typed into Codex (or a slash command, or Compact's focus) kept control characters such as Ctrl+C, which Codex could take as an interrupt. They're left out now, as they were from pasted prompts (HI-1920).
- **Switching a running agent to a mode that needs a restart waits for it to stop.** A CLI slow to exit (more than 8 seconds) made the switch fail with *This agent is already running or starting*. Hive now waits as long as that CLI may take to end, and in a slot for its earlier session to release it. If the agent still hasn't stopped, it says so and starts nothing (HI-1869).
- **A Codex agent resumed into a full context is heard again.** Codex can compact a conversation by itself and then run its next turn under the same turn id. Hive took that turn's hooks for old news, so the agent stayed ready with "no hooks from Codex" and wasn't given work until it was restarted. Hive now follows that turn (HI-1916).
- **A lane the Hive Assistant starts gets going.** Started for agents that weren't running, it was recorded as active and nothing happened: no slot was lent and no agent started. Its stopped agents now start as **Start Lane** in the window starts them: the builder's slot first, then the builder and reviewer with their brief. If no slot is free it waits for one, as the window's does. An agent already running is left as it is, working or not, and the Assistant is told to tell it about the lane (HI-1900, HI-1934).
- **Checks no longer fail when Hive is slow to answer.** `hive-progress --check` gave up after a fixed 5 seconds, so on a busy Hive checks failed with *Hive couldn't be reached* though Hive was up. It now waits as **Settings → Advanced → hive-progress: Hive's reply time** says (15 seconds by default) and asks once more before giving up. It says whether Hive was slow or couldn't be reached (a sandbox can block it too), sends a check's result again when Hive didn't confirm it, and says when the result may not have been recorded (HI-1892).
- **Builders resume into their slots after Hive restarts.** After a quit for an install, every builder's resume was refused, its slot held by its own ended session from the Hive that had quit, and a slot whose card was done stayed taken until released by hand. Hive now waits briefly as it quits for each stopped session's processes to end, keeps the sessions it saw end, and frees their slots by itself at the next start. A slot it can't settle (Hive closed unexpectedly, say) stays held, marked *Held by B1's ended session* with **Release…** in the Version Control tab's Worktrees list too (HI-1886).
- **Slots free themselves once what an agent left running ends, after Hive has closed.** After an update, slots stayed held, asking you to release them, even when nothing was running there any more: by test runs still going as Hive quit, and by processes the quit had just ended itself. Hive's process helper now stays after Hive while an agent's processes still run, ends nothing, notes in Hive's profile when they have all ended, and exits; the next Hive frees those slots by itself. This works after a crash too. A slot whose processes still run stays held, and **Release Slot…** lists them (HI-2055). In the release candidates the helper still ended with Hive, after a quit as after a crash, because Windows ends everything Hive starts directly once Hive ends: it is now started so that it stays (HI-2314).
- **Closing a workspace no longer leaves its slots held.** Closing a workspace (or a window that isn't the last) freed its agents' slots only after the workspace had closed, so the slots stayed held. After a restart, opening that workspace again asked you to release them, even though nothing was running there. Hive now frees them before the workspace closes, and reopening a workspace later, after a quit or not, finds them free (HI-2299).
- **A busy day no longer stops a slot freeing itself after Hive closes.** Hive remembered which helper watched each agent's processes for only its last 100 agent starts.
