An agent holding a slot through a busy day could drop out of that list, and after Hive closed, its slot stayed held though nothing it started was still running. Hive now keeps that record for as long as the slot needs it, however many agents start meanwhile (HI-2303).
- **The installed Hive runs its work on its own threads again, so typing no longer lags while agents work.** Hive moves its slow work off its main process onto threads of their own: recording what sessions used, git's commands, the repository lock, the board's database upkeep, writing the log, and watching for stalls. In the installed Hive none of those threads started, because Hive looked for each one's code on disk, where it is packed inside Hive's app archive. Their work ran on the main process instead, which also carries every keystroke, and the usage history opened and closed its database for each request. They now start in the installed Hive too, and Help → Copy Diagnostics says how each one runs. With twelve agents at work on a copy of a busy workspace, the installed Hive got through 64% more agent turns in the same time, with 42% less main-process time for each. Its pauses of more than a quarter of a second went from 2.3 a minute to none, and the longest from 0.4 s to 0.14 s (HI-1880).
- **Many agents at once leave Hive's main process more room for typing.** Each agent's comment, turn and edit had Hive read much more than it needed: every card on the board to say where one card is in its column, every session record of the project to change one, and every card's holds again to share out what sessions used. Each now reads only what it needs. With twelve agents at work on a copy of a busy workspace, the installed Hive spends a third less main-process time on each agent turn, and its main process went from 74% busy to 58% while getting through 18% more turns (HI-1887).
- **Hive's main process reads less again while many agents work.** Every file edit an agent made had Hive work out every agent's current card, each card's history read again, to find that one agent's. Each board change had several readers each read every card on the board. And Hive looked up the real location of a file on its main process for every usage request. Hive now keeps what it has read until the board next changes, works out only the agent that asked, rereads only the cards a change touched, and looks those locations up off its main process. With twelve agents at work on a copy of a busy workspace, the installed Hive spends about a sixth less main-process time on each agent turn (HI-1909).
- **Refusals are no longer counted as Hive's errors.** Adding an agent past **Agents per project**, starting a card on an agent that is busy, a second reviewer, a session that is still starting and other refusals by a rule or limit were answered through the Agent API as server errors (500), so Performance counted them as Hive faults. They are now refusals (400, 404 or 409) with the same words (HI-1952).
- **A failed review always sends the card back to its builder.** In a project with no workflow chosen yet, with the workflow rules off, or for a card from before 1.0, a reviewer failing a card through `hive_update_task` (or `PATCH /v1/tasks/{n}`) left it in Review, while the verdict tool sent it back to Active. Now both send it back to Active with its builder, so its fixes count towards the card's cost, and the builder's wake says who failed it (HI-1946).
- **A card's cost by round.** The card dialog's Work section ends with **Cost by round**: what each review round cost, the builder's and reviewer's parts apart, adding up to the card's cost. It shows whether extra rounds or a long read drove the cost. Cards from before Hive 1.0 show their total only (HI-1906).
- **The usage history stays small.** Hive's record of what sessions used (`.hive/usage.db`) kept when each request happened, to the second, for every session for good, and grew about 5 MB a day on a busy workspace. A session that ended more than two weeks ago now keeps that in 1-hour steps, and the space it frees goes back to the disk; totals and costs don't change. **Settings → Advanced → Usage history: keep full detail for** sets the two weeks. On a copy of a busy workspace's history, the file went from 63 MB to 45 MB (HI-1648).
- **The usage history is smaller still.** What lets Hive read a resumed session's transcript from where it stopped (kept for sessions used in the last two weeks) held every request's usage as Claude Code wrote it, up to 3.4 MB for a long session. It now keeps only the counts Hive reads, compressed. On the same copy, those 277 sessions' records went from 28 MB to under 2 MB and the file from 45 MB to 18 MB, and taking a long session up again got faster (HI-1912).
- **A Codex agent starts on a connection others are busy on.** Agents running on a connection write in its folder all the time (their databases and logs, Codex's sandbox), and Hive took that for the login changing, refusing a new agent there with *…'s login kept changing while Hive checked it*. Only a change to the login itself counts now, for Claude Code's connections too (HI-1873).
- **The board no longer jumps down a moment after it opens.** The toolbar's *Workflow: Git · …* link came after the board was drawn, and in a narrower window it moved the toolbar onto two lines, shifting every column down under the pointer (a drag begun then could miss its card). The link now comes with the board's cards (HI-1852).
- **An agent's refused request shows in the Progress panel at once.** A refusal changes only a card's record, not the card, and the window kept waiting for a change to the cards before reading it: the refused notch on the card's lane segment (with its reason in the hover) could be missing until something else changed. For you and a card's Work section read such changes at once too (HI-1859).
- **A drag to Review with nothing committed says what you can do.** Its message named the card twice and told you to ask "the user" before committing, words meant for an agent. It now reads *HI-12 can't move to Review: Nothing is committed to review yet. Commit the card's work, then move it again.* Agents' refusals are unchanged (HI-1851).
- **Set Up for signed-in connections.** A connection signed in through Claude Code's browser sign-in still has Claude Code's first-run questions (its look, its notes) to answer, which its first agent met in its pane. Once the sign-in ends, **Set Up** (beside Close, or on the connection's row in Settings → Accounts & Connections) opens Claude Code on the connection so you answer them there first (HI-1826).
- **Waiting for a check copy is no longer an API failure.** An isolated check waiting for a check copy asks Hive again and again, and Performance counted each ask as a failed request (thousands an hour while copies were short). It now counts them as **Waits**, apart from failures: a column of the Agent API table, grey in the chart. After a minute's wait the check asks less often, up to every 10 seconds. Hours recorded before this still show those waits as client errors (HI-1831).
- **An agent's old worktree shows as its slot as soon as the upgrade moves it.** Opening a 0.9 workspace, an agent whose worktree became the slot kept for it could still show in its old worktree (in Changes, its pane) until something else made Hive read the projects again; it now shows the slot at once.
- **No more freezes away from the Projects view while agents work.** With several agents printing, Settings (and the other views, less) could freeze the window for 5–17 seconds at a time: every agent pane hidden behind the view redrew all its rows for each new line of output, measuring each character again. A hidden pane now draws nothing until it's shown again (HI-1827).
- **Switching between pages of agent panes no longer freezes the window.** With agents running on two pages, showing the other page could freeze the window for half a second to several seconds: the panes just hidden gave their graphics back and were drawn again at once, measuring each character with a layout of the whole window. They now do that out of sight and draw nothing while hidden, and in a test with nine to twelve agents a page switch has no pause of a tenth of a second or more (HI-1828).
- **An approval no longer goes stale silently.** A reviewer's pass is refused when the card's branch has moved on to another change since it was submitted (a follow-up commit), saying which commit to have submitted, so the new commit is reviewed, checked and approved as any submission. If a pass goes stale afterwards, the builder is told at once what to submit, the card says so, and its lane shows as stalled after 10 minutes (HI-1763).
- **The long-conversation notice offers Hand Over to… again.** A conversation nearly always passes the size limit while its agent is working, and the notice made then had no button. It now waits for the agent's turn to end, so it offers **Hand Over to…** whenever the agent may hand over (HI-1695).
- **The Activity tab shows what's running now** (Alt+9). Each running agent of the project has a tab: its card (or its session), then what it's doing now (the tool call under way, or thinking or writing), its subagents and background tasks, and the processes it started, each with how long it has run. Click one for its status, its exact input (secrets hidden) and what it did last; end one of an agent's processes from there, after a confirmation. The agent's own CLI is hidden unless you ask. It opens frozen, a snapshot you can read: **Refresh** takes a new one and **Play** follows it live. It opens on one agent from its ⋯ menu, and a card in Doing or Review has **Activity** in its dialog and menu (#1254, HI-1743).
- **The Lanes tab shows each lane's cards and how long it has waited.** Lanes now stack from the bottom and fill the tab. Open a lane to see its cards in order, each with its state, round, how long it took and a done card's merge commit. A lane's header says how long it has waited, in amber once that's long: over an hour for you, or 15 minutes for a reviewer (both in Settings → Advanced) (HI-1926).
- **Runs keeps today's runs at the bottom.** In the Progress panel's **Runs** tab, what's running stays at the top and the finished runs, **Today** first, are docked at the bottom behind a line you can drag (about six and a half lanes high, up to ten), as Pulse's lanes are. Every run is one line, with when it ended; click it for its details, several at once (HI-1925).
- **Activity across the workspace.** **Activity** in the activity bar shows every project's running agents together, their tabs grouped by project, with the Hive Assistant too. A card's **Activity** now opens it on the agents working on that card, in any project, workspace cards included, or says nobody is. A run or a lane in the Progress panel has **Show in Activity**. Each project's own Activity tab stays as it was (HI-1664).
- **Activity's card line stays on one line.** A long card title no longer pushes its key onto two lines ("LE-" over "186") or the column below it: only the title is cut, and hovering it shows the whole title (HI-1884).
- **A session you deleted stays deleted.** A session deleted while its workspace was closed, in a project whose sessions hadn't moved into 1.0's board yet, came back the next time the workspace opened (HI-1736).
- **A project folder you move or delete stays gone.** A new project folder moved out of the workspace while Hive was still reading it could come back as an empty project folder, which Hive then listed (HI-1817).
- **Review copies named for what they are.** Setting up a review copy is now a run of the reviewer it's for (*HR6*, "in copy-1"), so the Runs tab's Agent filter lists each reviewer once instead of a "Hive (copy copy-1)" entry per copy; with no reviewer it's one **Hive (review copies)**. In Version Control → Branches & remotes, review copies and the merge queue's folder are groups of their own, **Review copies (Hive's)** (who uses each, or idle) and **Merge queue (Hive's)**, and no longer show as unused worktrees (HI-1710).
- **Your answers reach someone who can act on them.** When you answer an agent's question by agreeing to something only you or the Assistant can do (move a card to Done past its rules, make it no code, skip its review), it no longer reaches only the agent, which can't: your reply goes to the Assistant too, and if it can't be told, the card and a notice say so. An agent's question can offer **Skip Review**, a single offered action has a **… With This Reply** button, and a card that passes with nothing left to merge is proposed as no code (HI-1734).
- **Agents can ask the Assistant.** An agent that needs something only you or the Assistant can do on a card (park a card On Hold, move one to Done on your answer) now asks it with a tool rather than in a comment, which reached nobody: the request is on the card as the agent's comment and wakes the Assistant, even while it watches lanes. The Assistant acts on it only within its Control and what you decided, and if it can't be told, the agent is told to ask you, or the card and a notice say so (HI-1866).
- **A card started just after a merge gets its slot.** When the merge queue merged a card and freed its slot, Hive's next pass over the slots could fail every write with "the merge queue … is run by another Hive now", so a card started then waited, its slot never prepared. That pass now runs on its own (HI-1685).
- **No more pauses of several seconds when Windows makes a restore point.** Hive's log lines are now written by a thread of their own; a restore point holds writes to the drive for a few seconds, which held all of Hive up while it wrote a log line (HI-1730).
- **A card the merge queue sends back reaches its builder.** A builder that started watching its card before reading that the queue had returned it (a conflict) was never told, and waited for Done while the card waited for it. The return is now always given, a builder that starts watching its own returned card is told of it at once, and a lane whose returned card sits for 15 minutes with its builder idle or only watching counts as stalled. Your answer to an agent is no longer dropped when the agent moves its card on before reading it (HI-1721).
- **Retry merge is offered only when it can run.** A card waiting for its reviewer to check a follow-up no longer shows a Retry merge that is then refused, and a card already retried for its revision says a new revision is the way back. The confirmation only mentions "once per revision" after a return, where that limit applies (HI-1667).
- **Hive's log names what held it up.** A pause's line in hive.log now names a database step or log write that waited for the disk, and a pause of all of Hive (the PC or the process paused) is no longer counted as busy (HI-1692).
- **Saving the board's database no longer holds Hive up.** Copying its log into the file (a checkpoint) now runs in a thread of its own; on a busy disk it held typing and replies for up to most of a second (HI-1691).
- **No more pauses when several views read a merge queue at once.** The Progress panel, the Version Control tab and a card's Work section each listed a project's merge queue from the board as it changed, about ten at a time, holding Hive up for 300–400 ms on a big board. A queue's listing is now worked out once for each state and shared (HI-1683).
- **Less lag on a big board.** Each change to the board made Hive read every card in full, archived ones included, to find cards newly Done; on a board of 1,600 cards that held up typing and replies for about 200 ms at a time while agents worked. Hive now reads only what it needs, from the board's index (HI-1690).
- **The Pulse tab's lanes sit at the bottom of the Progress panel again,** growing upwards, with the runs above them: they had followed the runs, leaving empty space under them (HI-1673).
- **A question's actions line up.** Under For you, the actions an agent's question offers (Move to Done, Make It No Code, Unblock) share one column of buttons as wide as the widest, so their reason boxes start and end together; in a narrow window each button sits over its box.
- **Watching agents name their cards by key.** An agent waiting on cards shows *Waiting for HI-1168 → Approved*, not the card's workspace-wide number, in its pane, its tile and the Overviews, and agents and the Agent API read it so too (its `statusMessage` and `watching`). In **Running now**, an agent waiting on one card shows that card's chip as a working agent does, and a chip's key no longer breaks onto a second line: the title is cut with "…" instead.
- **A card moved to Done stays Done in its workflow too.** A card from before 1.0, started by its agent, could be moved to Done on the board while its workflow still had it active, so its agent's question stayed under For you. Such moves now go through the card's rules, and cards left that way are put right when the workspace opens (HI-1674).
- **A Codex agent stopped by an error needs you too.** Codex says nothing of a failed turn in its hooks, so Hive reads it from Codex's own record of the turn and lists the agent under **need you** as **Stopped by an error**, with Codex's message, until its next turn starts (#1658).
- **For you no longer keeps questions that were settled another way.** A question on a card that reaches Done, or one whose agent carried on with the card (answered in its pane, say), is closed, with a line in the card's history, and a lane no longer waits for you on it. No question an agent or the Assistant asked about a Done card is listed under For you (#1638).
- **Agents' questions under For you are formatted.** They showed their Markdown raw, with the line breaks lost: they now render as card comments do (numbered points, bold, code), a long one folded with **Show all**. Decisions on a card render the same way, and a question's notification shows its first sentence or two as plain text (#1637).
- **A passed card whose branch went back to the revision that passed is merged again.** Its work changed after the review, which took it out of the merge queue, then was put back exactly as passed: the pass counts again and Hive queues it again, with no need to submit it again. A follow-up on a queued card takes it out of the queue first, and the reviewer's OK puts it back. A stale card's next step now says to submit it again, and **Retry merge** queues a passed card that was taken out of the queue (#1623).
- **"Need you" counts only what needs you.** The status bar's count is exactly what its list shows: the requests on cards waiting for you (For you) and the agents stopped in their panes at a prompt, a sign-in, a question or, newly, an error such as a lost API connection. Agents that only finished are no longer counted: a quieter **✓ 3 finished** beside it says so, and the list, the tray and the taskbar badge keep them apart. Each says what it counts on hover (#1632).
- **Fewer pauses while the disk is busy:** the board's and the Assistant's journal's databases are tidied up (their write-ahead log copied into the file) once they have been idle for a moment, not in the middle of a save, where it could pause typing in every terminal while a slot was being prepared. A save or a merge lock slow enough to be felt is named in Hive's log (#1633).
- **An update never replaces a build you made yourself with the same or an older version:** Hive reads the copy installed on disk when it would install, and installs only a newer version, comparing versions with prereleases (1.0.0-rc.2 is older than 1.0.0). A local build (`npm run dist`) says so in Settings → Updates, is offered a published release of its own version (Open Release Page) rather than given it, and its installer waits for an update's installer to finish before it installs. Before, a downloaded release could be installed as Hive quit over a newer local build (#1647).
- **A lane's notice names the right lane:** a card's failed review could be told under a lane that once listed the card and was abandoned (one started on it by mistake), rather than the lane it is in now (#1645).
- **A project removed from Hive keeps its sessions in its folder.** A change to its sessions that was waiting when the removal ended, or a read while its id couldn't be read for a moment, could take the records Remove from Hive had written into the folder back into the workspace (#1161).
- **No more "Couldn't check" warnings for branches that are gone.** Hive kept checking agents' old branches from before slots, and card lines deleted after their merge, every few minutes, logging a warning each time. It now leaves branches that no longer exist alone, and counts a line's unmerged work against the project's target branch (HI-1698).
- **Archiving or deleting many sessions shows its progress.** Clean Up Old Sessions… and a branch's Archive All… or Delete All… now say how far they have got (*Archiving 120 of 300 sessions…*) in the dialog and the Sessions list, and work on a few sessions at a time: on a test project, archiving 300 sessions took 6 s rather than 8 s, and sessions with 40 MB transcripts half as long as before (#1659).
- **The Sessions tab opens faster with hundreds of sessions.** Listing a project's sessions looked each one's transcript up twice, reading the names of every folder in Claude Code's projects folder each time, and checked Hive's backups one file at a time: with 600 sessions the tab took over a second to open each time. Each transcript is now found once, those names are read again only when the folder changes, and the sessions are looked at a few at a time: the same list opens in about half a second (#1649).
- **Opening a 0.9 workspace shows what it is doing, and nothing fails against its board meanwhile.** Opening Hive 0.9's Tools workspace took a minute and a half with nothing on screen, and the window let another workspace be opened. The window now shows each step (reading its projects, upgrading its board with the 0.9 board kept, adopting its cards and its agents' worktrees) and opens no other workspace until it is done. The skills and modes are updated a few at a time, and an agent's launch copies only the skills that changed, not all of them each time (the Assistant's start spent 6 s on that on a drive a virus scanner watches). While the board waits for its upgrade, the branch updater, metrics and the opening's repairs leave it alone (they logged "no such table: lanes" and "readonly database"), and the log times each step of the open and of the upgrade (HI-1694).
- **The Sessions tab shows Hive's sessions first, and finds the others without reading every CLI folder.** With hundreds of sessions in a dozen slots, the sessions started outside Hive were found by reading each CLI's folders in full on every listing, Hive's own transcripts included, and a listing asked for while one ran waited for it and then for another: on a busy machine the tab took up to 19 seconds to open. Each CLI now leaves Hive's own sessions out before looking at their files, reads a folder again only when it changed, and a listing under way is shared; the tab shows Hive's sessions at once, saying it is still looking for the others (HI-1681).
- **The Overview always loads.** On a project with hundreds of sessions, each update of the project Overview (and the Sessions tab) started a new session listing before the last had finished, and dropped the one under way: listings piled up (61 at once, each taking minutes) and the Overview spun for good. Updates now wait for the one before, Hive lists a project's sessions once at a time for every view asking, Codex's sessions are looked through once per listing rather than once per folder, and a load with no answer after a minute says so, with Retry. The Workspace Overview waits the same way (#1646).
- **A lane's notice is shown once:** once a lane ended, Hive told its card's failed review again on every look at the lanes, stacking identical notices and filling the card's history with them (#1631).
- **Lanes no longer stall for a watch a notice ended.** An agent in a lane that waits on the other side's move (its card in Review, its design to be answered, the next submission) is woken by Hive when that move comes, so it is no longer shown as stalled for ending its turn without a card watch, nor asks you to renew one. It shows as stalled again if the move becomes its own and it sits idle (HI-1693).
- **An agent is told only of what it can read.** On a busy board, an agent's turn could keep being stopped to read a card loop's wake that its inbox then wouldn't show or let it ack: any card's change while it read held the wake back. Hive now names only what the inbox would give, holds a wake back only when its own card or lane changed, and drops a "carry on" for a card that is Done once its lane is over (HI-1687).
- **Typing no longer pauses while lines move after a merge.** The repository lock that merges, the Git view's switches and the line updater take saved itself on Hive's main thread, waiting for the disk each time: after a merge, a burst of about ten takes held every terminal's typing up for 100 ms to over a second each. It now runs in a background thread of its own, with the same rules across Hives: with the lock busy, a keystroke's echo went from 1.2 s to 0.15 s (HI-1680).
- **Typing no longer lags while agents stream output.** Every frame a terminal drew made Hive measure the window's corner again and restyle the whole window, using a full CPU core with a dozen agents at work; the Progress panel's working lanes now animate without repainting, and the board's work badges are read once for each change (not once for each view showing them), and only the cards that change are worked out again (#1629).
- **The merge queue's folder is no longer offered as an unused worktree.** Between merges, Version Control's **Unused worktrees** listed the folder the queue keeps for its next merge as a detached worktree with **Remove anyway…**; while cards that Hive merges are in progress it is now left out, and once the queue no longer needs it, it is listed as the *merge queue's old folder, not needed now* (#1644).
- **The For you chip's hover is short and readable.** It showed an agent's whole question as raw Markdown, run together in one block; it now says who asks for what (*HB5 asks you to decide*), the question's first sentence or two as plain text, and that a click opens the card to answer. A card's decisions in its hover lose their Markdown marks too (#1635).
- **A card's chips and status fit a narrow column.** The **For you** chip ran past the card's edge and the agent's status (*Waiting on cards*) was cut off with no way to read it; both now end in "…" with the whole on hover, and a collapsed card shows the For you chip as its bell alone (#1634).
- **A long card title in Approved no longer squeezes the board's other columns.** A collapsed card's one-line title set how narrow the Approved and Merging group could get, so it took most of the board and On Hold, Todo, Active and Review shrank to their least width; every column now keeps its share (#1619).
- **A lane's brief reaches its builder's working conversation.** A brief sent to a lane's builder as the lane started was lost when Hive then gave the builder its first card in a new conversation in its slot. Now `hive_lane` `start` takes the brief itself, and Hive gives it with each of the lane's cards (or, where cards aren't built in slots, delivers it once to the builder) (HI-1795).
- **A lane's builder starts in its slot when the lane follows another straight away.** Starting a lane just as its builder's previous lane ended (its last turn still finishing in the same slot) could fail with *slot-a still has another session in it, or processes one left running there*, leaving the builder not started. Start Fresh could fail the same way. The start now waits the moment it takes for that run to let go of the slot (HI-1867).
- **An agent starts in its slot again after you quit Hive.** Quitting while an agent worked in a slot could leave the slot marked as still in use, so the next start there said *slot-b still has another session in it, or processes one left running there* until you released the slot by hand. Hive now finishes freeing the slot before it quits (HI-1801).
- **Quitting just as an agent starts no longer leaves its CLI running.** An agent's CLI starting at the moment you quit could miss being closed and run on with no window, listed as a process the agent left running and keeping its slot in use after Hive restarted. Hive now doesn't start a CLI whose terminal is closing, and ends one that outlived its terminal (HI-1819).
- **A card handed over after a restart resumes its agent's conversation.** When Hive was quit just after a card started, before it reached its agent, the next start began that agent in a new conversation, setting its last one aside, and Resume All then said it was already running. Now Hive resumes the agent's last conversation in the card's slot and types the card into it. It waits for Hive's own resume after an update, never starts afresh while that conversation can't resume just now (the card says why), and tells you on the card if resuming fails three times. A Resume All that meets that resume reports it as resumed (HI-1746).
- **An agent's old worktree no longer stops it mid-lane.** An agent's worktree carried into slots at the upgrade was kept for the card it had in flight; when that card later finished, Hive stopped the agent even while it worked on another lane. Now an agent with work elsewhere keeps running (and is stopped once that work is over, so its old worktree is freed; HI-1784), and an old worktree kept for a card its agent has moved on from is let go at the agent's next start, so it retires on its own (HI-1731).
- **A resumed agent keeps one slot.** Resuming an agent puts it back in its last slot; when it then started a lane while that slot's card was still in review, the lane was given a second slot, and with one slot per builder another builder found none free. The lane now takes over the slot the agent resumed in (its last card's work stays on that card's branch), unless the slot has work no branch would keep, and lets it go once that work is safe (HI-1741).
- **A lane whose builder hasn't started its next card is reported as stalled.** When a lane's card is Done and its builder sits idle for 15 minutes without starting the next one, the Hive Assistant's lane watch now says so, as it does for other stalls (HI-1741).
- **A board an earlier 1.0 release candidate upgraded opens again.** A newer build asked to upgrade it once more, as if it were from 0.9, and the workspace then failed to open ("no such table: store_rows"). The board's later 1.0 steps now run without asking (HI-1718).
- **The Merging column collapses.** Its « did nothing, because the board's saved layout left Merging out. It now folds to a strip under Approved's heading like any other column, and stays folded after a restart (HI-1662).
- **The notices above a project fit a narrow window.** The notice offering a workflow for new projects cut its Not Now button off at the window's edge (900 px at 150% zoom), and the coding agents banner squeezed its text to a word a line beside Choose providers. Their buttons now wrap together under the text when they don't fit beside it, and stay whole however narrow the window (HI-1717).
- **The Task Board's toolbar fits a narrow window.** The search box shrank to a sliver and the Workflows button was cut short; now the search box keeps a usable width and the toolbar wraps onto a second line when its controls don't fit (HI-1725).
- **The Hive Assistant's panel leaves room for your work in a narrow window.** At 150% zoom in a 900 px window, opening the panel left the project's pane no width at all. Now what is beside your work gives way in turn (the panels narrow, then the sidebar, then the Projects list folds to its rail and the Progress panel to its strip) until there's room again; nothing covers your work, and your saved layout doesn't change (HI-1737).
- **A builder's next lane no longer finds "no slot is free" while its last lane winds down.** When a lane ended, the builder's session stayed in its slot until its turn was over; starting its next lane in that turn was refused with every other slot in use. Its next lane or card now carries on in that same slot (HI-1762).
- **Agents can read the files attached to a card.** A reviewer asked to check a builder's attached evidence couldn't open it: Hive's document tools don't read Hive's own folder. Agents now read a card's attachments through Hive with `hive_read_attachment`: text in pages, and the path of an image or other file to open themselves (HI-1751).
- **Folded columns are narrow strips again on a board with stages.** On Hold, Todo, Active, Review and Done stayed about 140 px wide when folded on a board that shows Approved with Merging; they now fold to the same narrow strip as Approved and Merging (HI-1724).
- **The board's columns no longer jump when it opens.** The board drew a default set of columns first, then added Merging and the Approved heading once your workflow was read, pushing every column down and Done to the right. Every column now comes with the board's cards, and the Approved heading sits on a line in the gap above the columns, like a fieldset's label, so the columns keep their tight top and nothing moves (HI-1682).
- **Security: Electron updated to 44.10.0 (Node 24.22.1), fixing CVE-2026-111497,** a flaw in Node's Brotli decoder (HI-1503). Hive's checks for a newer Claude Code, Codex or GitHub Copilot also refuse compressed replies, so no network reply reaches that decoder (#1582).
- **Editing Hive's settings files by hand no longer loses the edit.** When an editor or a sync tool saved `project.json`, `workspace.json` or `config.json` while Hive read it, Hive could take it for damaged and put its last good copy back. It now waits for a file that is still changing, treats it as damaged only once it has stayed unreadable for 2 seconds, and a change Hive makes to such a file waits too rather than overwrite your edit (#1598, #1600, #1601).
- **An agent with subagents still working isn't shown as finished:** it shows **Waiting on 2 background tasks** until the last has ended; Codex's spawned subagents count too (#1584).
- **Resizing the window no longer stops Hive reading an agent's screen** (#1572).
- **Upgrading a workspace's board needs no restart:** the merge queues, project keys, card ids and the other steps Hive skips while a board waits for its upgrade run once you choose Upgrade, and nothing logs errors about the read-only board meanwhile (#1621).
- **The Progress panel's lanes show whole lanes again:** with many runs above them, the Lanes section shrank with the runs, leaving part of a lane behind a small scroll bar however its top edge was dragged. The runs now give way and scroll (#1620).
- **Quitting Hive no longer logs a slot warning:** Hive's look at its slots (leases to release, lanes waiting for one, merged branches to delete) could still be running as Hive closed its boards, and failed with *No workspace is open*. Hive now finishes it before closing, starts no agent in a slot while quitting, and leaves anything it didn't get to for the next start (#1628).
- **Prompts Hive types aren't held back by an invisible character** (a zero-width space, a soft hyphen) pasted into a task: Claude Code 2.1.309 waited for another Enter (#1547).
- **Codex agents aren't left marked "A line Hive typed wasn't taken"** after reading it while compacting (#1514).
- **The Microsoft Store PowerShell notice gives its steps in the order that works:** uninstall the Store copy first, then install with winget (#1512, GitHub #8).
- **Progress keeps its estimates on a busy machine:** `hive-progress` remembers 1,000 commands, one-offs going first (#1549).
- **A workspace you close or switch away from is let go,** under every name for its folder (a junction or an 8.3 name), so Windows can move or delete it before Hive quits (#1341, #1379, #1402, #1456).
- **A worktree setup command with quotes runs:** a quoted path (`node "C:\My Tools\setup.js"`) reached the program with its quotes (#1454).
- **No reviewer left on a card after a close:** reviews of reviewers no longer running end as the workspace opens (#1392).
- **No crash when many agents' sessions end at once** (React error #185) (#1366).
- **A moved agent stays where you put it** while the move is saved.
- **A compaction or search hit opened from the Overview stays in view** (#1323).
- **Menus near the window's right edge keep their width** (#1329), and **a notice's buttons wrap** rather than run past its edge (#1574).
- **The Assistant's journal gives back the space its size cap frees.**
- **No more "Permission mode changed"** when an agent starts a new conversation with its mode unchanged (#1234).
- **A card's unmerged commits are counted against where it merges.** An agent's unmerged work in a slot (its pane's Merge… and its Changes tab) was counted against `main` when its lane's slot was lent before the project's target branch was passed on, so a card heading for another branch (such as `1.0.0`) showed hundreds of commits. It's now counted against the card's target, else the project's default branch, and a card's branch that isn't made yet no longer logs a warning (#1627).
- **Opening a workspace is fast again, even while other windows are busy.** Opening an already-upgraded workspace took 4 to 11 s on 1.0 while agents worked in another window: each file the open read waited its turn behind that window's file work, and the open read hundreds of them one after another, most of them to check the workspace's copies of Hive's skills and modes. The window now shows the workspace as soon as its projects are read, reading them all at once, and the skills and modes are brought up to date just after, in the background (an agent started meanwhile waits for them). With every file read held up the same way, an open that took 3.6 s now takes about half a second. The log times each part of the open, and what it starts in the background in one "after opening" line (HI-1705).
- **New Project opens the project it made again.** Since 1.0.0-rc.5, File → New Project… made the folder but left the window on the project shown before, with a "was added: how do you work?" notice instead of opening the new project's Version Control tab at that step. Giving the new project its id while the project list was being read made Hive hand back the list from before, without it (HI-1760).
- **A busy window no longer slows the others down.** Hive's file work all shares a few threads, four until now: while slow file work (one window's agents, a large read, a file the antivirus holds) kept them busy, another window's open, its skill updates and its session listings waited their turn for each file, and could take seconds. Hive now has sixteen: in a test with eight such jobs always running, a second window opened in 0.3 s instead of 1.6 s, finished bringing its skills and settings up to date in 0.2 s instead of 6 s, and listed its sessions in 50 ms instead of 400 ms, about as fast as with Hive quiet. The log notes when a file call was slow, waiting for a free thread or for the disk (HI-1702).
- **A card change no longer sends the whole board to every window.** Each change made each window read the board again: on a board of 1,600 cards, 3.3 MB and about 40 ms of Hive's main process each time, felt in typing while agents worked. A window now gets only the cards that changed, about 10 KB and 3 ms, and typing into an agent's pane stays quick meanwhile (HI-1715).
- **Checks no longer fail to count for files the card never touched.** A check started right after its folder was updated (an isolated check's copy set to the card's revision, or the target merged into the card) could be refused with "files changed in the folder while it ran", naming those files, because Windows reported their changes late. Hive now judges each file by when it was written, so only changes made while the check ran count.
- **No more "Could not put slot folder back" errors in hive.log.** Hive checks that nothing is running in a review copy or slot by moving its folder aside for a moment and back. When several of these checks hit one copy at once (several isolated checks starting together), one could move the folder out from under another, and a copy could even be treated as free while its folder was still moved aside. These checks now run one at a time per folder, and anything that looks for the folder meanwhile waits until it is back.
- **Reviewers get a review copy again while builders run checks.** A copy an isolated check (`hive-progress --check … --isolated`) had used stayed taken for as long as its builder kept working, which in a card loop is nearly all the time, so reviewers were left waiting for one. It is now free within seconds of the check ending. A reviewer still waiting is told which copy should come free first and what is holding it, and a wait of more than five minutes shows as stalled to the lane's watchers.
- **For you no longer reads every card's work on each change.** Each view showing For you read it for itself on every card change, about seven reads of every live card's work, around 50 ms of Hive's main process on a board of 1,600 cards. The views now share one read, which reads again only the cards that changed (about 1 ms), and main's longest pause after a change is down from about 52 ms to 18 ms (HI-1754).
- **Costs no longer keep Hive's main process busy while agents work.** Hive shares each session's use out between the cards its agent held. It did that after every card change, and before every card's cost line, working out every card's holds again for each agent: about half a second of the main process each time on a board of 1,600 cards. With a dozen agents at work it was busy most of the time, and typing and windows lagged. Each project's holds are now worked out once and kept until they change. On a copy of a busy workspace, a card's cost takes about 30 ms instead of 800 ms, and the main process's load while cards change went from 73% to 19% (HI-1748).
- **A card change no longer makes Hive re-read the lanes' cards.** After every card change, Hive's main process read every running lane's cards in full three times, folded their history again, and worked out each project's rules again. It now reads again only what changed, so its work after a change on a board of 1,600 cards dropped from about 18 ms to under 10, and its longest unbroken stretch from up to 18 ms to about 6 (HI-1780).
- **Git's commands no longer start on Hive's main process.** On Windows, starting a process holds up the thread that starts it for a few milliseconds, and Hive runs git constantly: for each check an agent runs, the Git view, the merge queue. Git's processes now start on a thread of their own, so a check's start costs Hive's main process about 10 ms instead of about 45 (HI-1783).
- **Each git Hive runs is one process, not two.** Where the `git` on Hive's PATH is Git for Windows' launcher (`Git\cmd\git.exe`, as most installs have it), every git Hive ran was the launcher starting the real git. Hive now starts the real git itself, with the environment the launcher gives it, so hooks and aliases run as before: half as many processes for git, and each git about 10 ms sooner. Any other git on PATH is started as before (HI-2137).
- **A window that freezes now says why in hive.log.** Hive's log notes the window's long tasks (over 200 ms): how long, which view, project, tab and dialog were open, and what had just arrived or changed. When the window stops responding, the log says where it was and when it recovers. Help → Copy Diagnostics includes these lines, so a freeze you report can be traced (HI-1775).
- **Board writes cost Hive's main process less.** Hive re-read its `.hive` folder's listing after every change to the board's database files there, and prepared each board query again on every read. It now watches only the folders it shows from and keeps its prepared queries, so a check run in its own copy (`--isolated`) costs the main process about 30% less (HI-1797).
- **A check in its own copy costs Hive's main process less again.** Each time a check copy was reset, Hive read every branch and worktree of the repository from its files on the main process, and it read and parsed each project's `project.json` in full several times per check. The repository's files are now read on git's own thread, and `project.json` is read again only once it has changed, so an isolated check's start costs the main process about 15% less (HI-1814).
- **The window keeps up with many agents at work.** Each agent's change of state re-drew the whole window, the Task Board's every card included: with a dozen agents working, the board stuttered for up to half a second at a time. Agents' states are now shown together a few times a second, and only the parts of the window that show a changed agent or card are drawn again. In a two-minute test with twelve agents, the Task Board's pauses of a tenth of a second or more fell from 123 to about 22 (HI-1806).
- **The window no longer asks Hive about the presets notice with each agent's change of state.** The notice after the upgrade from 0.9 asked Hive's main process again every time an agent changed state: with a dozen agents working, about 400 times in two minutes. It now asks again only when a project or its workflow changes (HI-1807).
- **Switching between Panes and Tiles can't fail any more.** It saved the choice in the project's `project.json`, so while another program had that file open the switch failed with *Could not change the view … EPERM*. The view (Panes or Tiles, the layout and where the panes are) is now kept on your PC with Hive's own window state, and a project's settings are still saved at once. When a file can't be saved because something holds it, Hive's log now says so, with the program that had it open (HI-1749).
- **No more empty info tips in Settings.** A setting with nothing to add beyond its description, such as Settings → Assistant → MCP servers (this workspace), showed an ⓘ that opened an empty box; now only settings with a tip show one (HI-2149).

## 0.9.3 — 2026-10-20

- **The Assistant's modes are in `.hive/modes`.** Modes were once called personas, and their files still were: each workspace's are now in `.hive/modes`, where Hive moves its `.hive/personas` folder when the workspace opens, your own modes and edits with it, and the mode you chose stays chosen. If the folder can't be moved yet (a file in it open), Hive uses it where it is and moves it next time (#1084).
- **Agent API: `personaBytes` is now `modeBytes`.** In `GET /v1/metrics`, each guidance series' `personaBytes` and `personaChars` are now `modeBytes` and `modeChars`. The old names are still sent in this release, and go in the next one (#1084).
- **Codex asks before other MCP servers' tools, as Claude Code and Copilot do.** Hive started Codex agents with the tools of every MCP server on in their project approved in advance, so a server you added ran its tools without Codex asking. Now only Hive's own `hive` tools are approved in advance, in every CLI; another server's tools follow Codex's own approval rules for the agent's mode, like its other tools: Read only and Ask for approval ask before such a call (unless the server marks the tool read-only, or you allowed it for the session or always), Approve for me leaves it to Codex's reviewer, and Full access doesn't ask. A hand-made `.hive/mcp/hive.json` (the name is Hive's own) is no longer given to sessions either.
- **The code editor uses the fixed DOMPurify.** Monaco, the Files tab's editor, ships its own copy of DOMPurify 3.4.15, which has two advisories (GHSA-p98j-92pf-mc4p, GHSA-6688-9rhm-gjv2; both need a way of sanitising Monaco doesn't use). Hive now builds Monaco with its own DOMPurify 3.4.16 instead, so the copy no longer ships and `npm audit` no longer lists it (#1141).
- **Tiles keep one size.** In the Tiles view, a tile grew when its agent started a long run (its progress bar) or had a longer reply, and shrank again after, so the tiles jumped as you read them. Every tile now has room for everything it can show and keeps that size; text too long for its line ends in an ellipsis and shows in full on hover (#1145).
- **A resumed agent isn't told of new work before Hive's tools are back.** A Claude Code session resumed (the Assistant's too) is ready a moment before Hive's tools have reconnected in it, and a message's notice typed then was a turn the agent couldn't read it in: it said its tools had disconnected, and was told again later. Hive now waits until the agent's tools have connected, at most 30 seconds (*hive tools: wait to connect* in Settings → Advanced), before it tells the agent (#865).
- **The first agent you resume after starting Hive starts sooner.** Hive tracks the processes each agent starts through a hidden PowerShell helper, which it started only when the first agent launched, so that launch (the first of a Resume All) waited about half a second for it. The helper now starts in the background as Hive reopens your workspace, and the first launch finds it running (#1155).
- **Purge what Hive keeps.** A new **Purge…** in **Project Settings → Storage** and **Settings → Workspace → Storage**, and **Purge Hive's Files…** on a session in the Sessions tab, remove everything Hive keeps for sessions: its backups of their transcripts, the archive, attachments, and what is left of deleted sessions. It lists every file and its size first, then moves exactly those to the Recycle Bin, or deletes them permanently if you choose. The coding agents' own files are never touched, running sessions are left alone, and what sessions used stays in your usage totals; a session whose only copy was Hive's is deleted, as Delete Session does, and the dialog warns you first (#1138).
- **Resuming a long session no longer makes Hive re-read its transcript.** A running agent's footer and context gauge were worked out in Hive's main process, and after a restart the first turn of a resumed session read its whole transcript again (several hundred MB for a long one), while every terminal's typing waited on the same process. They're now read in the background by the usage history's thread, which carries on from where the history left off, so a resumed 300 MB session's figures are ready in a few hundredths of a second and still follow each turn. Most of the wait after **Resume** on a very long session is the coding agent loading its conversation (#1147).
- **Transcript backups take about a third of the space, and can expire.** Once a session ends or is archived, Hive compresses its copy of the transcript in the project's `.hive` folder; you can still open, search, export and resume it as before, and backups from earlier sessions are compressed in the background. Storage now says what the backups hold before compression. A new setting, **Settings → Sessions → Keep transcript backups** (Always unless you change it), sends Hive's copies of sessions idle for 30 days to a year to the Recycle Bin, only for sessions the coding agent still has, so none becomes unreadable; a session whose only copy is Hive's is never removed this way (#1110).
- **The Workspace Overview opens at once, and usage totals no longer depend on transcripts.** Hive now records what each session used as it goes, in a usage history in the workspace's `.hive/usage.db`, read and written by a background thread, so the Overviews no longer read every transcript on Hive's main thread (on a workspace of 282 sessions and 565 MB of transcripts, the first open went from about 1.5 s to under 40 ms, and later ones from 75–140 ms to about 13 ms). A session's usage stays in the totals once you delete it or its transcripts are gone, a running session's is recorded about every minute, and card and lane costs come from the history too. The first time a workspace opens in this version, Hive imports what its existing sessions used in the background, losing nothing already counted; the Workspace Overview shows how far it is. `usage-cache.json` in your Hive profile is no longer used and is removed (#1106).
- **Polish: four more messages no longer guess a name's gender.** The note that a coding agent stayed busy while Hive switched its mode, the note above a provider's effort levels, and the "Hive closed unexpectedly" lines about a merge left half done made a past-tense verb agree with the agent's or provider's name. They now use the present tense or put "Agent" in front, as the rest of the Polish translation does (#1137).
- **Agents and the Assistant can tag cards without wiping their labels.** Changing a card's labels used to replace them all, so tagging fifty cards meant one change per card with each card's labels typed again, and a bulk change could wipe cards' other labels. The board tools and the Agent API now take `addLabels` and `removeLabels`, which add or take off only the labels named, in bulk too, and the card's history says just what was added or removed (#1131).
- **A long archive no longer slows the board down.** Hive read every card, archived ones included, for work it does many times a minute: checking for new decisions around each agent's board call, following lanes for their watches, ending reviews and archiving old Done cards. On a busy board that is mostly archived cards and their comments, so each check took about 65 ms and briefly held around 20 MB. These checks now read only the cards they need, in about 5–11 ms (#1107).
- **"Hive closed unexpectedly" knows about commands agents had only just started.** Hive keeps a record of what agents have started while it runs, so that after a crash its notice can list what is still running and offer **Stop Them**. A command an agent started just as its turn ended, or one started later by a process it left, could be missing from that record for up to a minute. Hive now updates the record a few seconds after anything new starts in an agent's session (#1134).
- **Cards say which doc sections to read first.** A card's new **Read first** lists sections of the project's docs or shared notes by file and heading (`docs/PLAN.md#setup`, `shared:plans/design.md#extraction-lanes`); each opens in Hive from the card, and the agent's start prompt lists them with their sizes, so it reads three sections rather than a whole build plan. Agents and the Assistant set them too. A reference that no longer resolves (its file gone, its heading renamed or now twice) shows on the card with the closest headings and is named in the agent's prompt, never silently dropped.
- **Agents read long documents by section.** Two new hive tools, **hive_doc_outline** and **hive_read_section**, give an agent a Markdown file's headings with each section's size, then just the section it needs, by heading or slug: for project docs and shared notes, so a 56 KB design note no longer fills an agent's context. The Agent API has them too (`/v1/docs/outline`, `/v1/docs/section`).
- **Put any agent in any pane.** Drag an agent's pane header onto an empty pane to move it there, or onto another agent's pane to swap them; panes can be left empty (*Drop an agent here*, with **Move Agent Here ▾** to bring in an agent from another page). From the keyboard, **Move Left/Right** and, in a grid, **Move Up/Down** (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+arrows) move the focused pane a cell, said to screen readers. Where you put them is kept for each layout and survives a restart, so switching layouts and back restores it; new agents take the first empty pane.
- **Less disk churn, and old files tidied.** Hive's settings file is written smaller and without a second copy at every save; the board's folds and the model lists now live beside it in `ui-state.json`. The backups kept when the board and other records moved into the workspace database go to the Recycle Bin two weeks after the move, Hive keeps only the newest three crash dumps, and its running marker is written without holding Hive up (#1111).
- **A lighter Progress panel.** While runs report progress, Hive now sends the window only the runs that changed, not every run it keeps up to four times a second, so a long Recent list no longer costs the window work on every step (#1108).
- **Fewer false "main process was busy" warnings, and real ones say what was running.** Time the PC spent asleep was counted as Hive being busy, so the log had warnings of many seconds while Hive sat idle. Hive no longer counts a pause that spans sleep, measures pauses more precisely, and each warning now names the window action, Agent API request or agent hook that was running, with how long it took (#1109).
- **Deleting the workspace folder no longer keeps Hive busy.** When the open workspace's folder was deleted or moved away, Windows kept telling Hive about it without end (about 99,000 notices in half a second), so Hive used a CPU core until it closed. Hive now stops watching the folder and looks for it again every few seconds, carrying on as before once it's back (#964).
- **A message left for an agent that's just starting on a task waits for that task.** An agent started with a task (a card's Start, a new conversation) is ready a moment before its coding agent takes the task. A message or wake that arrived just then could be typed first, so the agent read it before its task and Hive lost track of the line. Hive now waits until the task has been taken.
- **The Assistant hears your replies to agents at once.** When you answer an agent in its pane, the Assistant is told within seconds even when it isn't waiting on that agent at that moment (between its watches, or while it watches lanes), instead of a few turns later.
- **A stalled card says why when the agent's wake is stuck.** When an agent in a card loop watched its card but the wake Hive typed for it wasn't sent, the card now shows *"hasn't taken the wake Hive typed for #n: it waits in its prompt"*, with how to send it, instead of saying the agent ended its turn without watching.
- **Switching branch or pulling holds Hive's messages to that folder's agents.** While the Git view switches or pulls the project folder, the messages, wakes and tasks Hive sends agents working there, and agent starts there, wait until git is done, so no turn starts while files change under it.
- **Codex and Copilot agents take Hive's lines at the first Enter when busy.** A message's notice or a wake that reached a Codex or Copilot agent while it was busy for a moment could sit in its prompt until Hive pressed Enter again 10 seconds later, or, under heavy load, until you pressed it. Copilot now gets Hive's lines as a paste, as Claude Code does, and with Codex Hive presses Enter only once Codex shows the line.
- **The flight recorder catches rare events for you.** Some things happen too rarely to catch with the developer console open. Turn on a trigger in **Settings → Advanced** (all are off by default) and Hive saves the agent's recent developer console events by itself when it fires: a model's safeguards stopping a response, a CLI sending a field Hive doesn't know (often the first sign of a CLI update), an agent starting on a new CLI version, an agent gone silent mid-turn, or an event matching a pattern of your own. A capture is a redacted recording in Hive's `console-recordings` folder, with a notice and **Show in Folder**; the console's new **Captures** button lists them, and Copy Diagnostics offers the newest. Each trigger captures an agent at most once every 10 minutes, and Hive keeps the newest 20 captures. While a trigger is on, Hive keeps each agent's newest events in memory (redacted, at most 256 KB each); with all off, nothing changes (#698).
- **The developer console speaks your language.** Its window now loads Hive's language at all: it couldn't ask for it before, so it showed English everywhere (#1104).
- **The Performance view and the developer console are translated.** Both were English in every language; their headings, buttons, filters, explanations and notices now follow Hive's language, like the rest of the app. What the developer console records (events and recordings) stays English, as Hive's log does, so a recording reads the same to whoever it's sent to. The descriptions of the skills Hive ships are translated in the Skills view too, as the Assistant modes' already were; the skills themselves, which agents read, stay English (#1104).
- **Polish: "Aktywne" in the sidebar.** The Projects sidebar's section of active projects is now *Aktywne* in Polish, instead of *W pracy* (#1104).
- **No more garbled terminal text after long sessions.** Now and again, after hours of output, a line in an agent's terminal drew with letters from two drawings mixed (*Clicking the sow stili opegsathe agent*) until you scrolled. Terminals share a cache of drawn letters, and when it filled up and reorganised itself mid-drawing, letters already drawn in that moment pointed at the wrong place. Each terminal now redraws itself right after, so the text is right again at once (#1048).
- **No more endless "Compacting…" between turns.** When Claude Code prepares a compaction by itself while an agent is idle, it may never say it has finished; the agent then showed *Compacting the conversation…* until its next turn. It now shows ready again once the transcript shows the compaction, or after 10 minutes at most, as Hive's own Compact does (#1115).
- **Polish: Settings → Advanced's notes on a CLI no longer guess its gender.** The notes under a value that no longer matches what a CLI sent or showed made their verbs masculine to agree with the CLI's name; they are now worded without agreement, like their sibling note (#1132).
- **The code editor's right-click menu is readable again.** In the Files tab, the editor's menu (Go to Symbol, Format Document, Cut, Copy, Paste…) showed dark text with no background or border. It now looks like Hive's other menus in both themes: a surface with a border, readable entries and shortcuts, and the hovered entry filled with the accent colour (#1102).
- **An agent that stops its own background task no longer shows Working afterwards.** When a Claude Code agent stopped a background command itself (`TaskStop`) just before its turn ended, Hive took the task's end for one that wakes the agent and showed it working with no turn running, refusing tasks given to it, until Claude Code's idle notice a minute later. It is now finished at once (#640).

- **Session records join the board in its database.** Each project's `.hive/sessions.json`, rewritten whole at every change of a session (746 KB on a busy project, about 7 ms a time), is now rows of the workspace's `.hive/hive.db`: a change writes only the record it changes, in well under a millisecond, with no temp file or rename for Windows to refuse. The file moves in the first time Hive reads a project's sessions and is kept as `sessions.json.imported-<date>`. **Remove from Hive** writes a project's sessions back into its folder, so restoring it, or opening it in another workspace, brings them back; a folder copied or moved by hand starts with an empty list, as its cards do (#1061).
- **Agents and terminals find the Claude Code Hive found.** When Claude Code's own installer left its folder off your PATH, Agent Setup found Claude Code but a shell Hive started couldn't run `claude`. Hive now adds the folder of each CLI it finds to the PATH of what it starts, without changing your own PATH (#1103).
- **A warning for Codex with the Store's PowerShell 7.** Codex's Windows sandbox can't start the Microsoft Store's PowerShell 7 while another copy of it is open, so Codex agents' commands failed at random. Agent Setup now warns when that PowerShell is the one Codex would use, says how it shows, and how to switch to the installer's PowerShell 7 (#585).
- **Copilot compactions show up.** A Copilot agent's compactions now count in its usage and appear in the Sessions tab with their summary, and after a `/compact` typed in its terminal the agent shows as ready again once Copilot has finished, not only at your next message (#454).
- **Plan usage no longer flips between figures.** With several agents running, the status bar's weekly figure could jump back and forth (98%, then 96%, then 98%) because each session passes on the figures from its own latest request, and a resumed Codex session could even bring back last week's. Hive now keeps the highest figure each limit has reached in its reset period, takes the new one as soon as it resets, and its tooltip says which agent reported each limit (#383).
- **The Assistant's start view on Codex and Copilot.** An Assistant running on Codex or Copilot now gets the same short view of where things stand as on Claude Code: each time it starts, and again after its conversation is compacted (on Copilot, with your next message after a compaction) (#1065).
- **Card loops review committed work.** The card-loop skill now has the builder commit each round before sending a card to Review and name the commit; a reviewer handed uncommitted work asks for a commit rather than failing the round over it (#1074).
- **The Assistant steps in when a review keeps finding new edge cases.** When a lane's card fails review a second or third time on the same kind of finding, the Assistant brings you a simpler option with its recommendation instead of letting the loop run on. For a push or a release, it asks you to give your OK in the agent's own pane, since the agent's CLI refuses an OK passed on by the Assistant (#1075).
- **The Assistant puts your decisions on the card.** When you decide something about a card in the Assistant's pane, it records the decision on the card first, where the card's agent sees it, and its journal only notes it. An agent's question it brings you is passed on in the agent's own words (#1136).
- **QA triager mode files better cards.** A new Assistant skill, **triage-report**, has the QA triager find the cause in the code before it writes the card, check for a card that already covers the problem (including ones reviewed but not merged yet), and file the card with what you saw, the evidence and how to tell it's fixed (#408).
- **The Assistant tidies the board.** Ask it to tidy the board, and a new skill, **board-hygiene**, has it link duplicate cards for you to archive, record your answers as decisions on their cards, and raise blocked reasons that are out of date (#409).
- **A licence audit skill for agents.** **licence-audit** has an agent check the licences of everything a project ships (dependencies, bundled libraries, files vendored inside packages, fonts and icons, what the installer adds), report them in a table and flag anything that isn't plainly permissive for you to decide (#410).

## 0.9.2 — 2026-10-19

- **Retry a Copilot update that WinGet refuses.** If WinGet cannot remove the old portable package, Agent Setup explains that its files may have changed (for example, through Copilot's own updater) and offers **Update anyway** to retry with `--force`. Hive only forces the update when you click it, and still asks you to stop running Copilot agents first (#1101).

## 0.9.1 — 18 October 2026

- **MCP servers: a workspace switch and each project's own choice.** A server now runs in a project only when it's on in the workspace and on in that project. Adding a server adds it turned off, with no questions. Turning its switch on in the MCP view shows what it runs and asks which projects to turn it on in (the selected project ticked); turning it off stops it everywhere and keeps each project's choice for next time. New projects start with servers off, and a project's MCP tab says **Off in the workspace** where its switch is waiting on the workspace. Upgrading changes nothing that runs: servers on for all projects stay on wherever they were.

- **Start small, finish a block, start fresh.** The user guide's Getting started now says how to keep a plan's usage down: one agent to begin with, a lighter model for routine work on project agents (not the Assistant), work in blocks of a few hours, then start every session fresh. The Assistant suggests it once, in a line, when every lane has ended or an agent's card is done with nothing else running, and checks its journal holds what is still open before it does.
- **The Assistant's start view stays short on a big board.** What waits on you now shows at most ten lines; past that, agents asking now and cards blocked on a question first, then On Hold cards waiting on a decision of yours, before those waiting for a later version, then a count of the rest. The other sections are capped too, so a board with sixty On Hold cards no longer adds thousands of tokens to every new Assistant conversation (#1078).

## 0.9.0 — 18 October 2026

- **The Assistant keeps a journal.** Each workspace's Assistant has a journal, its memory across sessions: decisions, plans, events, incidents and what it learned, each a line with optional detail and links to cards and commits, searchable and recalled a few lines at a time instead of a long handover. Hive adds facts as they happen (lanes started and ended, merges, cards into Done, an unexpected close). **Settings → Assistant → Journal** turns it off (keeping it), chooses what it may store (work only by default), how long everyday entries stay (90 days) and a size cap (50 MB), and deletes it. It is kept on this computer only, in `.hive/assistant/journal.db` (#1031).
- **The Assistant picks up where it left off.** With its journal on, the Assistant gets a short view of where things stand each time it starts and after every compaction: what waits on you, the cards in hand, lanes and agents, and the few journal entries that matter now, kept to about two thousand tokens. It writes your decisions in your words as you make them, recalls before deciding from memory, and writes a weekly digest. Asked for a handover, it says one isn't needed and writes one only if you still want it; with the journal off it writes handovers as before and suggests the journal once (#1032).
- **A long conversation's notice no longer offers to cut into an agent's work.** When an agent's transcript passes *Warn when a transcript is over*, the notice says so, and offers **Hand Over to…** only while the agent is idle, not merging and not on a card loop's card; otherwise it says the agent can hand over when it has finished. Hive checks again when you click, and just before the conversation ends: an agent that took the merge slot or started other work while it wrote its handover keeps its conversation (#1057).
- **Read and correct what the Assistant remembers.** The Assistant view has a **Journal**: its journal as a timeline, newest first, with the cards each entry links to. Search it and filter it by kind, project, who wrote it and dates; open an entry for its detail and why, edit or delete it; **Forget These…** deletes everything a search finds, after saying how many. The weekly digests have their own list, the footer shows the size and retention, and while the journal is off it says so and stays readable (#1033).
- **Panes give way on a narrow window.** When the panes of your layout would be too narrow for a terminal (the narrowest window with the sidebar and the Progress panel open, say), the Session tab shows fewer columns for now (two columns become one at a time, three become two) instead of terminals cut off at the pane's edge. Your layout isn't changed: its button still shows it, and the panes go back to it once there is room. In Tiles' focus view, the agent's terminal comes first: the strip of agents takes at most two fifths of the width, narrowing to its least before the terminal narrows, then steps aside to keep the terminal whole.
- **Dismiss a "has finished" row in the Assistant panel without going there.** Pointing at (or tabbing to) a row in the panel's Workspace section now says what a click does (*Click to open HR3 and dismiss*), and a finished agent's row has a small **×** on its right that removes it without opening the agent. It comes back only when that agent finishes again.
- **Fewer project tabs, numbered in order.** **Memory**, **Skills**, **Templates** and **MCP** have moved into a **More ▾** menu at the end of the project's tabs, which shows the name of the one that's open. The tabs left are numbered as they appear: **Alt+1** Session, **Alt+2** Overview, **Alt+3** Performance, **Alt+4** Tasks, **Alt+5** Sessions, **Alt+6** Files, **Alt+7** Attachments and **Alt+8** Git (Alt+0 is still Project Settings), and the menu bar's **Project** menu lists them the same way. **Changed shortcuts:** Sessions was Alt+3, Files Alt+4, Attachments Alt+5 and Git Alt+6; Memory (Alt+7), Skills (Alt+8) and MCP (Alt+9) have none now, but are in the Project menu and the command palette, and you can give them one in **Settings → Keyboard Shortcuts**. Shortcuts you set yourself are kept.
- **The task board is kept in a database.** A workspace's cards move from a thousand small files in `.hive/tasks` into one SQLite file, `.hive/hive.db`, the first time 0.9 opens it (the old folder is kept as `.hive/tasks.imported-<date>`). Every change is saved whole or not at all, and the renames that Windows could refuse while another program held a card file are gone: archiving 170 cards with 12 agents running takes under a tenth of a second. The board sends archived cards to the window in short until you open **Archived** or one of them. **Export Board to JSON…** (the board's **⋯** menu) saves the cards as files again. (#1017).
- **Performance metrics join the board in its database.** Hive's performance metrics were one `.hive/metrics/metrics.json` rewritten whole, up to 2 MB, every 30 seconds while you worked; they are now rows of `.hive/hive.db`, one per hour or day, so a save writes the hour under way and adds each new one. The file moves in the first time 0.9 opens the workspace, with the same numbers on **Performance** and **Overview**, and is kept as `metrics.json.imported-<date>` (#1019).
- **Lanes, card watches and agents' inboxes join the board in its database.** The card loops' lanes (`lanes.json`), agents' card and agent watches (`watches.json`) and each project's deliveries, what Hive has for its agents to read (`deliveries.json`, the busiest of them, rewritten whole at every message), are now rows of `.hive/hive.db`: each change writes only what changed, in one transaction, with no temp file or rename for Windows to refuse, and a watch can no longer be saved after its workspace closed. Their files move in the first time 0.9 opens the workspace and are kept as `….imported-<date>` (#1018).
- **Review cards made by an older Hive.** An older Hive shows an empty board for a workspace whose board moved to the database; cards added there are set aside in `.hive/tasks.set-aside` when 0.9 opens it again. **Review…** on the notice (or **Set-Aside Cards…** in the board's **⋯** menu) lists them, and **Add to Board** adds each one, under its old number when it's free, or **Discard…** sends it to the Recycle Bin (#1044).
- **Backing up a transcript no longer gets in the agent's way.** Hive's first copy of a session's transcript could stop its coding agent writing to it at that moment (Windows reported the file busy): Hive now reads the transcript in a way that lets the agent keep writing. It was seen once in Hive's tests, where it ended an agent's session at its first prompt (#1041).
- **The window title no longer runs into the menu.** In a narrow window in a language with long menu names (German's "Hilfe" ran into the project's name), the title now moves aside and is shortened with "…", or hides when there is no room.
- **Switch Now doesn't wait on one agent.** When you change the permission mode setting with agents running, **Switch Now** switches them side by side: one Codex agent waiting for your answer to its Full access warning no longer holds up the others (#1024).
- **A test run left over from an ended session says so.** A test run waiting for its turn now names a run holding the slot whose agent's session has ended as *left over from a session that ended*, and a run starting in the same worktree says so, so a resumed agent knows its earlier run still goes; nothing is stopped on its own. The Agent API says whether an agent's launch still runs (`GET /v1/projects/{name}/agents/{agent}/launches/{run}`).
- **Codex switches to Full access without a restart.** Choosing Full access for a running Codex agent now switches it in place after Hive's confirmation, through Codex's own `/permissions` menu like the other presets; answer Codex's own warning in its terminal and the conversation carries on. Claude Code's Bypass permissions still restarts the session when Claude Code requires it, and the mode menu says *Restarts the session* only under modes that will.
- **Fewer leftover files in `.hive`.** A save that fails now always removes its temp file, and opening a workspace removes what earlier failed or interrupted saves left there (temp files over a day old) and the copies the project-id migration made of each card before changing it (`*.before-ids.bak`, over a week old), saying in Hive's log what went (#1016).
- **Tiles: your whole team on one screen.** Switch the Session tab to **Tiles** and each agent becomes a small tile: its state, name and provider, its status, its card, its progress and the first two lines of its latest reply, with badges for background tasks and warnings. Twelve fit a page, and agents waiting for you stand out. Click a tile (or press Enter) to open the agent's pane large, with the others in a strip beside it, each with a small chip after its name giving its card's number, tinted with the board column the card is in (Doing, Review, Passed, or red when blocked): click it to open the card. Long names end in an ellipsis before the chip. Drag the strip's edge to widen or narrow it (double-click resets it; the width is remembered); **Esc** outside the terminal, or **Ctrl+Alt+Esc** from anywhere, goes back. **Settings → Sessions → Tile order** keeps the panes' order (the default, with a count on the page buttons of agents waiting elsewhere) or puts the agents that need you first. The tiles show what Hive already knows: no terminal is drawn for them and no CLI is asked anything. In the panes' order, drag a tile onto another to swap them, or onto a page button to move it to that page (**Move Left** / **Move Right** in a tile's menu, or Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Left / Right, from the keyboard); the panes follow.
- **When Hive closed unexpectedly, the next start says so.** If Hive was ended without quitting (a crash, something ending its process, the power), the next start shows when it was last seen, which agents were running in which projects, whether a crash dump was kept and any merge left half done, with the folder to check. **Resume All** resumes those agents; nothing resumes on its own unless you turn on **Settings → Sessions → Resume agents after an unexpected close**. It also lists what the agents had started that is still running (test runs, servers), from Hive's own record of their processes, with **Stop Them** to end exactly those; nothing is stopped on its own. A command an agent ran through `hive-progress` (a test run, usually) is one item named by its title, and Stop Them ends the whole run. A process of the run that Windows lets Hive neither end nor check is never counted as stopped: each press of Stop Them after says it may still run, with its process ID to look for in Task Manager, since Hive can no longer tell it from another process.
