The Hive Assistant is told too, so it can report stalled card loops. A normal quit, a restart, an update or Windows shutting down never shows it.
- **Hive no longer runs out of memory when many cards change at once while agents are running.** Each card saved told the board twice, and for each time Hive read the whole board again once for every running agent, never waiting for the last read to finish: archiving 76 cards with agents running took over a minute and could close Hive (its main process ran out of memory). A batch of card changes (Archive All, a batch move, a project's cards) now tells the board once, after it, cards changed by hand or by another program are told once a burst, and what follows the board reads it one read at a time: the same archive takes a second or two.
- **Finding out what fills Hive's memory.** Hive's log now notes how much memory its main process uses at start, every five minutes and as it closes, and warns when it passes 70% and 85% of its limit; Help → Copy Diagnostics shows the latest. **Settings → Advanced → Save a memory snapshot if Hive is about to run out of memory** (off by default) has Hive write a snapshot of its memory to `%APPDATA%\Hive\heap` just before it would run out, keeping the newest two: they can be gigabytes, hold whatever Hive had in memory and stay on your PC (#1005).
- **Knowing why Hive stopped.** Hive now keeps a crash dump when it, its window or one of its helper processes crashes, in `%APPDATA%\Hive\Crashpad\reports` on your PC only: it never sends one. Help → Copy Diagnostics names the newest, for you to attach to an issue. Hive's log also says how every run ends (a quit and who asked for it, an update, Windows shutting down, sleep, a crash of its window or an error nothing caught), so a run whose log just stops was ended from outside.
- **A calmer project header and tabs.** The project header is one quiet row: the project's name, its agents' states in one line, counting the idle and stopped ones too (*1 waiting · 2 working · 7 idle · 2 stopped*), the **Active** switch, **Resume** and **Stop**, and an **Agents ▾** menu for **Start New**, **Archive and Start New** and **Remove All…**, which were three buttons. **Resume** and **Stop** are quiet buttons like **Agents ▾**, their colour on the icon (Stop turns red as you point at it), with short labels, **Resume (2)** and **Stop (10)**, whose tooltips say them in full. On a narrow window the header stays one row by folding: **Active** moves into **⋯**, the agents' states shorten to the most important one (hover for all of them), and at the very narrowest **Resume** and **Stop** move into ⋯ too (#1051). The header is a few pixels shorter, closer to the tabs, and the project tabs are shorter and tighter, and **Project Settings** has left them: it opens from the header's **⋯**, **Project → Go to Project Settings** or **Alt+0**.
- **A session toolbar instead of the agent strip.** Above the panes: **Panes | Tiles**, a chip that counts your agents by state (*1 waiting · 2 working · 3 idle*) and lists them, most urgent first, to jump to one (from the keyboard too), the pages and layouts, and **+ Add ▾** with Add Agent, Add Agent from Template and Templates. Each agent's own controls are on its pane: its menu in **⋯** or a right-click on its header, its details in its name's hover, and drag a pane by its header to move it. **Settings → Sessions → Default session view** chooses Panes or Tiles for projects that haven't chosen yet. On a narrow window the toolbar folds: **+ Add** becomes just **+**, then **Panes | Tiles** become icons (point at one for its name), and the agents' counts are shortened last.
- **Agent states you can tell apart without colour.** Hive sums up each agent as waiting for you, stalled, working or idle, each drawn with its own shape (a diamond, a square, a pulsing dot, a ring) as well as its own colour; the project header's state line uses them first.
- **A quieter pane header.** Each agent's pane header now shows only its state, its name, its status and its card, with **Stop** (or **Resume** while it isn't running) and **⋯**. Everything else is in **⋯**, also a right-click on the header: Compact…, Hand Over to…, Review Changes and Merge… (with what is left to merge), the agent's settings and Remove. Processes it left running, unread messages and the files it is editing are at the top of that menu, with a dot on ⋯ when something needs a look. **Session → Agent Menu…** opens it from the keyboard. Hover the agent's name for its provider.
- **A one-line pane footer.** Each agent's footer is now one quiet line: model · effort · mode on the left, and the transcript's size · ctx 42% · cost on the right, the same in the agents' panes, the focus view and the Assistant's panel. The context's tokens are in its hover. Warnings show only when they matter, in amber: the context past your Compact threshold, the transcript's size past its warn size (click it to hand over), and background tasks still running, just left of the size. In a narrow pane the size goes first. The running session's name, Open in Sessions Tab and Rename Session… are in the agent's ⋯.
- **The Assistant's panel in the same design.** Its header works like an agent pane's: its state mark, **Assistant**, its mode as a small pill, **Stop** (or **Resume** or **Start**) and **⋯**, which now has Compact…, New Conversation and Hide the Assistant too. Under it, one line says what it is doing, ending in **Done by the Assistant (n)**: click it to open that list. The workspace is one line per project, with a state mark for each running agent (and any stuck on the board): hover one for the agent, what it's doing and its cards, click it to go there, or turn on names beside the marks. The activity bar shows the Assistant as a crown on a hive cell, the queen of the hive, in place of the robot.
- **Moving agents quickly no longer makes the panes flicker.** When you moved or swapped agents a few times in quick succession while some were running, Hive's slower reads of the projects could finish after a later move and show the agents in an order from one or two moves back for a moment, redrawing the panes and tiles. A read that began before a later change to the project's agents is now dropped, and the next one shows the order as it is.

## 0.8.1 — 17 October 2026

- **Disable hardware acceleration.** For a graphics driver that leaves Hive's window blank, flickering or crashing, **Settings → Advanced → Disable hardware acceleration** has Hive draw without the graphics card, and its terminals without WebGL; **Restart Hive** applies it. To start once without the graphics card (a window that doesn't draw at all), run Hive with `--disable-gpu`. Copy Diagnostics says which is on (#676).
- **Handing over to an agent that isn't running no longer loses the handover.** Hive started the agent taking over and typed the handover into it a moment after it looked ready, and on a busy PC Codex could look ready before it took what was typed: the agent started with nothing to do, and the next hand-over from it was refused. The handover is now its launch's first task, as a task started on an agent is (#782).
- **An agent's new worktree opens at once in the Files and Changes tabs.** When an agent's worktree changed (a new agent, one moved or removed) while Hive was still reading the projects, an earlier read that finished late could put back the worktree from before: until the next refresh, the Files and Changes tabs refused the agent's current worktree and still opened the one it had left (#784).
- **Left-running processes stay tracked when Windows reuses a process id.** When an agent's launch ended just as Windows gave one of its process ids to another agent's process, Hive could stop following that new process, so what it left running outside its agent's job as it exited wasn't listed or ended with that agent. It was never seen happening, and never ended a process Hive didn't start (#604).
- **Pass right-click to Copilot.** Right-click is Hive's copy and paste in every pane; in a GitHub Copilot pane, Shift+drag selects text for it (a plain drag is Copilot's own selection, which Ctrl+C copies), as the user guide now says. To have right-click copy Copilot's own selection instead, as Copilot's footer suggests, turn on **Pass right-click to Copilot** under GitHub Copilot in **Settings → Advanced** (#737).
- **Changing many cards at once is faster on a big board.** Hive read through the whole board's folder each time a card was saved, so on a board of 500 cards a change to 30 cards at once took about 0.8 s; it now takes about 0.1 s, whatever the board's size. Cards edited by hand or by another program still show on the board at once (#588).
- **An agent releasing the merge slot releases its own.** An agent that claimed the slot for main and then checked its branch out in the project folder (as merging main into its branch does) and released it without naming main kept holding main's slot, and other agents waited for up to an hour. Releasing now gives up the agent's own hold, whatever branch the folder is on, and when it holds nothing there the reply says which slots it does hold (#586).
- **Your answer reaches a builder waiting at its lane's end.** When a card loop's builder blocks its last card, in Passed, to ask you something (its commit was refused, say), and you answer as a decision on the card or through the Assistant, Hive now tells the builder, as it does for a card in Doing. Before, the builder never heard and kept waiting (#877).

## 0.8.0 — 16 October 2026

Hive now speaks eight languages. This release also cleans up old sessions in one go, makes attachments easier to scan, runs a workspace's MCP servers only in the projects that need them, fits narrow windows better, and keeps **Sign in** in view in Agent Setup for new Copilot users.

### Languages
- **Hive in eight languages.** Hive's window, menus and messages now come in English, Deutsch, Español, Français, Polski, Português (Brasil), 日本語 and 简体中文. Hive starts in Windows' display language when it has it, else in English; **Settings → General → Language** chooses another (*Automatic* follows Windows), and the change shows at once. Numbers, sizes, percentages and the words in dates follow the language too (*1,5 MB* and *pon., 5 paź* in Polish), and so do key names in menus (Strg and Entf in German). The installer speaks the same eight languages. `--lang=<code>` starts Hive in a language for one run.
- **Seven of them are translated by AI, and say so.** Every language but English was drafted by AI, so expect the odd awkward phrase: the list marks them (*Polski (AI)*, *Deutsch (KI)*), with a note in that language under it. Each has all of Hive's text translated except two views for developers, Performance and the developer console, which stay in English.
- **Help → Improve This Translation.** Spotted a wrong or clumsy word? This opens the guide on GitHub for fixing a translation, or adding a language, with a pull request; each language's glossary and style notes are in [`docs/translating`](https://github.com/Suremise/hive-desktop/tree/main/docs/translating).
- **The user guide stays in English for now.** The documents in the Docs view (the user guide, the Agent API reference and the release notes) are in English whatever language Hive shows, though the view around them follows it; the Hive Assistant knows Hive well and answers you in your language. What Hive tells agents and the Assistant (their instructions, skills and the `hive` tools' replies) stays English too, so they work the same in every language. Names you gave things, and what agents wrote, stay as they are.
- **The plan limits tooltip speaks one language.** The status bar's tooltip mixed Hive's English with the day names of Windows' language; it is now all in the language Hive shows (Suremise/hive-desktop#1). **Date format: System** follows Windows' regional format, whatever language Hive shows.
- **Japanese and Chinese are set their own way.** Lists are joined with 、, sentences follow each other without a space, lines don't start with small kana or closing punctuation and labels break between phrases, paths show \ rather than ¥, and the "Hive can't start" window uses the language's own font.
- **Clearer wording in English too.** Counts in notifications, the tray menu, agents' tabs, lanes and archived cards agree with their number in every language; model and effort lists say "default" once: "Default (Opus 5.5)", "Default (Medium)", and "Claude Code's default" when Claude Code hasn't said which level that is (#892, #911, #916); and errors about cards say *card* in the window (the Agent API's replies are unchanged).

### Long languages and narrow windows
- **Text fits, or shows it's cut short.** Where text was cut off or ran past its edge in a longer language, and often in English in a narrow window, Hive now shortens it with "…" (in full on hover), wraps it or makes room. The project header folds its buttons to icons, keeping the project's name and status; the agent strip fades where more agents are out of view, with a › to scroll to them; board columns, the archived cards' table, the Sessions tab, the Assistant's skills and modes, the Progress panel's runs (a reporter's name shows whole, the project name gives way first), templates and the Load template dialog fit too (#886, #890, #892, #905).
- **Settings in a narrow pane.** A row too narrow for its text beside its control puts the control under the text, and the section list takes less room or becomes a row across the top; the shortcuts and API prices tables, the Updates status and About fit as well (#887).
- **Save stays in view in a narrow Files pane.** The file toolbar's buttons show as icons (named in tooltips) when their labels don't fit, and wrap onto a second row when even those don't (#814).
- **The Git tab's Changes fits a narrow window.** With the Projects sidebar and the Progress panel open, the list of changes pushed the unused worktrees or the diff out of sight; it now narrows first, then moves above them (#906).
- **Hive's window fits the display it opens on.** Opened on a smaller display than it was last on, the window ran off the display's edge; it now opens no larger than the display and wholly on it, unless the display is smaller than Hive's smallest window (900 × 560) (#812). The developer console's window does the same (#862).

### Sessions
- **Clean up old sessions in one go.** The Sessions tab's new **Clean Up Old Sessions…** button archives (the default) or deletes every session last active more than 24 hours, 7 days or 30 days ago. The dialog says how many will go before you confirm; running sessions, the one you're reading, the Hive Assistant's current conversation and any in use are always left alone, and the result says which (#895).

### Attachments
- **Easier to scan.** Files that aren't images show their type's icon (Markdown, JSON, PDF, zip, code, text) instead of one icon for all, every tile shows its size, and a session's heading shows the days its attachments were added ("Oct 9 – today"), with a divider where each day starts (#900).

### MCP servers
- **Turn a server on only in the projects that need it.** A server in the workspace is now available to every project and runs only where it is on: each project's MCP tab has a switch for every workspace server, and the switch in the MCP view turns one on for all projects. Adding a server from a file asks where to turn it on: the selected project, all projects, or none yet. Copy to workspace turns a project's own server on in that project. Servers you had on stay on where they were (#713).

### Agents
- **Agents as a table in more places.** Stop All, Start New, Archive and Start New, Remove All and the quit dialog list their agents as a table (each one's state, card, worktree and what happens to it) instead of a line each, and hovering the status bar's running sessions shows the running agents the same way (#572).
- **Remove All and the unused worktrees list are quicker on a busy machine.** Checking which worktrees could be deleted reads the project's worktree list and main branch once, not once per worktree.

### Progress panel
- **Put your lanes in your own order.** Drag a running lane up or down in the Progress panel's lane list (a line shows where it goes), or focus it and press Alt+Up or Alt+Down; its right-click menu has Move Up, Move Down and Usual Order. Each workspace keeps the order, after a restart too: a new lane comes in on top, and a lane that is merged or abandoned drops to the bottom, after those that stopped before it, so the latest is always last (#568).
- **The filter applies to merges too.** Filtered to a project (or one of its agents), the panel still showed every project's merge on top; it now shows only that project's, or that agent's (#810).

### Card loops
- **An agent's question for you no longer sits unseen on a card.** A builder or reviewer that needs your decision now blocks the card with its question as well as notifying you, so the card shows as blocked, the Assistant's lane watch picks it up, and the agent clears it once you decide. Before, a reviewer could ask only in a comment, which nothing flagged (#840).
- **A lane's merge that waits on you no longer sits unseen.** A builder whose merge at the lane's end needs your OK (its CLI refused a commit, for example) now blocks the last card saying what it needs, as well as notifying you. And if a lane's cards have all passed while its builder sits idle for 10 minutes with no merge slot claimed, the Assistant's lane watch is woken: the lane's merge is stalled (#869).
- **A review left without a verdict shows as stalled.** A reviewer that started reviewing a card and then went back to waiting on it for 15 minutes, with no verdict and no question blocking the card, now stalls it, on the board, in the Progress panel and for the Assistant's lane watch (#843).

### Hive Assistant
- **A decision on a card in Review reaches its card loop.** Recording your decision on a card in Review, or clearing its block, wakes neither the card's builder nor its reviewer, so the Assistant now follows it with a message to the builder, and to the reviewer when it changes what they check, instead of the loop waiting until its watch runs out.
- **Apps that follow the Assistant no longer have to read its sentences.** Each of its actions on the Agent API's event stream now says what it was (`kind`: starting a card, giving an agent a task, moving cards…) and what it was about (the project, the agent, the card or cards, the lane, the column), so a viewer such as Apiary keeps working when the sentence is reworded or translated. Nothing private is added. The API's version is now 5 (#763).

### Fixes
- **Sign in is always in sight in Agent Setup.** A CLI installed at a long path (WinGet's package folders) widened the row and pushed **Sign in** out of the dialog, so a new GitHub Copilot user couldn't sign in. The path now wraps, with the full path in its tooltip (#821, Suremise/hive-desktop#2).
- **What you install while Hive is open reaches new terminals and agents.** Hive now reads the PATH Windows gives new programs just before it starts an agent, an Agent Setup terminal or **Open External Terminal Here**, so a CLI or tool installed outside Hive is found there without **Check again** or a restart (#821).
- **The workspace no longer goes back to how it was before a change.** When Hive read the projects twice close together on a busy machine, the earlier reading could finish last and show an agent list from before the change, and say again that two agents share a name. The later reading now stands.
- **Retry in the Skills and Templates views shows the skill or template you then select.** When their list couldn't be read, Retry in the sidebar listed them again, but selecting one still showed the failed read (Skills) or nothing (Templates) until you left the view and came back (#841).
- **Copy Diagnostics no longer briefly offers an old recording.** Opened again, the dialog showed the last open's developer console recording row until it had read the newest one, even when the newest couldn't be attached (nothing was attached either way). It now shows no row until the newest is read.
- **Every link in the third-party notices opens a web page.** Eleven packages that name their repository in a short or SSH form (`owner/repo`, `git@github.com:…`) had links that went nowhere (#788).

## 0.7.1 — 13 October 2026

### Advanced settings
- **How Claude Code's and Copilot's footers show each permission mode is in Settings → Advanced too.** Hive reads an agent's mode from its footer, and watches it while switching modes with Shift+Tab: if a CLI update renames a mode there, the mode's setting fixes it without waiting for a Hive release.
- **How long a stopped Copilot agent gets to exit is a setting.** Settings → Advanced → GitHub Copilot → **Stop: exit grace** (10 seconds by default, 3 to 60): Hive ends a Copilot that hasn't exited by itself after that long, losing its token totals. Raise it if a very busy PC still loses them.

### Agents
- **A stopped Copilot agent keeps its token totals on a busy PC.** Copilot writes a session's totals only as it exits, which took up to 2.4 seconds under load, and Hive ended it after 2.5 seconds, so on a busier machine the totals were lost. Hive now gives it 10 seconds, ending only a Copilot that doesn't exit; quitting Hive waits for it.
- **Loading a template is quicker on a busy machine.** Its plan and load read the project's worktrees, branches and current branch once, and each worktree's state once, instead of once per agent: about a third to a half fewer git commands, so the Load template dialog no longer takes seconds to open while tests or builds run.
- **Waiting for an agent you just started on a task waits for that task.** `hive_wait_for_agents` (and `POST /v1/agents/wait`) called right after starting an agent with a prompt, or giving it one in a new conversation, could answer at once that it was ready: its CLI was up but hadn't begun the task yet. It now counts as working until its CLI takes the task.

### Hive Assistant
- **One watch for the lanes and the cards beside them.** The Assistant's watch on all running lanes can also follow cards in no lane, such as a release card: a comment, a move, a verdict or a block on one wakes it in the same line as the lanes' events, and its next lane watch keeps them. Its status says, for example, **Waiting on all running lanes and #720**.
- **The Assistant hears when an agent waits for you.** An agent showing a permission prompt or asking you something in its pane now wakes the Assistant's lane watch once, naming the agent, its card and what it asks, so it can tell you rather than let it wait unnoticed. It never answers for you.

### Messages
- **An agent waiting on its own timer still hears from you.** An agent that ended its turn while a background command of its own runs (a sleep, a reminder to renew the merge slot) now gets messages and card events within seconds, rather than once the command ends. Agents are also told to wait for things with a card watch rather than a timer.

### Terminal
- **Copying from a Copilot pane works.** Copilot selects text with the mouse itself and copies it when you press Ctrl+C, saying "copied to clipboard", but the clipboard kept what it had. Hive now takes a CLI's copy (an OSC 52 request) from the pane you're using, and never lets a CLI read the clipboard.

## 0.7.0 — 13 October 2026

### Agent names
- **Agent names are unique across the workspace.** Two projects can no longer each have a B1, which couldn't be told apart on the board, in notifications or by the Assistant. Adding or renaming an agent with a name another project's agent has is refused, and Hive offers one with your project's initials in front (B1 in *hive* becomes HB1). Agents that already share a name keep working: Hive says so once, with a button to rename one.
- **Name a template's agents as you load it.** The Load template dialog shows each agent's name in the template beside a box for the name it will get. A name another project's agent has is marked, saying whose, and **Load template** stays greyed out until it's changed.
- **Why a template won't load without git.** A template whose agents work in their own worktrees can't load into a folder that isn't a git repository (or has no commit yet). Hive now names those agents and says what to do: make it a repository, or put them in the project folder.
- **Add Agent** says what `hive/` in a branch name is for: it marks branches Hive made, so it can reuse and tidy them up.
- **Worktree branches name their workspace and project.** A new worktree agent's branch is `hive/<workspace>/<project>/<agent>`, such as `hive/HIVE/ambrosia/amb1`, so several workspaces can share one repository, even with projects of the same name, without their agents' branches clashing. Branches made before (`hive/b4`) are still Hive's: kept worktrees on them are reused and tidied as before.

### Messages
- **Messages to agents are kept, and you can see where they got to.** What the Assistant leaves an agent (`hive_message_agent`) is now a saved delivery with an id: it survives the agent stopping and Hive restarting, and its state goes from waiting to offered, **received** (the agent read it with its new inbox, `hive_inbox`) and **acked** (the agent says it acted on it). The pane shows **✉ 1 unread** until the agent has it, and **✉ 1 overdue** when it hasn't acked one within 30 minutes; the Assistant sees each one's state and times in the agent's activity. A message still waiting when the Assistant that left it stops, or Hive restarts, is refused rather than sent later, and the Assistant's activity list says so.
- **Nothing Hive tells an agent is lost or counted as delivered just because it was typed.** Card watch wakes, loop handoffs, your decisions, the Assistant's mode changes, a lock's go-ahead, the carry-on after a sign-in, handovers and tasks are now saved deliveries too, offered by one scheduler: each is received once the agent reads it in its inbox, and a card watch ends then, not when Hive types its wake. One Hive was typing when it stopped, or that an agent's restarted session never read, is offered once more, saying it may repeat. Tasks, handovers and your decisions are for the agent to ack; a wake or notice needs none.
- **Hive types only a short line into an idle agent.** Everything Hive has for an agent (a task, a card watch's wake, your decisions, a message, a notice) now arrives as one fixed line naming it by id (*"[Hive] New in your inbox: 1 task (#12) (m9). Call hive_inbox to read it."*), and the agent reads what it says from its inbox, labelled with who it is from. It counts as received only once the agent has read it; one let pass rings once more when the agent is idle again, and a message can be cancelled until it is read. With **Settings → Agent API** off, agents have no inbox: what Hive or the Assistant would give one is refused, saying so, and a card you Start yourself is typed in as your own input.
- **A busy agent hears of a message between its steps, without anything typed over its work.** A message the Assistant leaves a working agent now reaches it at its next tool call, as a short notice from Hive in its CLI's own hook reply, or just before its turn would end; the agent reads it from its inbox and acks it. Hive's notices say only how many items there are and their ids, so text in a card or a message that claims to come from you is read as a quote, never as your instruction, and agents ask you before deleting work or acting outside their project, whoever asks. Works in Claude Code, Codex and GitHub Copilot.
- **A message about a round goes stale, not late.** The Assistant can tie a message to a card's current round (`hive_message_agent` with `card`). If the card passes, leaves Doing and Review, or starts another round before the agent reads it, Hive drops the message as **stale** instead of delivering it late, so an agent no longer reopens a passed card for an old finding. The agent's activity and the Assistant's activity list say why.
- **Agent API**: `POST /v1/inbox` (an agent's or the Assistant's own inbox, and `ack`), `GET /v1/projects/{name}/agents/{agent}/deliveries`, and the activity's `inbox` replace `messagesWaiting` and `lastMessages`; the event stream's `queuedMessages` is now `inbox`. The API's contract version is now 4.

### Sessions
- **Transcript sizes in the Sessions tab.** Every session, and every Assistant conversation, shows its transcript's size beside its context, amber past **Warn when a transcript is over** with a tip to hand it over to a new conversation, and **—** when the CLI no longer has the file.

### Performance
- **What each card cost.** A card's panel now says what its agents used while they held it, such as **cost: ≈ $1.84 · 2 rounds**: its builder while it was in Doing and its reviewer from the start of each review to its verdict, as each provider reported it, with each side's part and tokens on hover. Performance has a sortable **Cards** table (cost, builder, reviewer, rounds) for the cards worked on in its range, a row opening its card; and hovering a lane's top line in the Progress panel shows what the lane cost, split into its cards and its **overhead**, what the builder and reviewer used holding no card (merges, watching, compactions), with the cost per finished card. Estimates are marked ≈ and what Hive can't know shows as **Unknown**, never $0. Cards worked on before this keep their cost too, worked out from their history.

### Progress
- **A lane's marks explain themselves.** Hovering a card's segment in a lane now says what its red underline means (*#650: review failed (round 4): waiting for round 5*, with the reviewer's one-line gist) and why red cross-hatching is there (the card's blocked reason, or that it's On Hold, waiting for you). Screen readers get the same text.

### Agents
- **A Copilot agent asking whether to trust its folder shows as waiting, even on a busy PC.** Copilot can draw its prompt a moment before its trust question; Hive took the prompt to mean it was ready and kept showing it ready while it asked, so nothing said it needed you. Hive now still reads the question until the agent has taken its first prompt.
- **More than twelve agents in a project.** **Agents per project** in Settings → Advanced raises the limit of twelve, up to 24, for all workspaces or just one. Above 12 it shows what more agents cost on your computer: the memory your running agents take now and how much is free. Pages, templates, the agents table and agent watches all work with 24. More than six pages show as **‹ 3 / 12 ›**, with a menu of every page. Lowering it removes no agents.
- **One agent's leftovers no longer take in other agents' PowerShell.** With the Microsoft Store's PowerShell 7 installed, the PowerShell one agent's session started could take in every Store PowerShell started after it on the PC, another agent's or another app's: they were listed as the first agent's leftovers, and **End Them** ended them too. Hive now follows such a process on its own, also when it catches one in the first moments after it starts, so each agent lists and ends only what it started.
- **See when a safety classifier stops an agent.** When a CLI's safety classifier stops an agent's response (Claude Code's model safeguards, Codex's cyber checks), a shield beside its status says when, on which model, and the names of its last few tool calls before it (never what was stopped), with what to do about a false positive. When the CLI asks you about it, the notification and pane say why; once you've answered, the agent no longer stays **Needs input** but shows idle after the stop, ready for a nudge. If its model changes after one (a silent fallback), the pane shows the model it runs on now, the agent is in **Agents that need you**, its card gets a note, and the Hive Assistant sees it. Performance counts the stops per agent and model. Copilot's sessions don't record them, so they aren't detected there.
- **A session's automatic name is the same everywhere.** Until it has a name, a session is named by when it started. The agent's footer and the Sessions tab could show that time a minute apart, and a resumed session's footer showed when it was resumed. Both now show when the session began.
- **What agents left running survives a restart.** Processes an agent left running that Hive didn't ask you about as it quit (a quit to install an update, or with no window open), or that were listed when it crashed, are listed again the next time Hive starts, with **End Them** and **Keep Running**. Hive checks that each is still the same process, so it never offers to end a program that has since taken its place. A dev copy of Hive that an agent's test run left open now shows up there too.
- **Agents stop what they start.** Agents watching their own test runs with `tail -f` left those watchers running long after the runs ended (16 from one agent in a day). Hive's guidance now tells agents to watch a run with something that ends with it (`hive-progress`, their CLI's notice that a background command ended) and to end the watchers, servers and app copies they started before a card goes to Review.
- **Stopping a run ends only what it started.** When a command run with `hive-progress` is stopped, Hive ends it with what it started, as before, but now checks when each process started: a process that only shares a parent id the command happens to have reused (an older one whose parent had ended) is left running.
- **An idle agent no longer stays "Working…".** When Hive missed how a Claude Code agent's turn ended, the agent could show **Working…** at an empty prompt for good, and the Hive Assistant couldn't give it a task. Hive now takes Claude Code's own "waiting for your input" signal, a minute after a turn, and shows the agent idle again. Claude Code's compaction while idle and its recaps leave an idle agent idle.
- **An agent's status follows its CLI's turns, so it can't freeze.** Hive now tracks each turn by the CLI's own id. A tool result that reaches Hive after its turn has ended no longer puts a finished agent back to **Working…**, and a turn whose end Hive never heard of is closed from the CLI's own record (Codex's log of the finished turn) or from its idle prompt on screen (Claude Code 2.1.296 and Codex 0.162.1). When there's no sign either way, the agent shows **Silent 23 min · state unknown** instead of pretending to work or to be idle: nothing is typed into it, and **Mark idle** in its pane puts it right. Each hook Hive receives is now logged, with its ids, in `hooks.log` beside `hive.log`, so a stuck status can be traced. A card loop's stall line now says what the reviewer is really doing, not just that it's watching.

### MCP servers
- **Give a server to some agents only.** An agent's settings now have **MCP servers: All of the project's / Only these**, so a server's tools reach only the agents that need it (a release-testing agent gets a sandbox server, the builders don't) and the others keep a smaller context. All of the project's stays the default; Hive's own `hive` server is always given; templates keep the choice. The agent's tooltip and the project's **MCP** tab show who gets each server, and the Agent API reports and changes it (`mcpServers`).

### Git
- **Initialise repository, in one click.** A project that isn't a git repository has an **Initialise repository…** button on its Git tab: it makes the folder a repository on a branch called main, keeps its .hive out of git before anything is staged, adds a starter .gitignore if you choose one (minimal, Node, Python, .NET or Godot; a .gitignore of the project's own is kept), and makes the first commit, so agents can work in worktrees at once. With no name or email set in git, it asks for them and saves them to that repository only, never your global config. A folder inside another repository needs your confirmation, and one another version control system keeps is refused.
- **The Git view.** A project's **Changes** tab is now **Git**, with three sections: **Changes** (as before), **History** (the last 20 commits and the tags) and **Branches & remotes** (the branch, or a detached HEAD or a merge or rebase in progress; the remotes and their URLs; the branch it tracks and how far ahead and behind it is; the other worktrees; and who commits are made as, this repository's and your global identity, with a warning when commits would fail or would show a personal email on GitHub). From there you can switch or create a branch, fetch, pull, push, tag, add a remote or change its URL, and set this repository's identity: these are yours alone, never an agent's. Hive never fetches by itself: ahead and behind are as of your last **Fetch**, with its time. Switching branch or pulling waits while an agent works in the project folder or a merge holds its merge slot, and says why; agents' worktrees are shown read-only. The status bar shows the selected project's git, such as **main · origin · ↑2 ↓0**: click it for Branches & remotes. A project without git says so, with how to set it up.

### Attachments
- **Attach any file to a prompt.** Drop a PDF, a log, a CSV or any other file onto an agent's or the Assistant's terminal, not only images: Hive pastes its path, copying it into the session's attachments first when it comes from outside the agent's folder (images are always kept). A chip over the terminal shows each attachment until you send the prompt, and says when the agent's CLI doesn't read that kind of file itself, so the agent opens it by its path (Claude Code reads PDFs itself; Codex and Copilot open them by path). Files over 20 MB are asked about first, and nothing attached is ever run.
- **Images is now Attachments.** The project tab and the Assistant view's section show every kind of file attached, with a filter by kind and previews of PDFs, Markdown, CSV and text in the viewer. Hive moves each project's `.hive/images` folder to `.hive/attachments` by itself, and old links to the images still open them. Storage and Clean Up… count and remove attachments.

### Developer features
- **See the text in the developer console.** **Settings → Advanced → Developer console: show prompt, reply and command text** (off by default) shows prompts, replies, commands and tools' arguments in the console's live view, under an **Unredacted** banner, and ticks text for new recordings. Tokens, API keys and sign-in details stay masked whatever it says, in the text too: everything after a credential's name (`--api-key`, `token=`, `password:`, `Authorization:`) is hidden to the end of that text, and device codes are masked, agents' `hive_console` stays redacted, and a recording with text is still never attached to Copy Diagnostics. Copy Diagnostics masks those in its log lines now too.
- **Show developer features** is now the first of the Advanced settings, in Settings → Advanced's list.
- **The developer console.** Turn on **Settings → Advanced → Show developer features**, then **Help → Developer Console** (Ctrl+Alt+J) opens a window of its own, like a browser's developer tools: pick an agent, the Hive Assistant or Hive itself and watch each hook, status line, launch and API call arrive beside what Hive made of it, redacted. Filter by event type or provider, search, pause and clear; Hive captures a source only while the console is open on it.
- **Record what a CLI sends.** The developer console's **Record** writes a source's events to a file, redacted unless you tick prompt and reply text for that recording, with the versions of Hive and your coding agents' CLIs. **Copy Diagnostics** can attach the newest redacted recording to a bug report.

### Advanced settings
- **Fix a CLI change without waiting for Hive.** **Settings → Advanced** now lists the details Hive relies on that a coding agent's update can break (each CLI's folder trust question, the text that means it's ready, the messages that mean its sign-in was refused, the fields Claude Code and Codex send plan limits in, Codex's title items) and timeouts for power users (each CLI's hook timeouts, the `hive` tools' reply timeout and Codex's timeout for them, the longest card and agent waits), plus how many lanes the Progress panel shows, which a workspace can set for itself. Search them, see which you changed with the default beside it, and **Reset**. They're kept in **advanced.json**, which you can also edit by hand: Hive reads it again when it changes. A value Hive can't use falls back to the default and says why. A value for a CLI records the CLI version it was made for and is flagged after that CLI updates, with **Keep**. **Reset all settings** resets them too. Copy Diagnostics lists what you changed (names only), and the Hive Assistant can explain and change them.
- **More of what a CLI update can break is in Settings → Advanced**: how Hive tells a safety classifier stopped an agent's response (Claude Code's transcript note and its question about switching models), what Claude Code's and Codex's screens show once a turn is done or while one runs, Codex's hint that it queues what you type, and the labels of Codex's `/permissions` menu.

### Files
- **A late paste no longer takes your selection.** In the Files tab, a paste, rename or new file that took a moment (a big folder, a slow disk) selected what it made when it finished, even if you had clicked another row meanwhile, so Ctrl+X or Ctrl+C then acted on the wrong file. The row you clicked now keeps the selection.
- **Deleting something open for a moment.** Delete Project, and every other delete to the Recycle Bin, failed at once when Windows couldn't move the item because something had a file in it open, even for an instant (an antivirus scan, the search indexer). Hive now tries again for about a second and a half, so only a program that keeps it open stops it, and Hive still says so.

### Agent API
- **Agents can read the developer console.** **Settings → Agent API → Let agents read the developer console** gives agents and the Hive Assistant a read-only tool, `hive_console`, showing what their CLI sends Hive (hooks, status line, transcript records, the terminal signs Hive reacts to) next to what Hive made of each. An agent reads only its own sessions; the Assistant reads its workspace's agents and Hive's own events (API calls, merge slots, watches, Progress). Hive captures only while the developer console is open, and what it captures is redacted: no prompt or reply text, commands, tokens or sign-in details. Off unless you turn it on.
- **Viewer tokens, for apps that show Hive.** An app that only shows what your agents are doing, such as Apiary, can now get a read-only **viewer token**: **Settings → Agent API → Viewer tokens** makes one, named for its app, that reads everything in your workspaces and follows its events but can't change anything (every change is refused); **Revoke** cuts that app off at once. Such an app finds a running Hive by itself from `%APPDATA%\Hive\agent-api-discovery.json`, which holds Hive's API address (never a token) while the Agent API is on.
- **More events, and the Progress panel's runs.** The event stream (`/v1/events`) now also sends progress runs, merge slots, Hive's notices, the Hive Assistant's actions and the agents it adds, so an app needn't poll for them. `GET /v1/progress` lists the Progress panel's runs. Nothing private goes out on the stream or in the runs: no prompts, messages, questions or replies, no CLI output, no command lines (their arguments can hold a token) and no notice text; a run's title goes out only when it was given on purpose (`hive-progress --title`), and a run titled by its command line is just "a command". An agent's state on the stream now says *that* a message waits for it or it asked something, not what.

## 0.6.0 — 11 October 2026

### MCP servers
- **Add MCP Server from File.** The MCP view adds a server from a `.json` definition or a `.zip` of a server's folder (its code and a `hive-mcp.json` definition), as the Skills view adds a skill from a file. Hive shows the name and what the server runs (its command and arguments or address, and its environment variables by name) before adding it, asks before replacing a server of the same name, and adds it turned off. Enabling any server now shows what it runs and asks first, and a server's view shows it too. Zips are unpacked safely: nothing in them runs, and paths outside the server's folder, links and oversized archives are refused.

### Agents
- **A kept worktree is used again.** Adding an agent with a new worktree named like an unused one (a new B4, with `hive/b4` kept from the last) now gives it that folder instead of making `hive/b4-2`, when it is merged and clean: Hive brings it up to main first, and runs the setup command again only if a lockfile changed. One that holds work is left alone, and Add Agent offers to reuse it with its work. The Agent API (`hive_add_agent`) does the same, can take an unused worktree by `existingWorktree`, and says which happened.
- **A project's agents as a table.** Hovering a project's tile in the compact sidebar, its agent count or status in the full one, or the status in the project's header shows its agents as a table: each one's state, its card (cut to one line) and what it waits for, a lane's builder beside its reviewer, instead of a wall of wrapped lines.
- **Hive offers to end what a stopped agent left running.** A preview server, a `tail -f` or a shell an agent started in the background could keep running long after the agent stopped, holding the project's folder ("in use", so it couldn't be renamed) or a port. Hive now keeps track of every process an agent's session starts, and when the agent stops with some still running, a notification lists them with **End Them** and **Keep Running**, and the agent's pane header shows **⚠ n left running** until you decide or they end. **Settings → Agents → Processes left running** can end them every time instead, and quitting Hive asks about any still listed. Hive only ever ends processes the agent started. That includes what an agent runs through the Microsoft Store's PowerShell 7 (Codex uses it where it is installed), whose processes Windows starts outside the session's tracking.

### Progress panel
- **Lanes: four by default, drag for more.** The lanes at the bottom of the Progress panel now show up to four (six took too much room from the runs) and scroll after that. Drag the line above **Lanes** up to show more, a whole lane at a time, or down for fewer; double-click it for four again, or use Up and Down on it from the keyboard. Each workspace keeps the height, folded or not and after a restart.
- **Honest run states.** A run queued for a test slot shows what it waits for and how long it has waited, never a time left ("about 0 s left" read as nearly done), with a still, hatched bar. A run past its estimate says "finishing", then "longer than expected". A command stopped with Ctrl+C shows as **Stopped**, and one whose `hive-progress` was ended with it (an agent stopping a preview server) as **Ended without a report** once its process is gone, instead of "stopped reporting".
- **Merges and lanes at a glance.** While an agent merges, a teal card at the top of the Progress panel says who is merging which cards, the branch going in (`hive/b1 → main`), the step its checks are on, how long so far and left, and who is waiting; it folds to one line. When builders and reviewers work through cards in lanes, the bottom of the panel shows each lane as a row: the pair, the card it is on, how many are finished, and a bar with a segment per card, coloured and patterned by where the card is (to do, doing, in review, passed, done, blocked or waiting for you, a failed review). Hover a segment for the card and its round; click it to open the card. Long lanes fold their finished cards into one "✓ n" segment, and the lanes fold down to their header. With no merge and no lanes, the panel is as before.

### Providers
- **Price tables follow the CLIs.** Each provider's **API prices** now lists the models its CLI has, so a model a CLI update adds shows without a Hive update, marked **No price: enter one** until you do; a priced model the CLI no longer lists stays, greyed, for older sessions. Claude Code (and Copilot) have a new **Session costs** choice: the CLI's own reported cost (the default, as before) or **My prices**, your table for every session. Codex's table says it is the only source of its estimates, with a link to OpenAI's pricing.
- **GitHub Copilot's Allow all needs no opt-in; Autopilot still does.** **Allow all** (approves every tool, path and web request without asking, in the agent's folder and outside it) is now offered like any other mode, with no red warning, so Copilot has a mode for working with few interruptions. **Autopilot** stays behind the opt-in, now **Settings → GitHub Copilot → Enable the Autopilot option**; if you had ticked it, Autopilot stays available. Checked with Copilot 1.0.93: once a turn has run in Autopilot, Copilot approves everything for the rest of the session, even after you switch out of it, and Hive's mode badge then shows Allow all. Autopilot's description and the user guide now say so.

### Lanes and loops
- **Card reports lead with a one-line summary.** Round reports and review verdicts start with one line (*"Round 2 ready: …"*, *"Round 1: FAILED: 2 findings: …"*) and the detail follows, and agents and the Assistant can read just those lines of a card's comments, so following a loop no longer means reading every long report in full.
- **The Assistant watches whole lanes.** Instead of a list of at most 20 cards it swapped in and out (and once missed a card waiting for review), the Assistant waits on every running lane at once, lanes started later included, and is woken only for what needs it: a failed review, a blocked or stalled card, a lane ready to merge, a lane ended. Routine moves no longer wake it.
- **The Assistant can leave a message for a busy agent.** Agents in a card loop are nearly always waiting or working, so the Assistant couldn't pass on your answers or re-brief them, and you pasted its messages into their panes yourself. Now it leaves the agent a message: one waiting on its cards gets it at once and keeps waiting afterwards, and a busy one gets it as soon as its turn ends. The agent's pane shows **✉ 1 message waiting** until then; hover to read it, click to cancel it.
- **Your card loop defaults are settings.** **Settings → Workspace → Card loop rounds** and **Card loop wait** set how many rounds a card gets and how long agents wait before telling you, for every loop in the workspace that doesn't say otherwise; a project can set its own in its **Settings → Card loops**. They start at 10 rounds and 6 hours (they were 5 and 2 hours), and what you say when you start a loop still wins.
- **Hive wakes reviewers and builders itself.** When a card arrives in Review, Hive wakes its reviewer; when the review gives its verdict, Hive wakes the card's builder, even when that agent didn't set a wait, so a loop no longer stalls because one agent forgot. It only does this for cards with a reviewer (the reviewer of the card's lane, or the agent that last reviewed it), only while the agent is idle, and never when the agent's own wait already tells it. The card's history says whom Hive woke. Turn it off for a project in its **Settings → Card loops**.
- **Hive knows the card loops it runs.** When two agents work through cards as builder and reviewer, the loop is kept as a **lane**: its builder and reviewer, its cards in order, its rounds and its wait. The builder registers it as it starts and the reviewer joins it, with nothing for you to set up. Hive follows the board from there: the lane is on the first card not yet passed, **paused** while that card is blocked, and **ended** once its cards are all Done. A lane whose builder or reviewer you remove is **abandoned**. The Assistant can list the lanes and start one when it briefs a pair, and scripts read them through the Agent API (`/v1/lanes`). A lane is only a record: an agent working on a card on its own has none, and a loop works the same without one.
- **Agents write tests that hold up on any machine.** The bundled work-on-card and review-agent-work skills now say that tests wait for what they expect to see, never for a length of time: no sleeping and then checking, no single read just after an action, no raised timeout as a fix, no test depending on another's leftovers. Reviewers fail a new or changed test that does, since it fails on a loaded or slower machine.
- **Reviewers don't repeat the builder's test runs.** In a card loop the builder's run record (which checks ran, on exactly which code) is what the reviewer checks, rather than running everything again: the bundled review-agent-work skill now has it run the quick checks, the tests the change affects and its own probes of the riskiest parts, and a whole suite again only when the record is missing, for other code or doubtful, or the change is to shared test code, saying why. Reviews take less time and fewer tokens, and don't load the machine while other agents test.
- **The Assistant runs a team out of the box.** In Coordinator mode, a new skill that comes with Hive, `run-lanes`, has the Assistant plan lanes by the files each touches, brief every builder and reviewer in full, and watch each lane to its end, checking the board whenever a lane changes and what agents report, instead of leaving lanes to "tell you". It does this when a project has a team (three or more agents, builder and reviewer pairs, or agents in worktrees); with one or two agents it stays light and at most suggests more structure in a line, and you can still use card loops and everything else. If it starts a lane without watching it, Hive's reply reminds it, and when a card wakes it while lanes are running, Hive reminds it to watch them all again.

### Fixes
- **Sizes and token counts read cleanly.** The Performance view showed an average under 1 KB unrounded ("160.17241379310346 B") and billions of tokens in millions ("12904M"). Sizes are now whole bytes ("160 B") and token counts have a billions unit ("12.9B"), everywhere Hive shows them; a value that would read "1000k" or "1024 KB" shows in the next unit ("1.0M", "1.0 MB").
- **Wakes are sent when Claude Code is busy for a moment.** About one wake line in fifty sat in an agent's or the Assistant's prompt unsent, even after Hive pressed Enter again, until you pressed Enter yourself. Claude Code, busy for a moment, read Hive's line together with the keys around it as a paste and kept those keys as text. Hive now puts its lines into Claude Code as a paste, which Claude Code reads as text however it arrives. A long line shows as *[Pasted text #1]* in the prompt until it is sent.
- **Board changes stay quick on a big board.** Each new card, move or batch change read every card file, one at a time, while other changes waited: on a board of 500 cards, a new card took about 0.2 s and a batch of 30 changes over 5 s. Placing a card now reads only the cards changed since, and the board is read several files at a time: about 0.1 s and 2 s.
- **A reviewer waiting for a card that came back to Review is woken.** A reviewer told a card was in Review, which then went back to Doing (blocked to ask you, say), and asked again to wait for it to be in Review, was never woken when it came back: on 10 Oct three reviewers sat waiting for five hours on cards already in Review. Hive now wakes them. A card that waits in Review for 15 minutes while its reviewer waits for it, without its review starting, now shows as **stalled** on the board and in its lane's row in the Progress panel, and an agent whose turn ended while its background tasks run (a long test run) is no longer reported as not watching its card.
- **Sessions are found when a folder has another name.** A project or worktree reached through a junction or symlink, or by an 8.3 short name (`C:\Users\RUNNER~1\…`), could lose its sessions: Claude Code files its transcripts under the folder's real path when started through a junction, and Codex and Copilot keep the name they were started with, so a session started by one name and looked for by another had no transcript, usage or entry in the Sessions list. Hive now finds a folder's sessions by where it is, whatever its name, and Repair, after a move, copies Claude Code's transcripts and memory from every name they were kept under.
- **Escape closes a new, empty card.** Pressing Escape just after opening the first New Card of a session could ask "Discard unsaved changes?" about a card with nothing in it. It closes the dialog now.
- **The Load template dialog shows the agents as a table.** Each new agent is a row with its coding agent, model, effort, mode and where it works, in the template's order. Settings left to the defaults say so in a muted *(default)*, and a new worktree shows its branch, with its folder on hover. Before, each agent was a long line that wrapped mid-path, which was hard to read for a template of 12 agents.
- **Save in a template's agent saves the template.** Editing a template, then pressing **Save** in one of its agents' settings, changed nothing until you also pressed **Save Template**, so the change was easy to lose. The agent's Save now saves the template, and the editor stays open. Other changes not saved yet are marked in the editor and in the list, and are kept if you go to another template and come back. Quitting or reloading asks about them, as it does for unsaved files.
- **A renamed project keeps its cards.** Renaming a project folder left its cards under the old name. They vanished from the project's board and **Archived**, and showed their agent as "(removed)". Repair moved the project but not its cards. Hive now names a project by an id kept in its own `.hive` folder, so cards, lanes and agents' watches follow a rename or a move, even one made while Hive runs, and watches also follow a move of the whole workspace. A merge left half done when Hive closed is reported in the project as it is now. A renamed project also keeps its handovers (so an agent picking up finds the latest one), its Performance history and kept comparisons, its Progress runs and its tabs' view settings. They also stay with their own project when two projects swap names or a new folder takes the old name. Cards saved before this version still name the old folder: for a project renamed since, the banner offers **Repair…**, which gives them to the project they belong to now. That is the project renamed from that name, or else the one whose agents worked on them.
- **A project renamed twice in quick succession keeps its settings.** If Hive failed once to carry a project's per-project settings and Progress runs over to a new folder name (a file in use, say), and you renamed the folder again before it tried again, the retry could act on the older name and record a rename back to it. The settings and runs then followed a folder that no longer existed. The retry now uses the folder names as they are now.
- **The board always hears that a project was renamed.** If Hive happened to read the projects part way through a rename, or a project folder was moved out of the workspace and back under another name, the open board kept showing its cards under the old name until something else made it read them again. It now updates.
- **A card started on an agent with extra arguments no longer sits idle.** When an agent's extra arguments ended with an option that takes a list, such as Claude Code's `--allowedTools mcp__hive Bash` or Codex's `--image a.png`, the CLI read the task Hive starts it on as one more value of that option: the agent opened at an empty prompt and the card stayed in Doing. Hive now passes the task after `--`, which ends the options. Extra arguments can no longer hold a bare `--` of their own: saving one is refused, saying why, and one saved before stops the agent from starting until you remove it. An option whose value is `--` is written `--option=--`.
- **A GitHub Copilot agent no longer shows Ready while it works on a new conversation's first prompt.** When you refused one of Copilot's prompts and then started another conversation (`/new`, `/clear`, `/resume`) and prompted straight away, the old conversation's end could be read after the new prompt, so the agent showed Ready while it worked. The old conversation's end now counts as older than the prompt that left it.
- **The Assistant at Look and advise can list the card loops.** Asking it *"which lanes are running?"* needed Control agents, though it only reads them; it now answers at every level (starting or ending a lane still needs Control agents).
- **A blocked card in a loop is reviewed when it comes back.** When a builder blocked its card to ask you a question, its reviewer dropped the card and moved on to the next one, so once you'd answered and the card was back in Review, nobody reviewed it and the loop stalled. A blocked card now stays in its loop, paused: the reviewer keeps waiting for it and reviews it when it returns.
- **Hive warns of a stray `.codex` folder.** A `.codex` folder an agent left scratch files in could stop every Codex agent's commands in that project ("setup refresh had errors"). Hive now warns when a project or worktree has a `.codex` folder holding something other than Codex's configuration (its config, custom agents, rules), and agents are told to keep scratch, probes and logs in a temp folder, out of projects and their `.codex`, `.claude` and `.agents` folders.
- **A restart no longer switches the Assistant to Codex.** Just after Hive started, the default provider (Automatic) could pick Codex for a moment when Codex answered before Claude Code had been found, so resuming the Assistant's Claude Code conversation was refused ("the Assistant now runs Codex") until Hive had finished looking. Automatic now waits until Hive knows which CLIs are installed (Settings shows **Automatic (checking…)** meanwhile), and a conversation always resumes in the provider it ran in, in the Assistant or in an agent whose provider you've changed since: a provider setting is for new conversations.
- **The Progress panel's lanes show what is happening now.** A merged or abandoned lane is greyed and still, and shows its cards as they were when it stopped, so a card another lane has taken on no longer looks worked in two lanes at once. Running lanes are listed first, six fit before the list scrolls, and a lane shows the card its builder is actually working on: one waiting for your answer no longer reads as paused once the builder has moved on to the next card.
- **One fewer third-party program in the installed app.** Hive no longer ships `resources/elevate.exe`, a helper the installer builder adds for updates that need administrator rights. Hive installs per user and its updates never need them. Its notice is gone from the third-party notices.
- **Open file in the Changes tab works for every worktree.** For an unused worktree outside the workspace (one made with `git worktree add` elsewhere), **Open file** above its diff did nothing but report an error. It now opens that worktree's copy of the file, as it does for agents' worktrees and the project folder.
- **The Assistant moves a whole set of cards in one go.** Moving or labelling many cards in one step (up to 100) counted each card toward the Assistant's 30 changes for a message, so moving 37 cards to the next column stopped part-way and asked to go on, which was easy to miss. The step now counts as one change, however many cards it has; each card is still listed on its own in **Done by the Assistant**.
- **The same folder by another name is the same folder.** A workspace reached through a junction or a short (8.3) name, such as `C:\Users\RUNNER~1\…`, could be opened in a second window while it was open in the first, and a workspace reopened by another name looked moved, offering **Repair…**. Removing a project could pass a worktree that is another project of the workspace when its path was written another way, and resuming a session was refused ("This session ran in …") when its worktree's path had changed form. An Agent API call naming its workspace by another name in the `X-Hive-Workspace` header wasn't found, and a locked file could be named as `..\..\RUNNER~1\…` rather than by its path in the agent's folder. Hive now compares these by where the folders really are.
- **Your answer reaches an agent that asked before starting its card.** An agent with a question about a card still in Todo could ask you and leave the card there with nobody on it, so the decision you recorded for it was nobody's and Hive didn't tell the agent. Agents now take the card (to Doing, themselves on it) before they ask, so Hive tells them of your decision as it does for a card they're working on.
- **Copy Diagnostics leaves out where Claude Code and Codex were looked for.** When a Claude Code or Codex Hive tried didn't report its version, the log line kept the folder it was in and what it printed instead, so a folder with a space in its name, or a private name in the message, could reach a bug report. Both are now left out, as GitHub Copilot's already were; the line still says a copy didn't report its version.

## 0.5.1 — 9 October 2026

### Fixes
- **Copilot panes are drawn at the right width from the start.** A GitHub Copilot agent's screen could load garbled (its input box and "Loading" lines drawn at about two thirds of the pane's width, several frames stacked, stray characters at the old edge) until you resized the window, and a mode switch could do the same. Each agent's terminal now starts at its process's size, a restarted agent starts at its pane's size, and Hive has Copilot redraw at its size once it has started and once it is ready, as a resize by hand did. Claude Code and Codex agents get the first two as well.
- **Copilot's "Manual Approval" in Accept edits is explained.** Copilot's own footer still says Manual Approval in Accept edits, since Hive approves the edits for it; the mode badge's tooltip and the user guide now say so, so it doesn't look as if the mode didn't take.

## 0.5.0 — 9 October 2026

### Hive Desktop
- **Hive is now called Hive Desktop.** The window title, About, the status bar, notifications, the tray, the installer, the Start menu and desktop shortcuts and the entry in **Installed apps** say **Hive Desktop** (in sentences, Hive is still Hive). Nothing you have moves: the update renames the shortcuts, and Hive stays installed where it was, as `Hive.exe`, with your settings and conversations in `%APPDATA%\Hive`; taskbar pins and notification settings carry over.

### Providers
- **New agents use the coding agent you have installed.** **Default provider** (Settings → Providers) now starts as **Automatic**: new agents use the first provider that is turned on and installed (Claude Code, then Codex, then GitHub Copilot). If you only use Codex or Copilot, an empty project no longer says Claude Code is missing, and **New Session** adds an agent with the CLI you have in one click. Hive warns only when no coding agent is installed, or when a provider you chose or an agent uses isn't: a provider you turned on but don't use no longer brings a banner or opens Agent Setup. If the default you chose is turned off, **Add Agent** says so instead of quietly picking another. If your default was Claude Code, it becomes Automatic, which still picks Claude Code wherever it is turned on and installed; a Codex or Copilot default stays. Agents you already have keep their provider.
- **GitHub Copilot agents.** Agents can now run on the GitHub Copilot CLI (`copilot`), beside Claude Code and Codex: turn it on in **Settings → GitHub Copilot**, then choose it when you add an agent. They get what the others get: statuses from Copilot's hooks (working, asking for permission, asking a question, finished), file locks (an edit of a file another agent is changing is refused, or handed to Copilot's own approval dialog in **Ask me**), the hive tools and Hive's guidance, the workspace's skills, sessions Hive names and resumes, and the mode in its footer (**Accept edits**, the default, **Ask**, **Plan**, and **Autopilot** and **Allow all** when turned on; Shift+Tab switches to Plan and Autopilot and back while it runs). Tokens other tools leave in your environment (`GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`) are kept from it, so it uses its own login, or `COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN` if you set one for it; the first start in a folder asks whether you trust it, in its terminal. Copilot Free runs the **Auto** model only; the account's models are read from the CLI. **Stop** ends a Copilot agent with Ctrl+C, as you would, so Copilot keeps the session's token totals: it takes a second or two, and Hive ends it after a few seconds if it doesn't exit.
- **Hive's own provider badges.** Claude Code, Codex and GitHub Copilot are now shown with badges of Hive's own instead of their makers' logos: a coral circle with a tilde for Claude Code, a teal rounded square with braces for Codex, and a violet diamond with a double chevron for GitHub Copilot, always beside the provider's name (or as its tooltip). On the status bar they're drawn in its colour, and their outlines tell them apart. About, the README and the user guide say Hive Desktop is independently developed and isn't affiliated with, endorsed or sponsored by Anthropic, OpenAI or GitHub.
- **Agent Setup for GitHub Copilot.** Its tab finds the Copilot CLI (installed with WinGet or npm), shows its version, and installs or updates it with the same installer. It says who Copilot is signed in as and how: a `COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN` you set, its own `copilot login`, or the GitHub CLI's login, which Copilot falls back to. Signed out, **Sign in** opens `copilot login` in a terminal for you to sign in. Hive reads only account names, never a token. A note says Copilot Free uses Auto only; choosing a model needs a paid plan. The user guide's new **GitHub Copilot** section covers installing, signing in, AI credits, and what Copilot Pro's "third-party agents" are (cloud agents, not Hive's Claude Code or Codex).

### Worktrees
- **The Changes tab leads with your unused worktrees.** Its picker now starts with **Unused worktrees**, then the worktrees agents work in, then the project folder, last. A project with worktrees opens on Unused worktrees; one without opens on its folder. Each unused worktree says who made it: **was B4** (the agent that last worked there), **made by Hive**, or **not made by Hive** (one an agent made itself with `git worktree add`, say). Listing them takes a few seconds, so the page shows a spinner and says what Hive is doing the first time, then shows the last list at once while it checks again, with **Refresh** to look again. Each page says in a sentence what it shows. Agents are now told to make no git worktree of their own to work in unless you ask (Hive's locks, Changes and merging follow the folder Hive gave them), and to tidy a subagent's worktree before they finish.

### Fixes
- **A CLI you install from Agent Setup is found straight away.** Installers add their folder to your PATH in Windows, which programs already running don't see, so Hive didn't find GitHub Copilot (or another CLI) it had just installed until you restarted Hive. Now, whenever Hive looks for the CLIs (when it starts, when an install or update ends, and with **Check again**), it also reads the PATH Windows gives new programs and adds its new folders to its own. Agent Setup shows the CLI installed, and agents you start from then on can use it, without restarting Hive (the installer's advice to restart your terminal is for terminals already open). The same goes for Git: install it, then **Check again**.
- **Copilot's danger setting names both modes it unlocks.** **Settings → GitHub Copilot** called it "Enable the Autopilot option", though it also unlocks **Allow all**. It now reads **Enable the Autopilot and Allow all options**, with its description and confirmation to match, and turning it off moves projects and agents using either mode back to Inherit (Allow all ones used to keep it). Claude Code's and Codex's read as before.
- **Refusing Copilot's path prompt no longer leaves the agent waiting.** When a GitHub Copilot agent asked for a path outside its folder ("Path permission needed") and you pressed **Esc**, Copilot ended the turn without telling Hive, so the agent showed **waiting** until your next prompt. Hive now reads the turn's end from Copilot's session and shows the agent ready.
- **A Codex agent you signed in again for stays working.** When Codex refused a turn for an expired sign-in and you sent the next prompt just as Hive was reading that refusal from Codex's session, the late read could still mark the working agent **Needs sign-in**. A refusal read before a newer prompt or other hook no longer overrides it.
- **Codex's sandbox setup and permission switches no longer stall on a busy computer.** Hive types `/permissions` into Codex for **Set up** in Agent Setup and when you switch a running Codex agent's preset. If Codex was busy just then, the command could stay in its input, never run: the setup prompt never came up, or the switch was undone after a while. Hive now also waits while Codex's screen shows it is busy holding what was typed ("tab to queue message"), and if Codex still holds the command once it is free, presses Enter again (twice more at most). A command Codex has taken is never sent twice, and if Codex stays busy, asks you something or stops meanwhile, Hive types nothing more and says the switch wasn't made.
- **Agent Setup's chips line up.** The **Signed in** chip's account icon was drawn at full size, so it stood taller and sat higher than the chips beside it on the Claude Code and Codex tabs. The chips now share one height and centre line, and wrap neatly. The **Git for Windows** and **Install** buttons' icons are back to button size too.

## 0.4.1 — 8 October 2026

### Task board
- **Your answers reach an agent waiting for you.** When an agent asks you something and its turn ends, and you answer through the Assistant (which records your answer as decisions on the card), the agent used to hear nothing until you pasted the answer into its pane. Now Hive types one line into it once nothing is in the way (*"#392 has 2 new decisions from the user: read them and carry on"*): decisions recorded together come in one line, never while it works or you type in its terminal, and the card's history says whom Hive told.
- **Save keeps a decision you're writing.** In a card, **Save** used to post a comment you were writing but drop a decision you were writing. It now records both (and a change you were making to a decision). **Add Decision** and **Comment** are highlighted while their box has text, and say that Save adds them too.

### Models
- **Claude Haiku 5.5** (released 7 October 2026) is priced: $0.10 per million input tokens and $0.50 per million output (prompts up to 100,000 tokens; longer prompts cost more and aren't estimated yet). Its cache prices follow Anthropic's pricing page. The **haiku** alias now means Haiku 5.5, as Claude Code's does, and Haiku 5.5 is in the pinned models; **Haiku 4.5** stays in the older versions, and its sessions keep their price.

### Skills
- **A card loop moves on when its card is taken away.** If a card an agent is waiting on in a card loop is given to another agent, reviewed by another, or blocked (for your decision, say), the agent is told once and carries on with its next card, instead of waiting until you cancel its wait by hand. A wait on several cards keeps the others, and the card's history says whose wait ended.
- **A card loop never waits for what has already happened.** A builder that started waiting for its card to reach Passed after the card was already there waited until its wait ran out (six hours, once), holding up the merge. A wait for a card to reach a column it's already in now ends at once. A reviewer waiting for a failed card to come back asks for the card's next move into Review (`fresh` in the Agent API), so it still waits while the card sits in Review.

### Hive Assistant
- **A tidier Assistant panel.** The header no longer repeats the status with a dot: the **Status** line under it says it, after the Assistant's own icon (a hive cell with a crown), in the status's colour with an agent's status dot on it. The same icon marks the Assistant on the panel's folded strip, its runs in the Progress panel, the inbox and the Workspace Overview. The cards it waits for are the same coloured card chips as everywhere else (click one to open the card). **Done by the Assistant** now sits between the status and the workspace, with the same last 3, Show all and fold. Each project in the workspace list folds to one line (its name, the dot of the agent that most needs you, and how many agents run), and stays folded for that workspace. Each agent in the list has a faint backing in its status's colour, a soft wash while it works and a light hatch while it waits on cards or for you, so you can see at a glance which ones are busy.
- **Several cards in one change.** The Assistant can move or update up to 100 cards in one step: the same column, position, blocked, labels or agent on each. Each card is checked and changed on its own, with its own history line, and one it can't change (in Doing with another agent, say) is left as it was while the rest change. Its 30 changes per message count each card; a batch that doesn't fit changes none of them, and says how many would fit. Agents can batch the cards of their own project too.
- **The Assistant waits on agents without checking in.** Asked to tell you when an agent is done with work that has no card (a build, a test run, a push and its CI), the Assistant used to check on it every few minutes, each check costing context and tokens. Now it starts an **agent watch** and stops: Hive wakes it with one line when the agent finishes, needs you, or stops (*"B6 (hive) finished: "The installer is built…""*), and its status line says **Waiting for B6 to finish** meanwhile. Agents can watch another agent the same way (`hive_wait_for_agents` with `wake`).
- **The Assistant knows you by name.** When Hive tells the Assistant you replied to an agent in its pane, the line now says *"User replied to B1…"*, or uses the name you set in **Settings → General → Your name** (*"Darren replied to B1…"*). The name goes into the Assistant's conversation only if you set one.
- **The Assistant hears when you answer an agent yourself.** When you reply to an agent in its pane while the Assistant is watching it (or its card), the Assistant is told the first line of what you sent, so it doesn't ask you again and can record your decision on the card. **Settings → Assistant → Tell the Assistant when I reply to an agent** turns it off.
- **Each new lane in a new conversation.** The Assistant can give an idle agent its next task in a new conversation in one step: its current conversation ends (it stays in the Sessions tab) and a new one starts on the task, with no handover. It does this when it starts an agent's next set of cards in a card loop, and keeps the same conversation within one. Shorter conversations cost much less, since every request re-reads the whole conversation. It is refused, as other tasks are, while the agent is working, starting, waiting on its tests, waiting for you or watching a card, or just after you typed in it.

### Hive
