- **A clear (×) button in every search and filter box.** The board's search, Settings, the Files and Sessions tabs, the sidebar's filters, tables' filters, Tips, Keyboard Shortcuts and the command palette show an × while they have text: click it, or press Escape, to clear the box. In a dialog or the palette, the first Escape clears the box and the next closes it.
- **Easier-to-read secondary text.** Timestamps, counts, card numbers, hints and other grey text, and amber text and icons, now meet the WCAG AA contrast level (4.5:1) on every background in both themes, hovered and selected rows included. Status dots meet 3:1, and a pulsing dot now changes shade instead of fading. Most of the change is in the light theme, where faint text was hard to read.

### Worktrees
- **Unused worktrees live in the Changes tab.** Worktrees no agent uses now (kept when you removed their agent, or from another template) are listed in the Changes tab's folder picker, under **Unused worktrees**, next to the agents' worktrees. **All unused worktrees** has the same Remove, Remove all merged, Remove anyway… and Give to an agent… as before, and **Open** shows one's changes like an agent's worktree, with **Merge** to bring its work onto main (and remove it afterwards, if you like). The project Overview keeps a one-line count with a link.

### Terminal
- **Card numbers are links.** When an agent or the Assistant mentions a card in its terminal, like "#383 is back On Hold", hover the number to see the card's title and column, and Ctrl+click it to open the card, as you would a file path. Only numbers of cards on your board are linked.

### Fixes
- **The command palette's highlighted command shows its shortcut.** In the light theme the shortcut of the highlighted command was white on white, and in the dark theme hard to read; it now reads clearly on the highlight in both.
- **A wake that isn't taken no longer sits unnoticed.** Sometimes a CLI (seen with Claude Code, the Assistant's too) isn't reading its input when Hive types a wake into it, and the line sits in its prompt. Hive already pressed Enter once more; now, if it still isn't taken, the agent's pane (or the Assistant's status line) says **A line Hive typed wasn't taken** and you're told once, so you can click its terminal and press Enter. Hive types nothing more into it until then, so a later task isn't lost behind it, and the log says when each wake was taken.
- **Sonnet 5.5's cache reads are priced at $0.10 per million tokens**, Anthropic's published price (5% of input), not $0.20. Estimated costs of Sonnet 5.5 sessions that Hive prices itself come out lower where most of the input is cached. A price you set for Sonnet 5.5 in Settings → Claude Code → API prices is kept.
- **Hive opens when its install folder's permissions don't cover sandboxed apps.** If the folder Hive is installed in had an entry for a Windows app package but none for **ALL APPLICATION PACKAGES**, Windows denied Hive's sandboxed windows access to it and Hive closed at start with nothing on screen. The installer and updates now give ALL APPLICATION PACKAGES read access to the folder, and Hive checks at start: it adds the entry to a folder you can change, and for one you can't, it says so with the command to run and a **Copy Command** button instead of failing silently.

## 0.4.0 — 7 October 2026

### Hive Assistant
- **The Assistant's status, at a glance.** A line under the panel's header says what it is doing: **Status: Working**, **Idle**, **Waiting for you** (highlighted), or the cards it is watching and until when, each card a link that opens it.
- **"Done by the Assistant" moved under the terminal.** It shows the last 3 changes at a fixed height; **Show all** scrolls through every one in the same space instead of pushing the terminal around, and clicking its title folds it away (remembered for the workspace).
- **New Mode… no longer opens again after Enter.** Pressing Enter to create a mode (or in any dialog opened from a button that you confirm with Enter) left a new, empty dialog open, because the same key press reached the button that opened it.
- **A resumed Assistant uses its current mode.** Resuming the Assistant's conversation kept the instructions it started with: Claude Code sends a conversation's first system prompt again on every resume, so a mode chosen while the Assistant was stopped, **Restart in This Mode…**, or a Hive update's new instructions didn't reach it. Hive now resumes it with its current mode's instructions, and when you switched mode while it wasn't running, tells it the new mode in its conversation once it is ready, as a switch while it runs does.
- **See the images you've pasted to the Assistant.** The Assistant view (the robot in the activity bar) now has **Modes**, **Conversations** and **Images**. **All Images** shows every screenshot you've pasted or dropped into its conversations, grouped by conversation, with the same viewer as a project's Images tab; click a conversation's name to read it, or move a whole conversation's images to the Recycle Bin at once. Its conversations use the new Sessions tree. Also in the command palette: **Show Assistant Images**.
- **The Images views say what they hold.** A line under the heading of a project's **Images** tab and the Assistant's **All Images** says whose images they are: those pasted or dropped into the project's agents' sessions, or into the Assistant.
- **The Assistant's workspace overview lists each project's agents under its name**, with a small indent, however wide the panel is. They used to start a third of the way across, drifting right as the panel widened.
- **The Assistant's personas are now working modes.** **Coordinator** (the default) runs the work across your agents, **Planner** shapes work into questions, lanes and cards, **QA triager** turns a bug report into a card with evidence, and **Release manager** takes a release through its checklist. They replace the character personas: an Assistant that used Overseer, Orchestrator or Reviewer moves to the closest mode, and personas you wrote or edited stay. Switching mode from the panel's header now takes effect at once and keeps the conversation (Hive tells the Assistant the new mode), instead of starting a new one; **Restart in This Mode…** resumes the conversation with the mode's full instructions. The Assistant may suggest another mode when your request fits it, but never switches by itself.
- **Ask the Assistant about Hive's settings.** It explains any setting (what it does, when it helps, where it is in Settings) and suggests one when it spots a problem a setting would fix, such as a very long transcript or agents editing the same files. With **Settings → Assistant → Control → Change settings** on (off by default), it also makes the change when you ask or agree, and lists it in its panel with the old and new value and a **Revert** button. Permission modes, the Agent API, what Hive runs and its own Control stay yours.
- **The Assistant suggests compacting too.** Like an agent's, the Assistant's **Compact** button and the context in its footer turn orange once its conversation passes a threshold: **Settings → Assistant → Highlight Compact over**, 500,000 tokens by default (higher than your agents' 200,000, since the Assistant usually works with a bigger context), or **Never**.
- **Start New (All) no longer blanks the Assistant.** Starting fresh sessions for many agents at once could turn the Assistant's panel black for a few seconds, after which its terminal drew a little wider than the panel until you resized it. Hive now gives back each ended terminal's graphics context straight away, so the Assistant keeps its own, and a terminal that loses one anyway is fitted to its pane again.
- **The Assistant's panel can go on the left.** If Windows notifications or other apps cover the right of the screen, **View → Move Assistant Panel to the Left** (also in the command palette and the panel's ⋯ menu) or **Settings → Assistant → Panel side** puts it between the project list and your work. It folds and resizes on that side, and the choice is kept for every workspace and after a restart.

### Skills
- **Agents take turns to merge: the merge slot.** When several agents finished close together, one agent's merge could land while another was still running its checks, and that one had to merge main in and run them all again. Now an agent claims the project's **merge slot** before its final merge (merge main in, check, merge) and releases it after; others wait in order, with "Waiting for the merge slot (B4 is merging #305)" on their status, so each agent's checks run once. Who holds it and who waits shows in the project's **Overview** and the **Progress panel**, where **Release** frees a stuck holder. A hold ends with its agent's session and after 60 minutes without progress (Hive tells you), and if Hive closes while an agent holds it, Hive says so when the workspace opens again. Your merges from the **Merge** dialog wait for it too. The **merge-ready** skill uses it, and scripts can read it through the Agent API.
- **Agents no longer lose each other's changes to a shared note.** Two agents that read a note, edited their own part and wrote it back could each drop the other's change. Reading a note now gives its revision, and an agent rewriting it names that revision: if the note changed meanwhile, the write is refused and the agent reads it again and merges. The **workspace-note** skill also has agents append dated, signed entries to busy log-like notes instead of rewriting them. Scripts using the Agent API can pass `expectedRevision` too (Agent API reference).
- **Agents merge only what they checked.** When several agents merge into the same branch, one could merge work checked against an older main, so main held a combination nobody had tested. The **merge-ready** skill now has agents note main's commit when they start their checks, compare it just before merging, and if another branch was merged meanwhile, merge main in again and rerun the checks first. It also says to wait and retry when another merge holds git's lock, never deleting it. **card-loop** points to it.
- **A project's Skills tab shows one provider at a time.** Pick Claude Code or Codex from a dropdown (it starts on the project's default provider); its skills sit folded under the Hive skills, and Hive remembers, for each project, the provider you picked and what you left open. The list is much shorter.

### Hive
- **A card loop can't stall unnoticed.** On 7 Oct a reviewer's wake line was typed into Codex but never sent, so it sat idle for hours while the builder waited on it. Hive now checks that a wake is taken and presses Enter again if it isn't. An agent in a card loop that ends up idle with no wait gets its card marked as stalled ("Codex isn't watching #333"), and you're told once. The card-loop skill also has agents check that their wait started before they end their turn.
- **Long text stays on the card.** A card's blocked reason with a long hash or path ran off the card's edge on the board and was cut off. It now wraps, shows its first three lines on the card, and all of it when you hover it or open the card, where the Blocked field wraps too. Long labels, a reviewer's or agent's long name, long titles in the Board's Stalled list and long words in tooltips stay inside their boxes as well.
- **Long waits through Hive's tools no longer fail after five minutes.** An agent waiting on cards (`hive_wait_for_tasks` without wake, up to 840 s) or on other agents (`hive_wait_for_agents`, up to 600 s) got an error after about 300 s instead of its reply. Waits now last as long as asked.
- **Hive tells you when files in a project's `.hive` are already committed to git.** Excluding `.hive` only keeps new files out, so files committed before (with `git add -A`, say) stayed tracked while Hive reported `.hive` as excluded. The project's Overview and Project Settings now say how many, with the command that fixes it (`git rm -r --cached .hive`, then commit), and **Untrack…** runs it for you after listing the files.
- **Tidy the worktrees no agent uses.** A worktree you keep when you remove its agent now shows up in the project's **Overview** under **Unused worktrees**, with its branch, last commit and size, and whether it is merged into main and clean. **Remove** deletes a merged, clean one with its branch (or **Remove all merged**); one that still holds work says what ("3 commits not on main · 2 changed files") and offers **Give to an agent…** or **Remove anyway…**, which names exactly what goes. Hive mentions kept worktrees after you remove an agent or delete a template whose agents used them, the Changes tab says when one holds unmerged work, Storage counts their size, and the Assistant can see how many there are (only you can remove them).
- **Worktrees reached by another name are recognised.** When Hive knew a workspace or a worktree by its short (8.3) name, such as `C:\Users\RUNNER~1\…`, or through a junction or symbolic link, it thought git didn't list the worktree, because git names worktrees by their full, real path: removing merged worktrees (**Remove All**, a template's load, **Unused worktrees**) refused every one, and loading a template again made new worktrees instead of working in its agents' old ones. Hive now compares the real folders; one it can't resolve is still never removed.
- **Hive tells you when git is missing.** **Help → Agent Setup…** now has a **Git** row with the version Hive found, or **Git not found** with how to fix it, and says what doesn't work with a git older than 2.38. When Hive can't run git (not installed, or not on its PATH), Add Agent, the Changes tab, the Merge dialog, Remove All and template loads say so, and the status bar shows **Git** in red, instead of saying the project isn't a git repository. A git command that fails no longer counts as "nothing to merge" or clean: Hive keeps any worktree or branch it can't check.
- **The card chip takes less room.** An agent's pane header now shows only the card's icon and number (`#4`, `+1` with more), as its tab in the agent strip does, at every width; hover it for the titles. The Assistant's workspace list shows the same chip beside each agent, with what it's doing: its test or build progress, background tasks or the cards it's watching.
- **The Progress panel keeps a longer history.** With many agents, a test run or build scrolled off **Recent** within minutes. Recent still shows the last 10 runs, and **Show all** now scrolls through the last 200 in the same space, without moving the rest of the panel; **Show fewer** folds it back, and each workspace remembers which. The project filter applies to them all, and Recent is still there after Hive restarts.
- **Showing an agent no longer takes the keyboard back.** After **Show the agent** in the Progress panel, a key press or click you make before its terminal appears now keeps the keyboard where you put it. Before, the terminal could take it a moment later, so Escape went to the agent instead of closing the run's details.
- **Tables work the same everywhere.** The keyboard shortcuts, Performance's and Compare's tables, Storage (with Clean Up…'s preview and every project in Settings → Workspace) and the Overviews' tables by project and by agent now sort by any column, filter and page like the compaction history, and remember each table's sort and rows per page.
- **Move any dialog out of the way.** Like an open card, every dialog (Add Agent, Agent Settings, Merge, questions…) can now be dragged by its header to see what's behind it; it stays inside the window, Escape while dragging puts it back, and it opens in the middle again next time. A question over a dialog moves on its own, and Escape now closes only the question. The image viewers stay put.

### Workspaces
- **See whether antivirus scanning slows your workspace.** On Windows, **Settings → Workspace → Antivirus scanning** shows whether Microsoft Defender scans the workspace, its worktrees and (for people developing Hive) its test area, and whether each is on a Dev Drive. With your consent, and one administrator prompt, Hive adds exclusions for exactly those folders, or removes the ones it added. Its confirmation lists the folders one per row, long paths wrapping, with a button to copy them all. It suggests this when it matters (several agents at once, or a long build or test run), and if you close the suggestion it reminds you at most once a day, until the folders are excluded or you say **Don't Ask Again**.
- **A deleted worktree comes back.** Starting an agent whose worktree folder is gone (deleted after a merge, or not copied to a new computer) offers to recreate the worktree on its branch, with its commits, and runs the project's setup command as for a new one. If the branch is gone too, it offers a new worktree, or to unlink it.
- **Move a workspace and keep its agents working.** When you open a workspace from a new folder (moved in Explorer, or to another drive), Hive says so in a banner and **Repair…** fixes what still points to the old folder: agents' worktrees (relinked with git, found whether you moved them along or left them), the folders sessions ran in, and Claude Code's conversations and memory, copied to the folder for the new path so agents keep what they remembered. It shows what it will do first, waits until agents are stopped, never deletes or overwrites anything, and running it again repeats nothing. A worktree it can't find can be recreated on its branch, located or unlinked; a file it couldn't copy is retried until it is. Open Recent lists the new folder, and the projects you were working on stay marked.
- **Tidy the recent workspaces.** In **File → Open Recent** and on the welcome page, remove a workspace from the list with its **✕** or right-click → **Remove from Recent**, or forget them all with **Clear Recently Opened…** (it asks first, and keeps the workspaces you have open). A workspace whose folder was moved or deleted is greyed with *not found*, and opening it offers to remove it instead of failing; one open in another window says so.

### Coding agents
- **See which Claude Code and Codex versions Hive was tested with.** Each release now records the CLI versions its tests ran against. **Help → Agent Setup** shows them next to the versions you have (**Tested with 2.1.287 · installed 2.1.290**), with a calm note when yours is newer or older, and **Copy Diagnostics** includes them for bug reports.
- **Hive's hook keys stay out of your projects.** The key Claude Code's and Codex's hooks use to report an agent's status to Hive was written into each project's `.hive` launch settings (and into Codex's command line), where a commit, a sync service or a zip could carry it. Hooks now read it from a file in Hive's own data folder, each session has its own key, and a key stops working when its session ends. Each Claude Code session's settings and MCP server configuration, which can carry your MCP servers' keys or a `--settings` file's `env`, moved there too, out of the project.
- **Hive tells you when a project's `.hive` isn't kept out of version control.** Hive adds `.hive` to git's exclude file by itself, now also for a repository made later (`git init`) or one in a folder above the project, and asks git whether it really ignores it. Where it can't (no git, another version control system, a OneDrive or Dropbox folder), the project's Overview and Project Settings say so, with **Exclude** where Hive can fix it.
- **Hive tells you when a sign-in expires.** When Claude Code's or Codex's sign-in expired, its agents' turns stopped but Hive kept showing them as working, so card loops sat stuck until someone noticed. Those agents now show **Needs sign-in** (a violet dot), you're told once for the CLI however many agents stopped, and the tooltip says how to sign in again. Once you have, **Resume All Agents** in the project header carries on the agents that didn't carry on by themselves. A Claude Code turn that ends on another API error (a rate limit, say) now ends as finished too, instead of staying working.
- **A `--settings` in Claude Code's extra arguments no longer switches off Hive's hooks.** Claude Code reads only one `--settings`, so adding your own replaced the one Hive starts it with: the agent's status, file locks and status line (context, cost, plan limits) stopped working. Hive now merges your settings into its own, so both apply. A `--settings` with no value, or settings that are missing, aren't a file of at most 2 MiB, aren't JSON, or would turn off Hive's hooks (`disableAllHooks`), stop the agent from starting, with a message saying which.
- **The context tooltip says where Claude Code compacts by itself, and why.** "Compacts by itself at about 167,000" is marked as Claude Code's default for the window, and when its settings change it (an auto-compact window in its settings or for one model, `--autocompact`, `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW`, also in a settings file's `env` block, an earlier percentage, or a `--settings` file or JSON in an agent's extra arguments) the tooltip gives that figure and names the setting, or says auto-compaction is off. Hive only reads those settings, when the session starts, and only the files Claude Code reads (`--setting-sources`, `--restricted`, its 2 MiB limit). A default is called an estimate, since managed policies Hive can't read could change it, and so is any answer when Hive couldn't read a settings file or can't tell which ones Claude Code reads.
- **Codex's sandbox setup brings up its prompt on a busy computer too.** Agent Setup's **Set up** for the Codex sandbox could type into Codex while it was still loading, so Codex queued the keys and its setup prompt never appeared. Hive now waits until Codex has loaded.
- **Restart session restarts on a busy computer too.** Clicking **Restart session** (after changing an agent's effort, skills or settings) could, on a busy computer, ask whether to stop a session that had already stopped (*…already has a running session*) instead of restarting it. It now restarts straight away.
- **A Codex approval prompt is told once.** On a busy computer, Hive could tell you twice that a Codex agent needed your input for the same approval prompt (the first one in a new session). It's told once now.
- **Footers show the model that actually runs.** An agent or the Assistant set to **Opus** now reads *Opus 5.5*, like an agent on the default (*Opus 5.5 (default)*): the running session's own model, else the model the alias stands for, with *chosen as Opus* in the tooltip. The model pickers list aliases with their model, *Opus (Opus 5.5)*, as Claude Code reports them.
- **New models show up without waiting for a Hive update.** Hive now asks Claude Code and Codex which models they have and what each can do, for your installed version and your account (no tokens, no session), so the model pickers list exactly what the CLI reports: Fable, GPT-6.1 Sol, or whatever your plan adds next. **Effort pickers offer only the chosen model's levels** (Haiku has none, Opus 4.6 stops at Max, GPT-5.5 at Extra high); an effort a model doesn't take stays chosen and is marked, never changed for you. With no effort chosen, the agent's footer shows the model's own default, e.g. *Medium (default)*. The Auto warning for Haiku now comes from what Claude Code says.
- **Every fallback is yours to edit.** When a CLI can't be asked, **Settings → <provider> → Models (fallback)** and **Effort levels (fallback)** are used, and you can add, remove and rename entries, with **Reset to defaults**. The **API prices** table now takes new models and removals too, with **Reset to defaults**, and says when Hive's prices were checked.

### Sessions
- **One colour per action, everywhere.** Stop is red, Remove violet, Start New and New Session green, Archive and Start New teal and Resume amber, in the project header, the agents' panes and menus, the Assistant and the questions that ask before them, so Remove All no longer looks like Stop. Every label is easy to read in both themes (WCAG AA), and labels and icons stay.
- **Remove every agent of a project at once.** **Remove All (n)** in the project header (also in the Session menu and the command palette) removes all of the project's agents after one question that lists them, says which are working (they're stopped first) and marks each worktree as merged and clean or kept. Worktrees and branches are kept unless you tick the box to delete the merged, clean ones; unmerged or uncommitted work is never deleted. Open cards are asked about once, and sessions stay in the Sessions tab.
- **The Sessions tab is a tree, like the Files tab.** Sessions sit under their provider and agent (sessions started outside Hive under *Not from an agent*), every branch shows its count, and Hive remembers which branches you open for each project. Search filters the tree across names, dates and transcripts and lists each session's matches under it, and the arrow keys move and fold as in Files. **Codex's guardian reviews** (one per action in *Approve for me*) now sit under the session that started them instead of flooding the list, and are never offered for resuming. Right-click any branch to **Archive All** or **Delete All** at once: Hive asks once, skips sessions that are running or whose files are in use, and says which. Sessions that can't be resumed are marked, with **Resume** greyed out and the reason on hover.
- **Agent templates.** Set a project's agents up once and reuse them: **Template ▾ → Save Template…** on the agent strip keeps each agent's settings and role and the layout, for one project or the whole workspace, and the templates listed under it load into any project, replacing its agents (it lists who goes and who comes first, and waits while an agent runs or has uncommitted work in its worktree). **Add Agent ▾ → Add Agent from Template** adds just one. Agents also have a **Role** (Agent Settings), such as *builder* or *reviewer*.
- **A kept worktree still gets set up.** Removing an agent before its first session but keeping its worktree used to skip the project's setup command (e.g. `npm install`) for the next agent given that worktree. Hive now remembers it, and runs setup for that agent first; a worktree whose setup already ran isn't set up again.
- **Edit templates where they are.** In the Templates view or a project's Templates tab, **Edit…** a template to add, copy, reorder or remove its agents, change each in the same Agent Settings dialog as a project's agent (including whether it gets its own worktree), and rename it, describe it or change its layout. Saving checks it first and never overwrites a change made meanwhile.
- **Switching templates reuses worktrees instead of piling them up.** Loading a template (or adding an agent from one) puts an agent back in the clean worktree of the same name, the replaced agent's or one kept from an earlier load, rather than making `hive/claude-2`. A worktree with uncommitted work is never reused: a new one is made and the dialog says why. The replaced agents' old worktrees that are merged and clean can be removed, with their branches, in the same step (a tick box, off by default); unmerged or uncommitted ones are always kept, and the dialog says why.
- **Loading a template says more before it replaces your agents.** For each new agent: its model, effort and mode, and for one with its own worktree, the branch and folder Hive will make for it in this project (never another project's). Agents replaced by a new one with the same name are marked, and the worktree setup command is mentioned when the project has one.
- **Worktree agents take less room.** An agent's tab and pane header show a worktree icon instead of its branch name; hover it, or Tab to it, for the branch, the folder, where it branched from and what's left to merge. Tabs are narrower and headers keep more of their buttons and card.
- **The page buttons sit beside the layouts.** On the agent strip, the numbered page buttons now come after **Template ▾**, right next to the layout buttons they depend on.
- **Two buttons for agents and templates instead of four.** The agent strip's **Add Agent** now opens the Add Agent dialog (**Configure Agent and Add…**), so you choose the new agent's provider and settings first; adding one at once with your defaults is **Ctrl+Alt+Shift+N** (**Add Agent** in the command palette). Its **▾** holds **Configure Agent and Add…** and **Add Agent from Template**, and **Template ▾** holds **Save Template…** above the templates to load. The menus work from the keyboard (↑ ↓, Enter, → for Add Agent from Template), and **Load Template…** and **Add Agent from Template…** are in the command palette.
- **Templates view and sharing.** A **Templates** view on the activity bar and a **Templates** tab in each project list your templates and show what each holds. From there you can load one into any project, rename, duplicate or delete it, and **export** it as a file to share. **Import** checks a file before bringing it in, and asks before replacing a template of the same name.
- **The compaction history is a table you can work with.** In the Overview, a session's compactions come ten to a page (choose how many), can be filtered by trigger or date and sorted by any column, and a click on one opens it in the Sessions tab at its divider, with the summary the agent continued from. Clicking the context in an agent's footer lands on it.
- **The context counts the last turn's thinking.** An agent's context (footer and Overview) now includes its last turn's output, thinking included, which stays in the conversation and is what Claude Code compacts on. A turn that thinks for 70K tokens no longer leaves the gauge at 116K while the agent compacts at 189K. Hover the context for the two parts and where Claude Code compacts by itself (about 167K of a 200K window); the compaction history says when one turn's output set off a compaction.
- **Click an agent's cost to see its session.** The cost in an agent's footer now opens the Overview at that agent's current session, as its tooltip says.
- **Dragging an agent onto another's pane swaps them.** Before, **Move here** often changed nothing, once agents had been clicked into panes. Now the two agents trade places, running or not, also across pages: hold a dragged agent over a page button for a moment and that page shows, so you can drop it on a pane there. Dragging along the strip still inserts between tabs, and a drop on an empty pane moves the agent there. The panes always show the agents in their order.
- **Every agent on a page has a pane.** A project now has one layout, and a page holds as many agents as it has panes: four agents in three columns are two pages (three, then the fourth), six in a grid of four are two. Before, a page always held six agents, so a layout with fewer panes hid some of them, reachable only by clicking their tab. **One at a time** has no page buttons, as the agent tabs already choose who's shown. A project that had a layout per page keeps page 1's.
- **Fresh sessions for every agent at once.** **Start New** in the project header (also in the Session menu and the command palette) starts a new session for every agent after one question that lists them and says which are in the middle of something; running agents are stopped first, and their conversations stay resumable. **Archive and Start New** archives each agent's session first. The header's buttons for several agents say how many they act on, as **Stop (3)**, **Start New (6)**, **Archive and Start New (5)** and **Resume All Agents (2)** (the count stays beside the icon when the window is narrow, and follows agents as they start and stop); **Stop All Agents** is now **Stop (n)**, **Explorer** and **Terminal** moved into the header's **⋯** menu, and thin lines now separate the agent tabs. The **Project** menu now lists every tab, Performance included, in the order of the tab strip.
- **Dates as 2026-10-04, or as you like.** Sessions named by when they started now read *2026-10-04 14:05* instead of *3 Oct 14:05*, in the Sessions tab, the Assistant's conversations, tooltips and exports. **Settings → General → Date format** (*yyyy-mm-dd*, *dd/mm/yyyy*, *mm/dd/yyyy* or *System*) and **Time format** (*24-hour* or *12-hour*) change every date Hive shows. Names you gave sessions are kept as they are, and "2 hours ago" stays, with the date on hover.
- **An agent's footer keeps its cost in view.** The session it's running is now just an icon (hover for its name and when it started; click and right-click as before). In a narrow pane the transcript size, then the context's tokens, then the whole context show just their icons, then the permission mode's name shortens, and only in a very narrow pane is the cost cut off. Before, the mode kept its full name while the cost was pushed off the edge.
- **The context in an agent's footer says what a click does**: its tooltip ends with *Click to view compaction history*.
- **See how far along long runs are: the Progress panel.** When agents run tests or builds that report progress, a panel on the right, beside the Assistant's, shows each run: which agent, how far along it is ("4 of 12: carddialog"), how long it has taken and about how long is left. Folded, it is a strip with a small bar per run, and it never opens by itself; **Ctrl+Alt+P** shows it. A passed run fades after a few seconds, a failed one stays until you have seen it and then moves to Recent, and one that stops reporting says so. Hive's taskbar button fills as runs go, and turns red when one fails, so you can see it from another app. Turn it off in **Settings → General → Progress panel**. The Assistant can see an agent's progress too.
- **The Progress panel: details for every run, and failures that tidy themselves away.** Click a run, or one under Recent, for everything Hive has for it: the command (copyable), who ran it, when it started and ended, how long it took against what was expected, its steps, its exit code, the log or run record it wrote, and a failure's summary (copyable). A failed run now stays in the list until you've seen it and then moves to Recent, marked red, instead of waiting for you to dismiss it; the strip's and the taskbar's red clear when you see it. Long commands no longer run past the run's box.
- **Showing a run's agent says so.** Clicking the agent's name on a run now flashes its pane and puts the keyboard in its terminal; before, showing an agent that was already on screen seemed to do nothing.
- **Show progress for any long command.** Agents run their tests, builds and other commands that take more than about 30 seconds as `hive-progress -- <command>` (for example `hive-progress -- npm test`) without being asked, and the **Progress panel** shows it with the agent's name, the time it usually takes and, when the command prints step lines (`##hive-progress step=4 total=12 name=carddialog`), its steps, even when it only knows how many after a slow start. The command's output and exit code are unchanged, and outside Hive it just runs the command. It's on the path of every session Hive starts, in Claude Code's and Codex's shells. To have agents use it only when you ask, turn off **Settings → General → Agents show long commands in the Progress panel**.
- **For scripts and tools:** `POST /v1/progress`, `PATCH /v1/progress/{id}` (which can also give a run started without a total its total, once) and `POST /v1/progress/{id}/finish` report a run, attributed to the calling agent by its token, and agents' status has a `progress` field.
- **The Sessions tab says where each session ran and which agent ran it**: *Coder · Project folder*, *Reviewer · Worktree · hive/reviewer*, on every session and in a **Ran in** line over its transcript (hover for the full folder). A session keeps its own agent: one you removed shows as *Coder (removed)*, and an agent added later with the same name or worktree no longer takes over its old sessions' label. **Resume in ▾** shows where each agent works, running ones too.
- **Long transcripts are flagged sooner: at 20 MB instead of 50 MB.** Well before 50 MB a long conversation slows down the coding agent and Hive. If you kept the old 50 MB default, it moves to 20 MB; a size you chose yourself, **Never** and project settings are kept. To go back, set **Settings → Sessions → Warn when a transcript is over** to 50.
- **A Codex agent's command left running is counted however Codex runs it.** Codex often runs commands from a small script and prints only part of the result; Hive missed those commands, so the agent looked idle while one ran. Hive now also counts one from the id the script printed on its own, or, for a script that runs a single command, from what Codex records.
- **Codex cost estimates for GPT-6.1 Sol**, Codex's default model, at OpenAI's published prices, and GPT-6.1 Sol in the Codex model pickers. A session on a model Hive has no price for now shows its cost as unknown (**$?** in the agent's footer, **Unknown** on the Overview, saying where to add a price) instead of nothing, and cost totals no longer count such sessions as $0: they show **Unknown**, or what is known with **+ ?**.
- **Claude Code agents show the right permission mode with `NO_COLOR` set.** With `NO_COLOR` in your environment (or Hive started from a shell that sets it), Claude Code redraws only the changed letters of its footer, so Hive kept showing the old mode after Shift+Tab, and switching the mode from Hive failed. Hive now reads the footer as it appears on the terminal's screen.
- **Allow on a Codex file-lock notice always reaches the agent.** With **File locks: Ask me**, clicking **Allow** while Codex was still replying to the blocked edit did nothing: Codex finished waiting for a go-ahead that never came. Hive now tells it to go ahead as soon as that reply ends.
- **Switching a running Claude Code agent's permission mode is more reliable on a busy machine.** Switch Now and the mode menu no longer give up when Claude Code is slow to show the new mode.

- **See what Hive keeps for a project, and clean it up.** **Project Settings → Storage** shows how much space the project's transcript backups, archive, images and agents' worktrees take, measured in the background; **Settings → Workspace** shows the workspace's total and its biggest projects. **Clean Up…** moves images and backups of old archived sessions, and images of deleted sessions, to the Recycle Bin, after showing you exactly which files go. Nothing is cleaned up automatically, running and non-archived sessions are never touched, and what the sessions used still counts in the totals.
- **A worktree agent's Merge button is an icon with its count**, like Compact and Stop, so the header keeps its labelled buttons in narrower panes. Hover it for what it merges.
- **A project's tabs shrink to icons when their names don't fit**, so all twelve stay on screen in a narrow window instead of some scrolling out of sight; the tab you're on keeps its name, and hovering one shows its name and shortcut.

### Windows
- **Notices show in Hive while you use it.** Windows notifications always appear at the bottom right, over the Assistant's panel where you type, so while any Hive window is focused an agent finishing or needing you is a **banner** in that window instead: at the top centre (or another place you choose), gone after 6 seconds for a finished agent, and staying until you handle it for one waiting for you. Click it to go to the project. **Settings → Notifications** chooses what shows while Hive is focused (*Show in Hive*, *Show nothing* or *Windows notification*), which notices get banners (all workspaces, this workspace or this project), where they go and how long they stay. With Hive in the background, Windows notifications work as before. *Only when Hive is in the background* is replaced, and every profile starts on *Show in Hive*.
- **Keep a Hive window on top.** The pin in the title bar, **View → Always on Top** or **Ctrl+Alt+O** keeps the window above your other apps, so a test run or an emulator can work underneath. Each window has its own pin, remembered with its workspace when you reopen it or start Hive again.
- **File → Close Window** (Ctrl+Shift+W) closes just the current window, as its X does. With several windows open, **File → Exit** now reads **Exit Hive (all windows)**, and its dialog says it closes them all and lists the agents by workspace (telling apart two with the same folder name by their parent folder), with **Close this window only** if that's what you meant. **Close Workspace** says it keeps the window open.

### Task board
- **Decisions on cards.** What you decide about a card now has its own place: **Decisions**, pinned above the comments in the card, each saying who wrote it down and marked **new since start** when it came after work began. You, the Assistant or an agent you tell can record one (it is always your decision); only you change or remove one. Agents read a card's decisions when they start and again before Review, and follow them over the description; reviewers fail work that ignores one. A decision recorded mid-card doesn't interrupt the agent: its next reply from a Hive tool flags it, and it checks at its next step.
- **Archive a whole column, or the whole board, and undo it.** Each column's **⋯** has **Archive All in Done (23)…**, and the board's **⋯** **Archive All Cards…**, counted per column. Cards an agent is working on, reviewing or waiting on are skipped (and listed) unless you tick **Also archive cards agents are working on**, and while a search hides cards only the ones shown are archived. **Undo** in the toast puts them back in their columns and order; later, **Archived** → **Batch** → **Unarchive this batch** does too. An agent waiting on a card you archive is told it was archived.
- **Two new columns: On Hold and Passed.** The board now runs **On Hold**, Todo, Doing, Review, **Passed**, Done. Park work you don't want picked up yet On Hold: only you and the Assistant put cards there. A card that passes its review goes to **Passed** (reviewed, not merged yet), so Review holds only what is still being reviewed, and **Done now means merged**: the agent that built a card moves it there after merging its work. Blocked cards stay where they are, marked as before, and nothing on your board moves when you update.
- **Find archived cards in a table.** **Archived** on the board now shows a table instead of a long list: sort by any column, filter by number, title, agent, project, label or the column a card was archived from, page through them, and open one with a click. **Unarchive** brings a card back to the column it came from, and **Unarchive Selected** brings back every card you tick, or all that match a filter.
- **Collapse columns and fold cards.** The **«** in a column's header folds it sideways to a narrow strip with its name and card count, and the other columns widen to fill the board; drop a card on a strip to put it at the top of that column. Each card folds to one line with its own arrow, and a column's **⋯** collapses or expands all its cards. Hive remembers what you folded for each workspace.
- **Two agents can build and review a list of cards in turn**, without you passing each card between them: tell one *"work through #111 to #117 as the builder"* and another to review them. Each waits for the other without running, and Hive wakes it with a line when the card changes. The waiting agent shows **Waiting for #112 → Review** in its header, with **Cancel**. After two hours with no change, or when the fifth round on one card fails its review (a round is one build and its review), it asks you what to do; a finding that comes back is marked in the review and counts as another round, and one the builder disputes stops the loop to ask you. The wake line names the reviewer of a verdict (*"#112 is in Review: Codex (hive) failed it"*), and on another agent's card, one an agent's own work depends on, whose card it is and that Passed isn't merged yet (*"#111 (Claude's card) is in Passed: Codex (hive) passed it (Passed isn't merged)"*); Done means merged. New `card-loop` skill.
- **For scripts:** `POST /v1/tasks/wait` waits for cards to change (a move, a comment, a review verdict), and agents can be woken by it instead of waiting. Agents with a card watch have the new status `watching`.
- **Move an open card out of the way.** Drag a card's dialog by its header to see what's behind it; it stays within the window, and opens in the usual place next time.
- **A project's Overview shows its cards at a glance**, as the Workspace Overview does for the whole board: how many of the project's cards are in Todo, Doing, Waiting for review and Done, and how many are stalled or blocked, updated as cards change. Click a number to open the project's Tasks tab.
- **An agent can't finish another agent's work in progress.** A card in Doing with one agent can't be moved to Review or Done by another agent (for example a reviewer whose review ended because the card went back for more work); Hive refuses it and leaves the card as it was. The card's own agent, you, the Assistant and scripts with the workspace token still can.
- **Agents review cards without taking them.** Ask an agent to review a card and it stays in **Review** with the agent that did the work, showing **Reviewing: Codex** on the board and an eye on the reviewer's tab, until the reviewer gives its verdict as a comment. Before, a reviewing agent moved the card to Doing, so the board showed the agent that did the work as busy on it. One agent reviews a card at a time, the review ends by itself if the reviewer stops or the card moves, and the Sessions tab says which cards a session reviewed.
- **Agents keep to their own project's cards.** An agent can see and change only its project's cards on the board: another project's cards are for that project's agents, the Assistant and you. Before, an agent in one project could list, read, comment on and move any card in the workspace. An agent also can't read or type into another project's agents' conversations any more, where those cards appear as their tasks. Each agent now calls Hive with a token of its own, so it can't get round this by saying it is someone else. The Assistant and your board still see everything.
- **Moving a card to another project takes it from its agent**, and its history says whose it was. Give it to one of the new project's agents afterwards (the card dialog does this when you change both).
- **"Check the latest comment"**: agents can read just a card's newest comment instead of the whole card, which keeps long cards from filling their context.
- **Agents move a card to Doing when they start on it.** Hand an agent a card in its conversation (*"task #59 please"*) and it moves the card to Doing before it starts, then to Review when it's done. This includes a card back from Review with follow-up work, which before stayed in Review while the agent changed it. (**Start…** on the board already moved cards to Doing.)
- **More work on a finished card goes back through Doing to Review.** **Start…** now works on a card in Done too: it moves to Doing, and its agent moves it to Review when the new work is done, for you to check again, never straight back to Done. The agent's prompt says the card is back for more work. An agent picking up a card in its conversation does the same.
- **An agent that starts on a card takes it.** When an agent moves a card into Doing, the card is given to it, also if another agent had it (in Todo, Review or Done, so not being worked on); the history says so. A card already in Doing with another agent stays theirs.
- **Moving a card to Doing asks who works on it.** Drag a card into Doing, choose **Move to → Doing**, or change its column in the card dialog, and Hive asks: **Nobody yet**, **Assign an agent** (nothing starts), or **Assign and start**, which gives it to an idle or stopped agent as **Start…** does. **Cancel** leaves the card where it was. Ask the Assistant to move a card to Doing and it asks the same, unless you've already said which.
- **The Assistant sends follow-up work through the card.** Ask the Assistant to have an agent act on a card's review feedback and it starts the card again with a note saying what to do, so the card is in Doing before the agent starts, instead of typing the request to the agent with the card left in Review.
- **Agents can move cards to Done.** Ask an agent *"looks good, move #65 to done"* and it does; the Assistant can too. Agents still move finished work to Review by default, for you to check. Every move is in the card's history with who made it, and a card can be moved back out of Done from the board or by asking. Before, only you could move cards into or out of Done.

### Skills and agent guidance
- **A warning over Hive's skills.** The Skills view, and a Hive skill while you edit it, say that editing these skills may change how agents behave in Hive.
- **Hive keeps its skills up to date in your workspaces.** When a new version of Hive improves one of its skills, a copy you haven't changed is updated when the workspace opens, and skills new in that version are added. Before, only a new workspace got them, and existing ones had to **Revert** each by hand. A skill you edited stays as you wrote it: its page says **Update available** when Hive has a newer version, and **Revert to default** takes it. A skill you deleted stays deleted. Hive's personas are kept up to date the same way.
- **New skills: `work-on-card`, `coordinate-agents` and `use-hive-api`.** Agents carry a card from Doing to Review with `work-on-card`. The Hive Assistant runs agents and cards with `coordinate-agents`, its first skill (before, the Assistant got none). `use-hive-api` helps an agent write scripts against Hive's Agent API. `review-agent-work`, `pick-up`, `handover`, `merge-ready`, `split-work` and `workspace-note` are clearer about when they apply and what they check. For example, a review covers what changed since the last one, and a pick-up doesn't stop to ask when you've said to carry on.
- **Leaner instructions.** What agents and the Assistant are always told about Hive is much shorter: how to work is in the skills, which load only when they're needed. The rules that matter whatever happens (Review when done, Done only when you ask) stay in every session. The Assistant's personas keep their character, and what it may do comes only from **Settings → Assistant → Control**.
- **Write a skill for the Assistant.** Add `audience: assistant` (or `all`) under `metadata:` in a skill's header, and the Assistant gets it. Your other skills go to agents only, as before.
- **The Skills view says who gets each skill**: **Project agents**, **Assistant** or **Agents + Assistant**, on its page and (when it isn't the default) in the list. A project's Skills tab lists only what its agents get, saying how many Assistant skills it leaves out. Deleting a skill, and **Copy to workspace**, say who it's for, and what happens to running Claude Code and Codex sessions, instead of "every agent".
- **A card back for more work brings its feedback.** When a card in Review or Done is started again, the agent's prompt includes the card's latest comment, so the review it came back with is right there.
- **For scripts and diagnostics:**
  - `GET /v1/status` says who the caller is to Hive, and which version of the API and of Hive's guidance and skills is running. Each running agent's status says what it launched with, so an agent still on older instructions is easy to spot. Help → Copy Diagnostics includes them.
  - `GET /v1/skills/{name}` reads one Hive skill, and `GET /v1/docs/agent-api` serves the API reference for the running version.
  - Agents can read just the newest comments on a long card, page through a long board, and get a short summary of another agent's activity (with the details on request).
  - Skill listings no longer show folder paths, and an agent can't list another project's own skills.
  - A project's skill listing (`GET /v1/skills?project=`, an agent's `hive_list_skills`) leaves out the Hive skills for the Assistant alone, as its Skills tab does. An agent's activity says when its task or reply was cut short (`clipped`), and `hive_agent_activity` no longer claims `detail` gives everything: it gives the last ten tool calls and the first 2,000 and 3,000 characters.
  - Polling `/v1/status` no longer re-reads every skill each time: a skill is checked again only when its files change, and calls at the same time share the work. A skill too big to check (over 64 MB, 2000 files or 12 folders deep) says so, and isn't copied for a new session. A skill's file list says when it was cut short (`filesTruncated`).
  - **Changed for scripts**:
    - `/v1/status` no longer has `agent` (Claude Code's install info from 0.1): use `providers`.
    - An agent's activity is now a summary unless you add `?detail=true`.
    - Starting a card no longer returns `note`.
    - `workspace.json` and `project.json` drop the skill switches from before 0.2 when next saved.

### Performance
- **Hive now measures what its own parts cost**, for each workspace: Agent API requests, its tools' replies as agents get them, what each session is given at launch, and the skill service's work, next to the providers' reported token usage. Only totals, kept 30 days on this machine, never prompts or replies; no guessed token counts. On by default (**Settings → Sessions → Record performance metrics**, with **Reset**); scripts read them from `GET /v1/metrics`.
- **Performance**, in the activity bar and as a tab in each project, shows those metrics for the last day, week or month: requests, latency, tool reply sizes, guidance per launch and provider usage, with a trend chart. Filter by project; by who did the work (agents, the Assistant, scripts), which frames the cards, chart and tables it changes; and by provider, next to the launches and provider usage, the only parts recorded per provider, offering every provider whatever is picked. It says how long Hive was recording, so hours it wasn't read as no data rather than zero. **Export** saves what the page shows as JSON (Shift+click to replace project names).
- **Performance → Compare** puts a baseline next to a run: views you kept before and after a change, Performance exports, or scenario benchmarks from Hive's development scenarios. Correctness comes first: a smaller result whose checks fail is shown as *smaller but failing*, and runs that measured different things are marked not comparable, with why.
- **Provider usage reads cleanly at any width.** Its cells no longer wrap wherever the text happens to break ("≈" on one line and "$25.87" on the next, "Claude Code" in two): Sessions shows the count with *3 running* under it, Context the average with *909k max of 1.0M* under it, and the cost stays whole, with any unknown sessions under it. Numbers in Hive's tables, and the costs in the Overviews, never split; when a table doesn't fit, it scrolls sideways in its section.
- **The Overview charts show every day.** In the project Overview and the Workspace Overview, each day of the 7- and 30-day charts is now a tile of its own, as in the Performance chart, so a quiet day reads as its own column instead of empty space. The three charts share one look.

### Fixes
- **Codex 0.161: switching a Codex agent's mode and Agent Setup's sandbox Set up work again.** Codex 0.161 reordered its `/permissions` menu, so Hive picked the wrong preset: a switch to Ask for approval reported it couldn't confirm, and **Set up** never reached Codex's sandbox setup. Hive now picks each preset by the name Codex shows, with older and newer Codex alike, and says so if it can't find one instead of choosing another.
- **Amber buttons are easier to read in the light theme.** The white label on the amber primary buttons (Save, Close, a dialog's default choice) and the amber label of an empty project's **Add Agent** fell short of the WCAG AA contrast for text. Both now meet it, resting and hovered: the primary fill is the same amber a shade deeper, and Add Agent's label matches Resume's.
- **Menus are readable where you point.** A hovered or keyboard-chosen menu entry (and the command palette's chosen row) put white text on the light amber, and an entry's second line, such as a session's age in the Resume picker, nearly vanished on the highlight in both themes. Every part of the chosen entry now meets AA contrast.
- **The status bar and the count badges are easier to read.** In the light theme, the white text on the amber status bar and on the small counts (in the activity bar and beside a project) fell short of the WCAG AA contrast for text, and got fainter on hover; the bar is now a shade deeper amber. In both themes, a status item's secondary part (such as a plan limit) is no longer faded, and the highlighted items (agents needing you, an update downloading or ready) meet AA too.
- **Enter on Unarchive unarchives.** In the archived cards' table, pressing Enter on a row's **Unarchive** button opened the card instead; it now unarchives it, as a click does. Enter or Space on the row itself still opens it.
- **Filter the Progress panel by project.** When runs come from several projects, a dropdown at the top of the panel shows one project's runs (or one agent's in it, or the Assistant's and scripts'), running and recent alike. Each workspace remembers it, and the folded strip follows it.
- **Show log in a run's details did nothing** for a log outside the workspace, such as an e2e run record. The Progress panel's details now have **Open log** and **Show in folder**, which open the log that run reported wherever it is, and say why when they can't. **Copy summary** is now **Copy details**: the whole run as text, every field shown, ready to paste to the Assistant. Every run and Recent row ends with a chevron to open and close its details. Agents are also asked to give their runs a short title (`hive-progress --title "unit tests" -- <command>`), so the panel says what each run is for.
- **Agents' output no longer turns into a narrow column after visiting Settings.** Opening Settings, another tab or another project while an agent ran could tell its CLI the terminal was a few characters wide; Claude Code then redrew everything one letter per line, and the terminal jumped to the top. Hidden terminals now keep their size, and a terminal scrolled to the bottom stays there.
- **The Workspace Overview fits a narrow window.** With the sidebar open in a small window (or zoomed in), its period buttons stuck out and the whole page scrolled sideways. They now go under the title and wrap when space is short, and the summary cards stack, while the table by project still scrolls on its own. Even with only a sliver of the window left beside the sidebar and the Assistant, both Overviews now fit: words wrap rather than push the page sideways, and the board's counts, the period buttons and the cards take a row each.
- **Dragging an agent only highlights places where dropping it does something.** The last agent no longer gets **Move here** on the spare panes, or a highlighted page button for the page it's already at the end of; dropping there changed nothing.
- **A date filter in the Overview's compaction table follows a change of date format.** After switching **Settings → General → Date format**, the When filter kept matching the old format until something else changed; it now matches the dates as they're shown.
- **Two windows no longer undo each other's Skills tab and Sessions tree choices.** With a workspace in each window, the provider and open groups you chose in one project's Skills tab (or the Sessions branches you opened) could be lost when you changed another project's in the other window, and come back reset after a restart. Each project's choice is now saved on its own.
- **The welcome page fits a narrow window.** With the sidebar open in a small or zoomed window, a recent workspace with a long path made the page wider than the window, and tabbing to an entry's ✕ scrolled it sideways, cutting off the title and the Start and Recent lists. The two columns now stack when there's no room, and long paths (and, if need be, long names) shorten instead.
- **Folders whose names end in .asar work like any other.** Since Hive stopped opening a project's `.asar` archives, it also refused images, PDFs and files in an ordinary folder named, say, `art.asar`: their previews didn't show and they wouldn't open. Only paths inside a real `.asar` archive are refused now.
- **Closing a window stops the storage measuring it started.** Measuring a big worktree for Project Settings → Storage or Settings → Workspace went on in the background after you closed that window (or reloaded it) until it had walked every file. It now stops, unless another page is still waiting for the same measurement.
- **Hive no longer closes by itself when you stop an agent and then switch or close the workspace (or quit) straight away.** On a busy computer, the agent being stopped could be stopped a second time before it had finished, which crashed Hive, with nothing in its log. Each agent's terminal is now stopped once.
- **Escape straight after opening a card no longer asks to discard changes.** Pressing Escape the moment a card opened (quicker on a busy computer) could ask *Discard unsaved changes?* though nothing had changed. It now just closes the card, and every dialog's Escape works from the moment it shows.
- **Two windows no longer bring back tips you've seen.** With two Hive windows open, what one window's tips remembered (the tips shown, today's tip, commands you've run) could be overwritten by the other window's older copy, so seen tips showed again after a restart. Each change is now saved on its own, and every window follows it.
- **A project's Overview fits a very narrow window, session details included.** With the sidebar and the Assistant open in a small window, the table under **Most recent session** (model, version, started, last activity) widened the page so it scrolled sideways. It now scrolls in its own box, its labels whole.
- **A window that opens while another changes tips keeps the change.** A window still loading could undo what another window had just changed (today's tip shown, so it showed it again; a project's Skills tab or Sessions tree choice), because it applied its older starting copy on top. It now applies such changes again once it has loaded.
- **Enter on a recent workspace's ✕ on the welcome page only removes it.** It used to open the workspace as well, putting it back at the top of the list. Enter on the entry itself still opens it.
- **The image viewer no longer offers Delete for a running session's image.** In a project's Images tab and the Assistant's, Delete is greyed out (hover it to see why) while the image's session or conversation runs, as the right-click menu already was; it comes back as you move to another session's image or the session stops.
- **Tooltips near the right edge of the window now appear next to their button.** The Notifications panel's ✕ showed its "Close" far to the left, over the first notification, and other buttons on the right (the Assistant panel's header, the title bar, the status bar) did the same: every tooltip was placed as if it were the widest one. Each is now placed by its own size, beside its button, and kept inside the window.
- **Rebuilding an Electron app in a project could fail while Hive was open** ("EBUSY" replacing `dist/win-unpacked/resources/app.asar`, as `npm run dist` does). Hive's storage sizes, Files tab and workspace watcher looked at `.asar` files the way Electron opens its own app, as archives, and kept them open until Hive quit. Hive now treats them as the plain files they are: they no longer stay open, the Files tab lists them as files with their size, and Storage counts their size (it counted them as empty). Leaving a Storage page also stops measuring when nothing else waits for it.
- **A project's buttons no longer spill over the side panels when they leave it little room.** With the sidebar, the Assistant and the Progress panel open in a small or zoomed window, the empty project page's text and buttons (and a narrow pane's New Session and Resume) spread over the panels and the neighbouring pane. Now everything stays in its own area: in very little room the page keeps just its title and buttons, and scrolls there if even they don't fit. A project without agents shows **No agents yet** again, instead of an empty pane covering it.
- **Your own `git add` or `git commit` could fail with "index.lock: File exists" while Hive was open.** Hive checks a project's changes and branch status in the background, and git updated its index as it did, locking it for a moment. These background checks no longer write the index, so your git commands (and your agents') don't run into them. Hive's own git actions, such as Merge, still update it as usual.
- **Ignored folders such as `dist` sometimes showed undimmed in the Files tab**, when the folder was listed just as another git command (yours or an agent's) was updating the repository. Hive now tries again, keeps the folder's last known dimming meanwhile, and lists it again a moment later.
- **A card loop could stall when two cards were reviewed together.** If one passed and the other failed a second apart, the builder was told only about the first, and its next wait started after the second, so it waited for a verdict that had already come. Now the line names every card that changed (*"#217 is in Review: Codex passed it; #119 is in Review: Codex failed it"*), and an agent's next wait counts what others changed since its last wake. The card-loop skill tells agents to check every card they wait on when woken.
- **A card loop could stall after a failed review.** A builder that fixed the findings and moved the card to Review while it was still there changed nothing, so the reviewer, waiting for the card to come back, was never woken, and both agents waited until you noticed. Now the card's agent moving a failed card to Review where it is returns it for review: its history says *Returned for review, round 2*, and the reviewer is woken (*"#159 (Claudette's card) was returned for review (round 2)"*). You can do the same from the board (right-click → **Move to → Review (next round)**). The card-loop skill also tells builders to move a failed card to Doing while they fix it.
- **A skill meant only for the Assistant could go to your project agents instead**, when its header wrote the audience in a valid but less common YAML form (a comment after it, `metadata: { audience: assistant }`, a comment line above it). Headers are now read as YAML. A skill whose header can't be read, or whose audience is misspelt, now goes to nobody and is marked **Not given** with the reason, instead of going to agents.
- **A Hive skill growing while Hive copied it for a Codex session could replace the session's good copy with an oversized one**, reported as delivered with no revision. Copies now keep the size limits as they are made and are checked before they go in; a copy that fails leaves the previous one (and says why), for Claude Code and Codex alike.
- **Editing a Hive skill or persona while Hive updated it could lose your edit.** When a workspace opened, Hive decided an untouched older copy could be updated, and a change you made in the moments before it was replaced was overwritten. Hive now checks the copy again before, during and after replacing it, and keeps your version (and waits for the next time) if it changed.
- **A Codex launch could report none of its Hive skills as delivered** when one skill's folder had been deleted or couldn't be read at that moment: the agent's details (`launched` in the Agent API) showed every skill as not copied, though the others were copied and the old copy of the failing one was still there. Each skill now succeeds or fails on its own, and a skill that couldn't be refreshed shows the copy Codex still reads, and why.
- **The Hive Assistant on Codex couldn't start when Codex was installed with npm** (`codex.cmd`): Windows ran it through cmd.exe, whose command line is too short for the Assistant's launch, and it stopped at once. Hive now starts a Node CLI's `.cmd` launcher as its node and script directly, which also passes first tasks and names to it as they are. A launcher Hive can't start directly that would be too long says so and what to do, instead of failing silently.
- **The tip card and notifications could cover Resume and New** in the bottom-right corner: the Assistant's, or the last agent pane's once its session ended. They now sit left of the Assistant's panel while it is open, and move up above such a bar.
- With a dialog open, the window's minimise, maximise and close buttons stayed bright while the rest of the window dimmed. They now dim with it, as much as the window behind (more with a question over a card), and still work.
- With the command palette open over a dialog, Escape closed the dialog underneath and left the palette open. It now closes the palette.
- **The Hive Assistant's Resume and New couldn't be clicked after its conversation ended**: the terminal's own drawing layer lay over the bar, so the pointer showed a text cursor there and clicks went to the terminal. The bar is now on top. The Assistant's header also has its own **Resume** button (|▷) once a conversation has stopped, kept in a narrow panel, so it no longer takes the **⋯** menu.
- In **Approve for me**, while Codex's reviewer checked an action, the agent's header filled with the whole command (*Auto-review: Codex asks to run $env:… npm.cmd run …*). The agent now stays **Working…** with a small shield beside it; hover the shield to see what's being checked. Hive still doesn't ask for you, and agents reading Hive's status see it as `reviewing`.
- Opening a file, a diff or a Markdown preview with code shrank the icons around the window (the activity bar's from 24 to 16 pixels, and those in the status bar, tabs and buttons) until Hive was restarted. They now keep their size.
- The **Notifications** bell on the activity bar was easy to miss with unread notifications: only a small dot in a grey icon. It now turns orange as well, clearly visible in both themes, and screen readers hear how many are unread.
- Turning off **Settings → Notifications → Flash the taskbar button when an agent needs input** didn't stop a flash that had already started; it went on until you switched to Hive. It now stops at once.
- When several agents finished together, their notification came a few seconds later even if you had turned notifications off or come back to Hive's window meanwhile. It is now left out then; with agents in two windows, only those whose window you aren't looking at are told.
- After **Compact**, an agent you had already given a new message could show as **Ready** while it worked on it, when the coding agent didn't report the compaction's end and Hive noticed it later. A new message after a compaction now counts as the compaction being over, and the agent stays **working**.
- **Task board**: a card's number (#79) was hard to read, in faint grey on the card. It now has the same contrast as the rest of the card's text, in both themes and with column colours.
- In a narrow agent pane, **Merge…**'s count spilled out of its button over its neighbour, and a long agent name, branch or card pushed the header's buttons (even ⋯) off its edge. The buttons now always fit: a count widens its button, and the agent's details shorten instead (hover for the full text).
- **Compact**'s spinner in the agent header stood still while the conversation compacted. It now turns until the compaction has finished, in the Assistant's header too.
- **Task board: long columns while dragging a card.** In a column taller than the window you could only drop a card among the cards on screen. Hold a dragged card near the top or bottom of a column and it now scrolls, faster nearer the edge, until the very top or bottom, also with the mouse held still; the drop marker follows, and the card lands where it shows. Near the board's sides the board scrolls to other columns.
- **A tab could show another project's list.** With the Skills tab open in two projects, switching to one whose skills couldn't be read showed the other project's skills, labelled as this one's older list; a slow read showed them until it finished, and one that finished late could replace the right list. The Sessions list, Memory, Images and MCP tabs, the Files filter, transcript search, the workspace's Skills and hidden projects did the same. Each now shows only its own project's (or workspace's) results, *Loading…* until they arrive, and its own error with **Retry** if they can't be read.
- **The tray's Need you list with several windows open** put the first window's agents before the others', whoever had waited longest, and with ten of them listed, an agent waiting longer in another window was left out under *and n more*. The list is now oldest first across every window, and To review the same.
- In the compact Projects sidebar, a project's count of agents needing you was cut off at the top of the first tile (and at the side at larger zoom), and could cover the status dot of the project above. The badges and dots now always show in full.
- **Codex agents said they needed you while they were still working.** In **Approve for me**, every action Codex's own reviewer was checking showed the agent as waiting for your input, with a chime and a notification, though nobody had asked you anything; a rejected action could leave it showing as waiting until its turn ended. Hive now goes by Codex itself: an agent needs you only while Codex actually shows you an approval or a question. A question Codex asks while it carries on working shows as **has a question for you**, and is in the list of agents that need you until you answer it.
- **File links in the terminal**: a path printed in different case from the file (`SRC/APP.TS`) opened the Files tab without selecting the file or showing its line. Paths with spaces (quoted, or like `C:/ws/my app/src/app.ts`) and names with brackets (`app/[slug]/page.tsx`) are now links too, and a piece of a longer path no longer links to some other file.
- Changing a number setting and then ticking **Never** straight away could leave the number saved and Never unticked, mostly in Project Settings. A number box now saves one change at a time, and your last choice wins.
- **Help → Copy Diagnostics…** left some of your own text in the log lines it copied: card titles, what the Hive Assistant did, session names, and the names and folders of workspaces you had opened before. Hive now marks your text as it writes its log and the report leaves it out, including what an agent was started with; lines an older version wrote show only their time and source, and folders that aren't Hive's or the coding agents' own are hidden. The report also says which Windows sandbox Codex uses.
- The Assistant's **Resume** button (on its start page and when its conversation has ended) is amber, like every other Resume button, instead of grey.

## 0.3.1 — 2 October 2026

### Fixes
- **Remove Project** refused a project whose agent worktree was saved under a short Windows folder name (such as `C:\Users\JOHNSM~1\…`), saying git didn't list it as one of the project's worktrees. **Add Agent → existing worktree** had the same problem. Paths are now compared by their real, full name.

## 0.3.0 — 2 October 2026

### Agent footer
- **Context as a percentage of the window**: the footer shows *84k · 42%* once the CLI has reported the session's context window, so you can see how close it is to needing Compact whether the model has 200K or 1M. In a narrow pane only the percentage shows; the tooltip has both numbers.

### Notifications
- **The taskbar button shows when agents need you**: a badge with the count over Hive's icon, the count before the window title (Alt+Tab), and a flash when an agent starts waiting for your answer while Hive is in the background. Each window shows its own workspace. Both can be turned off in **Settings → Notifications**.
- **Agents finishing together chime once and get one notification**: "3 agents finished in hive" naming them, or "3 agents finished" with how many in each project, instead of a chime and a pop-up each. An agent asking for your input is still told at once, on its own.

### Sleep and shutdown
- **The PC stays awake while agents work.** A PC set to sleep after a while idle no longer sleeps mid-task because nobody touched the keyboard; once no agent is working it may sleep again (the screen can still turn off and lock). The status bar says so while it lasts. **Settings → General → Keep the PC awake while agents work**: *When plugged in* (default), *Always* or *Never*.
- **Windows shutting down, restarting or signing you out** no longer loses the end of a transcript: Hive backs them up first, without holding up the shutdown.
- **After sleep**, agents' states and plan usage are read again straight away.

### When Hive's window crashes
- **A crashed window reloads by itself**, and says your agents kept running (they run outside the window, so they always did). Before, the window just went blank, and quitting Hive to fix it stopped the agents. If it crashes again within a minute, Hive asks: **Reload**, **Open Logs** or **Quit Hive**.
- **A window that stops responding** gets **Wait** or **Reload** after a few seconds; the question goes away if it recovers.

### Bug reports
- **Help → Copy Diagnostics…** gathers what a bug report needs (Hive's and Windows' versions, your coding agents and whether they're signed in, counts, the settings that change how Hive behaves, and the end of Hive's log) and shows it before copying. Folders, names, keys and tokens are taken out.

### Terminal
- **Ctrl+click a file path to open it.** File paths in an agent's or the Assistant's terminal (`src/app.ts`, `tests/x.test.ts:42:7`, stack traces) are links: Ctrl+click opens the file in the project's Files tab with the cursor on that line, in the agent's worktree for a worktree agent, or in another project of the workspace when the path leads there. Only files that exist are underlined.

### Tips
- **A tip when Hive starts**: once a day, a small card in the bottom-right corner shows something Hive can do, with **Try it**, **Learn more** (the user guide at that section) and **Next tip**. It never gets in the way, and steps aside while you type in a terminal under it.
- **Tips at the right moment**, once each: the first time a transcript turns amber, the context passes your Compact threshold, you add a second agent or you paste a screenshot. Tips about things you already do are skipped.
- **Help → Tips…** lists them all, grouped and searchable. **Don't show tips** on the card, or **Settings → General → Show a tip when Hive starts**, turns them off.

### Agent header
- **Archive & New** moved from the agent header into its **⋯** menu, as **Archive and Start New…**, leaving more room for the agent's name, card and session.
- **Compact** and **Stop** are small icon buttons, like the Assistant's (hover for what they do).
- **The session's name moved to the agent's footer**, on the right before the context, and no longer repeats the project and agent: until the session has a name it shows when it started (*14:05*). Right-click it to **Rename…**; in a narrow pane it gives way first.
